# Changelog

All notable changes to `ai-olympus` will be documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

- 🎭 **Changed**: Casey Jones now has a portrait in the shared Krang mascot style, replacing the placeholder in the full-size avatar, thumbnail, and social preview. The generation prompt is recorded with the other agents (issue #182).

- 🧰 **Added**: `interactive-testing` checks that the local environment is ready before the first scenario, and prepares every item that is not ready. The new *Local readiness* section lists the items: the checkout is on the head under test, the dependencies match the lock files (installed from the committed lock files only, with an `outbound-request` audit line for each install), `.env` exists, the local database is migrated, the caches are current, the front-end assets match the head, and the application answers on the project URL with the processes a scenario needs. Each item is prepared with the project's own documented command, only on a local target, without editing a tracked file or discarding uncommitted changes. The report lists every preparation step, and the run stops the processes it started, switches back to the branch it started on, and deletes a local branch it created. The checkout is pulled with `git pull --ff-only`. `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` names the lock-file install as the one exception to the outbound-request ban and lists it in the consent table. An item that cannot be made ready blocks only the scenarios that depend on it. `raphael`'s Bash boundary and `auto-interactive-testing` *Prepare the environment* reference the section. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 💥 **Added**: the `blast-radius-analysis` skill maps a planned change to everything that depends on it before implementation starts. It adds only what the repository did not have: a depth per tier (`FAST` skips it, `STANDARD` walks direct callers and write paths, `CRITICAL` adds one hop over shared symbols and every touched risk area), three evidence grades (`Verified`, `Likely`, `Unknown`), a short YAML impact report, and a scope-change procedure that stops the affected step and analyses only the new part. The walks themselves stay owned by `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` (*Behaviour changed outside the diff*, *Input consistency*, and the outside-caller verification gap), the tier stays owned by `classify-risk.sh`, and the pre-flight stays `resolve-issue` *Read, Map & Verify*, which now records its Map result through the skill on `STANDARD` and `CRITICAL` tasks. The report travels in the shared brief: `donatello` names it in its handoff, `leonardo` verifies its claims instead of trusting them and raises a Moderate finding when the diff reaches an unlisted shared interface, schema, or security boundary, and `raphael` adds its runtime scenarios as regression rows (#190).

- 🧪 **Changed**: `composer shell-self-tests` prints one aligned row per script with its status, check count, and duration, and a summary line at the end. The new `scripts/run-shell-self-tests.sh` runs each script's `--self-test` and prints the full output, indented, only for a script that failed. Before, each script printed its own format with different column widths and temporary paths. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧬 **Added**: a subclass does not copy a parent method to change one step of it. `rules/php/core-standards.md` *Structure* states the rule: the parent calls the step as a separate `protected` method (Template Method) when the project owns the parent, or the class receives the step as a collaborator (composition) when the step varies in more than one way or the parent is a vendor class without a hook. An override that calls `parent::method()` and adds work around it is not this case. Code review raises such an override as a Moderate finding: `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` gains the catalog bullet *Override that copies its parent method to change one step*, and `rules/code-review/general.md` *Reuse Existing Logic* does not raise the same override again as a Critical duplicate. The finding is about the copied body, never about the size of the method. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧱 **Changed**: a code review of a refactoring-only change checks first that the logic stayed the original. `rules/refactoring/general.md` *Code Review Application* makes behaviour preservation the primary check: the review compares the old and the new code of every restructured unit before every other lens. A behaviour change, a bug fix included, is now a Moderate finding instead of a Critical one. It still blocks the merge and is never deferred to a sub-issue at round 3, because the loop restores the old behaviour. A change under the S1–S3 security carve-out keeps its security severity. `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md`, `leonardo` (*Refactoring agenda*), and `process-code-review` (`references/round-three-deferral.md`) carry it. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🐬 **Added**: the `casey` agent writes the MySQL analysis of a code review as a separate, advisory report. `splinter` dispatches it once per diff, after the review converged, when the diff touches database operations on MySQL / MariaDB or on an unresolved engine. It runs `mysql-problem-solver` with the new `MODE=cr-report` and publishes one PR comment in the `mysql-report` marker namespace that proposes optimizations for critical MySQL problems only. The report never enters the review counts, the convergence gate, or the merge gate. `leonardo` and the CR wrappers no longer run `mysql-problem-solver` inline; on PostgreSQL `postgres-patterns` stays the inline lens. A caller that cannot dispatch `casey` writes `MySQL report not run — dispatch casey`. The agent ships for Claude Code (`agents/casey.md`) and Codex (`codex/agents/casey.toml`) with a placeholder avatar (issue #180).

- ✂️ **Changed**: `merge-github-pr` and `pr-summary` fit the 5 000-token skill body limit again, so `composer build` runs without a `skill-check` warning. No rule changed and no text was dropped. The `FAST`-tier, dependency-only, and HOTFIX merge cases moved to `skills/merge-github-pr/references/merge-exemptions.md`. The terse output style moved to `skills/pr-summary/references/terse-output-style.md`, and the JIRA and Bugsnag markup rules moved to `skills/pr-summary/references/markup-per-target.md`. Each skill keeps a short pointer section under the old heading.

- 🔎 **Changed**: a query condition the code applies in two or more places is a local scope on the model, never a private Repository method or a shared closure. `rules/laravel/laravel.md` *Database and Eloquent* states the rule: a scope that combines `orWhere` wraps its conditions in a nested `where()`, and a test proves that the scope narrows the result. `rules/laravel/dynamodb.md` gains *Local Scopes on DynamoDB Models*: `baopham/laravel-dynamodb` supports local scopes, but `callScope()` adds a scope's conditions flat to the query, so an ungrouped `OR` turns the `FilterExpression` into `A AND B AND C OR D` and can return rows of other tenants (Critical). A misspelled scope is silently ignored, because `__call()` returns the builder for every unknown method, so only a test catches it. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧩 **Changed**: an issue with several pull requests gets its review remarks and findings split per pull request. `rules/code-review/general.md` *An issue with several pull requests — remarks and findings split per pull request* states the rule: each pull request keeps its own technical comment, and the comment on the issue renders the assignment verdict once and groups each pull request's findings, remarks, and per-PR sections under one heading per pull request. Before, the one comment per issue was updated in place, so the review of a second pull request overwrote the findings of the first. A run that reviews one pull request now rewrites only its own group and carries the others over. `pr-summary` renders it through the new `references/several-pull-requests.md`, and `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `cr-wrapper-contract.md`, and the JIRA template point at it. The package owner requested the change directly (#175).
- 🏷️ **Changed**: `/report-code-review` mentions the author of the changes on each finding about a missing test and on each DRY finding, so the author sees which gap in their own change the point names. `rules/code-review/general.md` *The author of the changes is the account that pushed the pull request* adds this second exception next to the unmet acceptance criterion; every other finding, and every run that is not review-only, still names no author. The GitHub pull-request comment carries an `Author` field on such a finding and a new `## Missing tests added` section that lists each test the missing-tests commit had to add, with the same mention. The tracker comment ends the matching `Review findings` bullet with the mention in the tracker's own syntax. `cr-wrapper-contract.md`, `pr-summary`, `leonardo`, `donatello` (its missing-tests handoff now names the behaviour each test protects), and the command carry it. The package owner requested the change directly (#175).
- 🧱 **Changed**: a refactoring-only change keeps every observable behaviour of the code it restructures, bugs included. `rules/refactoring/general.md` *Behaviour stays identical — bugs included* states the rule once: a wrong result, an inconsistency between two code paths, and a race condition stay as they are, and the refactoring fixes none of them, not even in a separate commit of the same pull request. Every discrepancy the refactoring finds is filed as a follow-up issue in the originating tracker, and the pull request links it from `## TODO`. The filing bar in `rules/compound-engineering/tracker.md` now files three things: such a discrepancy joins the critical business-logic gap and the critical security issue. `class-refactoring` no longer fixes obvious pre-existing bugs before a refactoring, and `refactor-entry-point-to-action` no longer fixes them while it extracts an Action. The pre-existing-issue handling of `resolve-issue`, `process-code-review`, `create-test`, and `create-missing-tests-in-pr` files a bug or a security weakness instead of fixing it when the work is refactoring-only. Code review raises a behaviour change in a refactoring-only pull request as a Critical finding and settles a carried-over discrepancy by a follow-up issue, never by a fix; a finding under the S1–S3 security carve-out still blocks the merge. The package owner requested the change directly (#175).
- 🧩 **Added**: every code-review comment answers two questions in its header: "is the data stored consistently whatever input in the application writes it?" and "can existing code in the codebase be used instead of repeating it?". `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` gains the mandatory *Input consistency* walk: it lists every entry point that writes the changed data and marks each one `covered` or `bypasses the change`. A bypassing entry point is a Critical finding. The new `Input consistency:` and `Reuse (DRY):` header lines and the `## Input consistency` section are defined in `rules/code-review/general.md` *Input consistency and reuse*. Both review templates, `cr-wrapper-contract.md`, and `leonardo` (*Input-consistency and reuse agenda*) carry them. The package owner requested the change directly.
- 🧪 **Added**: the `auto-interactive-testing` skill works through every open issue labelled `interactive-testing` as one unattended batch. It takes the issues oldest first and skips an issue that another run claimed. For each issue it reads the body and every comment, claims the issue, and first fixes the defects the issue already links: `splinter` resolves each one, runs the review to convergence, and merges it through `merge-github-pr` without a new follow-up issue. Then it checks the local environment, runs the scenarios with `interactive-testing` on the freshly pulled default branch, files, fixes, and merges every new defect the change caused, and runs the affected scenarios again. It restores every setting it changed and deletes its test data and throwaway branches. Last, it posts one verdict table on the issue and closes the issue only when every scenario passed. Per user request.
- 📝 **Changed**: the `analyze-support-issue` comment is always a TL;DR. It has one heading `TL;DR`, one verdict line, one bullet per field (what is happening, what to do now, the open gaps, the documentation, the sources), and the answer for the client as a one-line quote. The cap drops from 3 000 to 1 500 characters. `templates/support-comment-jira.md` now lists the format rules that keep the Wiki Markup source intact through the ADF conversion: one line per block, no blank line between bullets, a quote that opens and closes on one line, and no Markdown. `scripts/check-comment.php` enforces them before the publish, and the read-back requires the rendered heading, list, and quote. A contract test converts a valid TL;DR to ADF and checks its node types. The package owner requested the change directly.
- 🧭 **Changed**: `merge-github-pr` files an `interactive-testing` follow-up issue only when the merged diff changes the front-end UI. A project that asks for such an issue after every merge got one also for a change to the build, the tests, or the back end, where there is nothing to click through. *Post-merge* points to the new `references/interactive-testing-follow-up.md`. A front-end UI change is the frontend surface `code-review` already defines (*Frontend surface detected in the diff*), plus scripts under `resources/js/**`, public assets a page loads, and user-visible strings in `lang/**`. Tests (a browser test included), documentation, build and CI configuration, dependency manifests, agent configuration, and back-end code that renders nothing never count. The step reads `files[]` from the loader document of the merged pull request, files the issue through `create-issue`, and records an `external-write` audit line. Without a UI change the merge report says `interactive-testing: skipped, no front-end UI change`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ⏱️ **Added**: a run that needs a CI result waits for it instead of stopping. `rules/git/pull-requests.md` *Waiting for CI — read the checks every 3 minutes* reads `gh pr checks <PR-URL> --json name,bucket` in a background loop every 180 seconds, with no time limit, until no check is pending. A single failed read is retried after the next interval, and three failed reads in a row end the wait. A step with a local substitute, such as a code review, does not wait. After the wait, the run loads the pull request again and runs every check of its next step again, so a red check still blocks and the billing exception applies unchanged. `merge-github-pr` names the pending states and no longer skips a pull request only because a check is still running. `skills/code-review-github/scripts/load-issue.sh` now reports a running check run as `IN_PROGRESS` or `QUEUED`: it took the empty `conclusion` GitHub gives a running check, so such a check reached every consumer of `statusCheckRollup[]` with the state `""`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🎧 **Added**: the `analyze-support-issue` skill analyses a JIRA issue a support team filed and publishes one plain-language comment a non-technical colleague can act on. It picks one of nine verdicts — an application bug, a known bug, correct behaviour the documentation describes or does not describe, a documentation error, a missing feature, a data request, something outside the application, or not yet decidable — and every statement in the comment rests on evidence the run produced itself: code on the default branch, git history, a local reproduction, the raw text of a documentation article, or a tracker record. Earlier analyses, web-fetch summaries, and search hits are never evidence. Before the publish, `scripts/check-comment.php` fails a comment that carries an estimating word, a developer token, or more than 3 000 characters; it matches whole words, so `snadný` is not `snad`, and after three failed checks the run publishes nothing. The comment follows the language of the issue, Czech when there is no signal, and has a fixed shape: the verdict, what is happening, a ready answer for the client, the next steps, the documentation, the open gaps with the role that closes each one, and the sources. The skill runs unattended, publishes under the new `support-analysis` marker namespace, updates its earlier comment in place, and publishes nothing when no other account and no unmarked comment of its own account was created or edited after its last analysis. Marker text in another account's comment never counts as an earlier analysis. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔗 **Changed**: a JIRA comment may link related tracker items and articles of the project's product documentation. `rules/reports/general.md` *A JIRA comment is written for a non-technical reader* adds this third exception next to the user-facing string and the one pull-request link: a support analysis has no pull request, and its reader needs the known issue, the duplicate, and the help-centre article to act. A link to code, a commit, a CI run, or a log stays banned. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🏷️ **Changed**: the JIRA `upsert-comment.sh` helper publishes into any marker namespace, like the GitHub helper. Before, every `MARKER_KEY` other than `agent-note` fell back to `cr-comment`, so a project skill that published its own comment on a ticket either overwrote the code-review comment of the same account or, with `agent-note`, added a new comment on every run. A namespace that matches `[a-z][a-z0-9-]*` is now looked up and updated in place and never touches a comment of another namespace; any other value exits 1 before a JIRA call. The lookup now requires a non-namespace character before the marker, so the namespace `comment` never matches a `cr-comment:actor=` line. `rules/code-review/general.md` *Authorship trust* reads every `<namespace>:actor=` marker as agent output, also a namespace a project skill chose, and `rules/jira/general.md` keeps one comment per actor and marker namespace; its MCP fallback carries the caller's namespace. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧾 **Added**: agents share one quality-gate run per git tree. `skills/_shared/run-gate.sh --tier full|pr` runs the manifest gate without a shell, under an atomic `mkdir` lock (`flock(1)` does not exist on macOS), and writes a machine record keyed to the tree: the commands with exit codes and durations, `HEAD`, the tree, the merge base, an environment fingerprint, the actor, the times, and the SHA-256 of the log. A second run on the same tree takes the result over. `skills/_shared/verify-gate.sh --tier full|pr <sha>` accepts a record only for the same tree, the same commands, the same environment, and an intact log, then runs `gate-fresh` again; it exits `0` valid, `10` missing, `11` failed, or `12` stale, and its JSON reports the `gate-fresh` verdict as `fresh_exit_code`. A failed run stops at its first failing command, so its record lists only those commands; `verify-gate.sh` reports that record as `11` failed, never as `12` stale. A HOTFIX waives a failed `run-gate.sh` run only when the tier's last command is the only failure and that verdict is `0`. A rebase, squash, or amend that keeps the tree keeps the record. Three new manifest keys configure it: `pr-gate` (the fast pre-push gate `donatello` runs before each push), `gate-fresh` (commands never reused, default `composer audit` when `composer.lock` exists), and `gate-evidence` (the git-ignored record directory, default `.claude/run/gates`). `process-code-review` *Finalization* writes the record, `merge-github-pr`, `verify-merge-readiness`, and `splinter` verify it before running a gate, `leonardo` only cites it, and `record-metrics.sh --gate-record` stores the gate's run time. The command checks of `run-validation.sh` moved into the shared `skills/_shared/project-commands.sh`, so all three runners refuse the same commands. The record is opt-in: a project that sets none of the three keys keeps the textual `Quality gate:` record exactly as before, even when it sets `gate` (#169).

- 🧪 **Changed**: `/report-code-review` may add the missing tests for the pull request's current diff. Before the review, `splinter` dispatches `donatello` once in the new *Missing-tests mode*: it adds the missing tests, runs them, and pushes all of them in exactly one commit on the pull request branch. The commit changes test code only and never changes the business logic of the change under review, unless the user explicitly asks for that change. A test that fails against the current code is not committed; the review reports the behaviour it exposed. `leonardo` then reviews the new head and still fixes nothing. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🗄️ **Changed**: all SQL query composition stays in the Repository. `rules/laravel/architecture.md` drops the *Basic queries only* rule. That rule moved use case–specific queries out of the Repository into a Service or an Action. A use case–specific query is now an intent-named Repository method. A Repository never returns a `Builder` or a `Relation` for the caller to extend. It never takes a closure over the query builder, and it never takes conditions, columns, operators, or raw SQL that the caller assembled. One exception stays: an equality lookup on the model's own attributes through the inherited `*ByParams` methods. Services and Actions call the method, and they never filter or sort Repository results in memory in place of a query condition. Code review raises a Moderate finding for query composition outside a Repository and for a Repository method that lets a query part out. `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md`, `rules/laravel/livewire.md`, `rules/sql/optimalize.md`, `class-refactoring`, and `latency-critical-systems` follow the rule. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔁 **Fixed**: `skills/_shared/delete-owned-github-comment.sh` deletes a superseded comment again. It checked the repository of every protected comment through `.repository_url`, but `GET repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/comments/{id}` returns no such field, so every check failed with exit 4 "belongs to a different repository" and merge-preparation cleanup could never remove an old TL;DR. The helper now reads `.issue_url` and requires it to name an issue or pull request of the target repository. The test double of `gh` returned the missing field, which hid the bug; it now returns the real shape, and a new case rejects a protected comment from another repository. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧭 **Added**: a JIRA comment ends its sections with `Impact after deployment` — the parts of the application a production deployment of the change can affect, so the ticket owner knows what to watch after the release. It follows `What changed`, carries at most five bullets of one feature each, and retells the newest review's `## Affected behaviour outside the diff` as features, never as files or methods. A review that omits that section found nothing outside the diff, so the section lists only the features the diff changes; only a head with no review yet carries the caveat that the review has not confirmed it. A Czech ticket renders it as `Co může změna ovlivnit po nasazení`. When the comment overflows the 3 000-character cap, `What changed` is shortened first, then the impact bullets are merged. `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` and `templates/pr-summary-jira.md` own the section; `rules/reports/general.md`, `code-review-jira`, `code-review`, `cr-wrapper-contract.md`, `verify-merge-readiness`, `tracker-follow-up.md`, and `april` name it in their copy of the JIRA shape.

- 🧩 **Changed**: the limit of 4 parameters no longer applies to an Action's constructor. `rules/php/core-standards.md` adds a third exemption to the >4-parameter rule: an Action composes collaborators, and the container injects them through its constructor, so a DTO of injected services is no fix. The Action's `__invoke()` stays subject to the limit. The code-review walk and `class-refactoring` read the exemption list from that rule, so they follow it without their own change. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧪 **Added**: `interactive-testing` creates the test data a scenario is missing and finishes the walkthrough. The new *Missing test data* section names the sources in order: existing test data, the application's UI, the project's seed mechanism, and the application's factories, actions, or services through `tinker`. It allows data only on a local target, only in a state the application can produce, marked as test data, without touching existing records or triggering unauthorized side effects, and the run reports and deletes every record it created. Missing data no longer makes the run ask for a workspace or stop as `Blocked`, unless no source can produce it inside these limits. `raphael`'s Bash boundary allows the same data creation. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧾 **Changed**: a JIRA comment renders one shape on every run — the review-only summary, the code-review mirror, the post-convergence report, and the merge-readiness TL;DR alike. It opens with a status line of one to three sentences, then `Acceptance criteria`, `Review findings`, and `What changed`, the closing links line, and the footer. `Review findings` retells the newest pull-request review in plain language, with the point that blocks the merge first. A finding outside the assignment reaches the ticket only when it is Critical. The comment has no `How to test` and no `Clarifying questions` section: an open question closes the bullet of the acceptance criterion it concerns, or the finding it belongs to. The tester's steps stay in the pull-request description. On a JIRA source, `verify-merge-readiness` publishes every open decision in a `merge-readiness` comment on the pull request, the JIRA TL;DR shows only the Critical ones, and `merge-github-pr` *Open merge decisions* reads the decisions of a JIRA source there, so the merge still waits for every answer. `april` no longer puts the English headline on a JIRA comment. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔁 **Fixed**: `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/wiki-markup-to-adf.php` produces ADF that JIRA accepts and renders as written. Inline code inside bold or italic text got the marks `code` + `strong`, but ADF allows the `code` mark beside a link only, so `acli ... comment update --body-adf` failed and the helper left the comment unchanged. The code span now keeps the `code` mark alone. Strong and emphasis no longer open or close inside a word, so an identifier such as `campaign_report_stats` stays literal text instead of rendering `report` in italics, and a code span inside them may carry the delimiter. A line that starts with `- ` now becomes a bullet list, the same as `* `. An agent once read such a failed update as a structure problem and republished the comment without headings and lists. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🆕 **Changed**: `/report-code-review` always publishes new comments and never updates a comment an earlier review left. The GitHub and JIRA `upsert-comment.sh` helpers gain the leading `--create` option: it skips the lookup of an existing marked comment and creates a new one in the same marker namespace, so a later run without the option still updates the newest comment. The review-only run passes it for the GitHub pull-request comment and for the `pr-summary` tracker comment. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔁 **Fixed**: `skills/_shared/carry-operator-lines.php` no longer carries an agent-written JIRA paragraph that contains a mention as an operator line. JIRA stores the display name in `attrs.text` of a `mention` node, while `wiki-markup-to-adf.php` writes only `attrs.id`, so the same paragraph produced two texts and two fingerprints, and every rewrite appended a copy after the footer. `carryAdfText()` now renders a mention from `attrs.id` only, before and after JIRA stores it. Per issue #164.

- 🌿 **Changed**: `/report-code-review` always reviews the current code of the pull request branch. The command, `splinter` *Review-only mode*, and `leonardo` in `review_only` switch the current working tree to the pull request's head branch, pull it, and confirm that local `HEAD` equals the head SHA before the review. A review-only run never reviews in a worktree, from a stale branch, or from the remote diff alone, and it stops when the checkout fails. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔁 **Fixed**: the JIRA `upsert-comment.sh` helper finds the comment it has just created. `acli` 1.3.x returns no comment ID from `comment create`, so the helper re-reads the issue to find it. Jira Cloud can answer the first read after a create without the new comment, and the helper then exited 3 with "ID is missing". The comment stayed in its create-time form: the ADF update never ran, so a mention showed as raw `[~accountid:…]` text. The re-read now runs up to three times, one second apart, and the helper prints the error of the last failed re-read. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 📌 **Added**: a line the operator added to an agent comment, for example `Rozhodnuto: …`, survives the next rewrite. The operator posts under the same account as the agents, so the new `skills/_shared/carry-operator-lines.php` records a fingerprint of the agent-written lines in every published version: a hidden `<!-- <namespace>:lines=… -->` line on GitHub, a ` lines=…` suffix in the visible marker on JIRA. On a rewrite, the GitHub and JIRA `upsert-comment.sh` helpers carry every line with an unknown fingerprint verbatim (a whole ADF node on JIRA, mentions included) and print `carried_lines=<n>` plus each `carried:` line. `rules/code-review/general.md` *A line the operator added survives the rewrite* makes the calling run quote every carried line in its report, and `leonardo` and `april` say so. A comment published before this change has no fingerprint, so its first rewrite carries nothing and reports `reason=no-fingerprint`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ✅ **Added**: every code-review comment answers "can we merge, and is the assignment met with no Critical finding?" in its header. The new `Merge verdict:` line says `yes` or `no`, states `assignment met` and `open Critical` separately, and names every blocker when the answer is no. `rules/code-review/general.md` *Merge verdict* defines the four conditions of a `yes`, and a `yes` never lifts the merge gate of `merge-github-pr`. Both review templates, `cr-wrapper-contract.md`, and `leonardo` (*Merge-verdict agenda*) carry the line. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧭 **Added**: the code review reports every untouched part of the application whose behaviour the current diff changes. `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` gains the mandatory *Behaviour changed outside the diff* walk: it lists each symbol whose behaviour the diff changes, finds every caller and consumer outside the diff, and records the before and after behaviour, whether the assignment asks for it, and the covering test. A change the assignment does not ask for is a finding, Critical when it changes persisted data, a user-visible output, an API contract, authorization, or money. The review comment renders the new `## Affected behaviour outside the diff` section, on a converged run too, and `leonardo` gains the *Impact agenda*. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 💬 **Added**: `leonardo` reads every comment on the JIRA ticket and on the pull request before it raises a question, and never asks a question that somebody already answered. The new *Comment agenda* in `agents/leonardo.md` names the sources. `skills/code-review-jira/references/clarifying-questions.md` *Already-answered walk* now walks the pull request's conversation comments, review summaries, and every review thread, resolved and unresolved. The *Reviewer Comment Fulfillment Gate* in `cr-wrapper-contract.md` treats a reviewer question as answered by any later comment on the pull request or the tracker item, not only by a reply on the same thread. `rules/code-review/general.md` *The questions the review settles itself* states the same rule. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧾 **Fixed**: a JIRA issue an agent creates shows a formatted description, the same as a comment. `skills/process-code-review/scripts/file-deferred-moderate.sh` passed the Wiki Markup body to `acli jira workitem create --description-file` as plain text, so `h2.`, `*`, and `{{code}}` appeared as literal text. The script now converts the body with `wiki-markup-to-adf.php` and validates the ADF before the write. `rules/jira/general.md` gains *Issue Description Format*, and `skills/resolve-issue/references/deferred-follow-up.md` points to it. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 📖 **Added**: the acceptance criteria are compared with the product documentation, not only the change. `rules/code-review/general.md` *Published product documentation is a requirement the assignment need not restate* gains the per-criterion comparison, with the statuses `consistent`, `contradicts`, and `not documented`, and a fourth mismatch shape: a criterion that asks for a behaviour the documentation describes differently. `assignment-compliance-check` runs the comparison as its step 2a and returns the statuses beside its block, so the wrapper publishes a contradiction as a Moderate finding on the pull request and a clarifying question on the tracker. `test-assignment` runs it in step 1 and asks the question in `Decisions before merge`.

- 🧪 **Added**: the `/test-assignment <reference> [report|fix]` command and the `test-assignment` skill test a task's pull request against its assignment and prove that nothing else broke. The skill loads the assignment with every comment, maps every input that reaches the changed behaviour and every duplicate implementation of the same logic, and builds a test matrix of real data only. `donatello` runs the whole suite and the gate on the final head and classifies each failure as caused by the pull request, pre-existing, or flaky. `raphael` exercises the running application and compares the same request set with the base branch. `report` mode changes nothing: `april` publishes the technical report on the pull request and a plain-language summary on the source issue through the new *Test report mode*, under the new `test-report` marker namespace. `fix` mode adds the missing tests and fixes and continues through `verify-merge-readiness`. `splinter` gains *Test mode*, and `raphael` may switch the main checkout between the branch under test and its base branch when the dispatch asks for that comparison. Neither mode merges.

- 🔑 **Fixed**: `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/transition-to-in-progress.sh` recognises an issue that the authenticated user already owns. The ownership lookup requested only the `key` field, and `acli` 1.3.39 answers such a search with `[null]`. An owned issue in review or in progress therefore read as claimed by another run, and the helper refused it with exit 3 or 4. The lookup now requests `summary`, and `key` stays in every result. The test fake of `acli` now returns the real output shape.

- 📣 **Added**: an unmet acceptance criterion addresses the author of the changes. `pr-summary` *An unmet criterion addresses the author* states why each criterion is not met and adds one sentence that mentions the author, who confirms and fixes the gap or refutes the claim: `@login` on GitHub, an ADF mention on JIRA. `wiki-markup-to-adf.php` converts `[~accountid:<id>]` into an ADF mention node, and `load-issue.sh` returns `assigneeAccountId` to help resolve the account. When no JIRA account resolves, the name stays plain text. `rules/code-review/general.md` names this as the one case where the author is reported. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the installer no longer generates a `CLAUDE.md`. `templates/CLAUDE.md` is deleted, `install` writes no root `CLAUDE.md` and, on a Laravel Boost project, no `.ai/guidelines/ai-olympus.md`. An existing `CLAUDE.md` is never touched. `AGENTS.md` is still installed when the project has none, and Claude Code reads it when `CLAUDE.md` is absent. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔁 **Added**: code review detects N+1 queries. `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` gains the mandatory *N+1 queries* walk. It traces every per-item relation read back to the query that produced the collection, also outside the diff, and flags a relation property, a nested chain, a per-item aggregate or relation query, an unloaded relation read by an accessor, an API Resource, a Blade view, or a Livewire render, and a removed eager load. The finding is Moderate, Critical on an unbounded per-request collection, and its Suggested Fix writes out the eager load. The report renders it in `## Database Analysis` with the `N+1:` prefix. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ✍️ **Added**: code review knows the author of the changes. `rules/code-review/general.md` *The author of the changes is the account that pushed the pull request* makes the pull request's `author.login` the author, never the issue reporter, the assignee, or the reviewing agent. The author is internal information for the agent: no header line, finding, TL;DR line, or tracker comment reports it. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 📏 **Changed**: code review never reports the size of a file or a method. `rules/code-review/general.md` gains *File and method size is never a code-review finding*, and `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` drops the walk that raised a Moderate finding when a file over the size threshold grew. The size ratchet in `rules/php/core-standards.md` *Structure* stays authoring guidance only. A defect inside large code, such as mixed responsibilities, stays a finding under its own rule. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔎 **Added**: the `/report-code-review <reference>` command reviews one task's pull request and only reports the result. It takes a GitHub issue or pull-request URL, a JIRA key or URL, or a Bugsnag error URL. `splinter` gains a *Review-only mode* that dispatches `leonardo` once with `review_only`. `leonardo` then skips the fix loop and runs the tracker-matching code-review wrapper once in publish mode, so the technical findings land on the GitHub pull request and the non-technical summary lands on the source issue. The summary carries no *How to test* section. In its place, *Review findings* retells the GitHub report in plain language, so a non-technical reader understands what the review reported and can reply with feedback. `pr-summary` gains the caller option `review-only` for it, and `rules/reports/general.md` names it as the one exception to its section shape. The run dispatches no implementation, changes no tracker status, and never merges. Codex reaches the same review through `$code-review-github`, `$code-review-jira`, or `$code-review-bugsnag`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧹 **Changed**: a prompt audit of every tracked rule, skill, agent, and command file against the current Claude models. The cleanup removes history narration ("used to", "retired", "What is lost", issue archaeology), sections kept only to say a mechanism is retired, and references to passes, sections, and agents that no longer exist (the late-iteration narrowing, the layout-splitting walk, *Savings mode*, the old agent names). It also resolves contradictions between files: the converged-review comment shape, the security-review reproducer fields, the trusted-author requirement in `assignment-compliance-check`, and `leonardo`'s model tier. Flag-only and low-confidence findings were not applied. Content tests that pinned the removed narration now check that the retired mechanisms are absent. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ❓ **Changed**: the tracker comment carries only clarifying questions that still hold and belong to the ticket. `skills/code-review-jira/references/clarifying-questions.md` gains a *Scope gate* that runs after the severity gate and the already-answered walk: it drops a question whose behaviour the current head no longer contains, and a question about pre-existing behaviour, a neighbouring feature, a refactoring, an optimization, or a follow-up idea. An unclear question is kept, and a commit alone never settles a question. `rules/code-review/general.md`, `code-review-jira`, and the GitHub wrapper contract point to it. The `Decisions before merge` section of the merge-readiness comment is unchanged, because its purpose is to ask about points outside the assignment. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🎲 **Changed**: a flaky test outside the diff and unrelated to the assignment is left alone. `skills/resolve-issue/references/quality-gates.md` *A flaky test outside the diff and the assignment is left alone* confirms the flakiness with a re-run of the test on the same commit, checks that the diff does not touch the test and that it covers neither the changed code nor the assignment's area, and then neither modifies, skips, nor files it. The failure does not block the gate and is named in the handoff and the merge report. A test that fails again, or a related or unclear one, is handled as before. `rules/code-testing/general.md` and `e2e-testing` point to it. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ⏩ **Changed**: a rebase that only moved the base no longer re-runs the whole gate. `skills/resolve-issue/references/quality-gates.md` *Rebase that moves the head — analyse the incoming changes first* compares the branch's own diff fingerprint before and after the rebase and classifies every change the default branch brought in. A related change — a file the PR touches, code it calls or is called by, the assignment's area, a conflict, or a project-wide file such as a lockfile or a checker configuration — takes the full gate exactly as before, and so does an unclear one. When every incoming change is unrelated, the gate verdict carries forward and only the dependency advisory audit runs again. `rules/git/general.md`, `merge-github-pr`, and `git-workflow` point to the analysis. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ⏱️ **Changed**: `splinter` checks a long-running dispatched agent every 15 minutes instead of every 10 (`skills/_shared/orchestration/dispatch-liveness.md`). Three checks without progress (about 45 minutes) mark the agent as stuck, and the check count stays capped at 6 per round (about 90 minutes). The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧹 **Fixed**: `rules/compound-engineering/tracker.md` *Claim a tracker issue before working on it* no longer names the removed `autoresolve-oldest-github-issue` skill. The claimed-issue exclusion now applies to any run that selects the next issue on its own. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🗳️ **Changed**: `/prepare-issue-for-merge` changes only what the assignment and the merge gate require, and a human decides everything else. `skills/verify-merge-readiness/SKILL.md` *Scope — the assignment only* allows a change for an acceptance criterion, a Critical or Moderate finding on the diff, a security point, the exact-head quality gate, CI, or a merge conflict. An optimization, a refactoring, a pre-existing problem outside the security floor, and a nice-to-have point are not implemented, not filed, and not dropped: they become open decisions. `april` publishes them in a separate `Decisions before merge` section of the source-issue TL;DR, one numbered question each, with the options *implement in this PR*, *file as a follow-up issue*, or *drop*, and a recommendation. `merge-github-pr` gains the hard gate *Open merge decisions*: it merges only after a human answers every question, in the session or in a trusted comment, and it files a follow-up issue for every *B* answer. `rules/compound-engineering/tracker.md` states the exception to its filing bar, `rules/git/pull-requests.md` names the gate, and `splinter`'s prepare-only mode repeats the scope in every dispatch. A content test pins the contract. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧪 **Added**: `test-audit` gains a `diff` mode. `donatello` runs it over the current diff before the pull request (`resolve-issue` step 16): it deletes or merges every test the diff adds that proves no logic from the assignment, keeps a modified pre-existing test under the retention bar unless removal evidence is complete, and verifies 100% coverage of the changed lines after the deletions. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ♻️ **Changed**: **DRY is one of the most important checks of every review, and no class is created for simple logic that does not repeat.** `rules/php/core-standards.md` *Design Principles* now forbids a new class — an Action above all, but also a Service, a helper, a DTO, a Data Builder, a Data Validator, or a trait — whose logic is simple and not repeated; it stays inline, and `rules/laravel/architecture.md`, `rules/refactoring/general.md`, `class-refactoring`, and `refactor-entry-point-to-action` no longer demand an Action for it. The reuse-first gate now declares its severity as **Critical**, searches the whole project, covers logic repeated inside the diff, and runs first in the core analysis; a duplicated query scope moves from Moderate to Critical to match. `donatello`, `leonardo`, `splinter`, `michelangelo`, and `raphael` each carry the DRY duty of their role, and `resolve-issue` and `test-driven-development` search for existing logic before they write any. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧪 **Added**: `test-audit`, the single authority on test value. The testing skills wrote tests to close coverage gaps, so an uncovered line was reason enough for a new test. The new skill turns the order around — behaviour, risk, or contract first, the test second, coverage only as verification — under one rule: minimum tests, maximum confidence. It has three modes. `authoring` is a four-question gate every authoring skill runs before a test is written: what the test protects, which real regression fails it, whether a stronger test already guards it, and whether it needs a test-only production seam. `cr` is a read-only code-review lens that runs only when the diff adds or modifies a test. `audit` is a read-only sweep of a named scope that runs only on an explicit request and proposes KEEP / CONSOLIDATE / MOVE / DELETE, where a `DELETE` needs recorded evidence and git history. The skill also owns the owner-boundary principle (one primary test per contract), the regression-test rules (fail before the fix, for the right reason), twelve junk patterns (the tautology list moved here from `rules/code-testing/general.md`), and a retention bar. `create-test`, `create-missing-tests-in-pr`, `test-driven-development`, and `rewrite-tests-pest` now point to it instead of restating rules: coverage alone never justifies a test, and a coverage gap with no testable contract is reported, never closed with a fake test. Four Test Hints that prescribed call-shape tests now name behaviour, a reproducer test is skipped when a finding's Test Hint says none is needed, and `e2e-testing` no longer quarantines flaky tests with `test.fixme`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧭 **Added**: a **project manifest** in the host project's `composer.json` under `extra.ai-olympus`. The package used to guess how a project runs its checks (a Phing file, then Composer scripts) and carried a fixed list of executables and sensitive paths, so a project with its own build tool had to override those guesses in prose. The manifest now states them as data: `gate`, `coverage`, `env`, `validation.executables`, `risk.critical-paths`, `language.github`, `timezone`, `tenancy`, and `product-docs`. The new `skills/_shared/read-manifest.sh` reads it from the **default branch only**, so a branch under review cannot change what the package executes. `run-validation.sh` accepts the manifest's extra `vendor/bin/*` executables and exports its `env` (only through `read-manifest.sh --env`, which refuses names such as `PATH`, `HOME`, `GIT_*`, `LD_*`, or `BASH_ENV`), `classify-risk.sh` forces `CRITICAL` on a project-declared critical path, and `skills/resolve-issue/references/quality-gates.md` discovers the gate from the manifest first; every other file points to it instead of restating the discovery order. The canonical description is `rules/general/general.md` *Project manifest*; `docs/installation.md` documents every key. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ⚖️ **Changed**: **project instructions take precedence over the packaged rules at every severity.** `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` (including the block Laravel Boost composes from `.ai/guidelines`), `.ai/rules/**`, and the manifest now win wherever they disagree with a rule or skill. The only floor is security: a project instruction cannot weaken a security check or finding, lift a merge gate, or move the untrusted-content boundary. The review no longer lets a packaged rule win just because it is Critical, raises a project-only convention at the severity the project states (Moderate by default), and also loads the Boost-layout `.ai/rules/**` files that cover the changed paths. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🎯 **Changed**: **code review knows only Critical and Moderate.** The last Minor bucket — security-lens findings published at any severity — is gone. Security lenses now map Critical → Critical and High / Medium / Low → Moderate; Info is not published. Every template, `Counts:` line, lens bucket, and Exclusion Gate clause lost its Minor slot, and the Minor-only checks in `rules/api/general.md`, `rules/laravel/livewire.md`, and `rules/php/core-standards.md` were deleted rather than upgraded. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ✂️ **Changed**: **a PHP comment carries `@` annotations and nothing else.** `rules/php/core-standards.md` *Documentation* drops the three retained-comment categories: the *what* goes into names, the *why* into the commit message, the PR description, or the ticket, and a pointer stays as `@see`. Every rule that asked for a justification in a code comment now asks for it in the PR description, and `// Arrange` / `// Act` / `// Assert` comments are no longer allowed. A comment block with prose is one Moderate finding. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧩 **Added**: Laravel Boost support. The new `rules/laravel/boost.md` covers the generated guideline file (Boost writes Claude Code's to `AGENTS.md` by default, so a committed `CLAUDE.md` must be that file or a one-line `@AGENTS.md` import), regeneration in the same commit, the 150 000-character loader limit and `@scoped` guidelines, and project skills in `.ai/skills`. `--prune` never removes a symlink that points outside the package, so the project skills Boost links into `.claude/skills` survive it, and on a Boost project the installer never writes a root `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md` — Boost generates both — while a Boost project with no `CLAUDE.md` yet receives the template as `.ai/guidelines/ai-olympus.md`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🏗️ **Added**: universal rules taken from a production Laravel project — the authenticated user is read only in an entry point; `Resolver` joins the forbidden bare suffixes with a walk that routes each responsibility to its layer; agents never generate documentation into `docs/` (except `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md`); every dispatched queue has a consumer in the deploy configuration the project already runs (Critical); a caller outside the repository is a verification gap, never proof of "unused"; a review of a changed query names its connection and blast radius; `tenancy: database` means a missing tenant `where` on the tenant connection is not an IDOR; and `laravel/pao` output is judged by the exit code alone. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🧹 **Added**: `github-issue-triage` has two new modes beside the label taxonomy. The **backlog sweep** (`skills/github-issue-triage/references/backlog-sweep.md`) closes the open issues whose every acceptance criterion is already on the default branch, keeps every epic out of other epics, moves each issue under the epic that owns it, and corrects labels and priorities only on written evidence. It reads the backlog through the new read-only `scripts/collect-sweep-evidence.sh`, which prints each open issue's epic, parent, and referencing pull requests, plus every defect of the epic tree; its `--self-test` runs in `composer build`. The **pending-analysis pass** (`skills/github-issue-triage/references/pending-analysis.md`) runs `analyze-problem` on every issue labelled `analyze`, publishes each analysis as a new `agent-note` comment, and relabels the issue. It runs in the top-level session, because a backlog run never analyses. On a public repository it skips issues labelled `security` and never publishes an unfixed vulnerability. Closing, re-parenting, and commenting stay L2: each runs only when the user's request names it, and a label change gets no comment (`rules/compound-engineering/backlog.md`, `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md`). No mode closes or edits an epic, edits an issue body, changes a workflow label such as `Resolve_by_AI`, assigns or lowers `priority: critical`, or reverts a label a person changed after the last triage.

- 🪪 **Added**: agents now recognise the operator's own tracker comments, and never trust agent output published under that same account. The account a run's tracker tool is authenticated as (`acli` for JIRA, `gh` for GitHub) belongs to the operator, and agents publish under the same account. `rules/code-review/general.md` *Authorship trust* is now the single definition, and defines the **agent-marker family by its own shape**: any `<namespace>:actor=` marker a package helper writes into a comment body, with the namespaces `cr-comment` (the upserted, one-per-actor comment), `merge-readiness` (the merge-readiness TL;DR `skills/verify-merge-readiness/SKILL.md` and `agents/april.md` publish), and `agent-note` (a separate, create-only agent comment the upsert helper never looks up or updates). A comment by the operator's account carrying no agent marker is trusted, exactly like a write-access account; a comment carrying any agent marker is agent output and is never trusted, even when that same account also holds `OWNER`/`COLLABORATOR` or is the tracker's assignee. `rules/compound-engineering/tracker.md`, `rules/security/general.md`, `rules/jira/general.md`, and `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` all point at that one definition instead of listing a subset. Every comment an agent publishes on GitHub or JIRA now carries a marker, on the canonical helper and on every MCP fallback, including the two previously undocumented ones in `skills/code-review-github/references/cr-wrapper-contract.md` and `skills/tester-cookbook/SKILL.md`; a comment path there that cannot carry one is not sanctioned. Bugsnag comments are outside the rule, because the operator test covers only the `acli` and `gh` accounts and the Bugsnag publisher carries no marker by design; label writes and status transitions carry no comment body and are outside it too. When no account e-mail can be resolved, the JIRA MCP fallback cannot compute the digest either, so the run stops as blocked. `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` and `skills/code-review-github/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` both gain a create-only `agent-note` mode: it never looks up or updates an existing comment, so a runbook or note published that way is never picked up and overwritten by a later `cr-comment` publish; every other call keeps today's lookup-and-update behaviour exactly. The JIRA helper now also refuses to publish an unmarked comment: when `acli jira auth status` carries no account e-mail, no marker can be built, so the helper exits 3 and creates nothing instead of publishing a comment a later run would read as the operator's own. It still takes its before-create snapshot in `agent-note` mode, so a create that reports no comment ID never resolves to an older agent note of the same actor — it fails closed instead of risking an overwrite or, on a failed update, a delete of that older note. `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/load-issue.sh` adds `authorAccountId` and `mentionAccountIds` (the ADF `mention` nodes' account IDs) to each comment, with a new `--self-test` mode registered in `composer.json`, so the operator and mention tests run on account IDs instead of a spoofable display name or `@Name` text. Content tests pin the texts, including one that collects every marker namespace passed to `upsert-comment.sh` across `skills/` and `agents/` and asserts it is in the family *Authorship trust* defines. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔐 **Changed**: a user-visible change is now always checked in the application's UI, and a missing login no longer stops that check. Before this change, `interactive-testing` told the testing agent to wait for the user to log in, `raphael` started a browser only when the diff changed a UI file, and the planner added `raphael` on `CRITICAL` runs only. `skills/interactive-testing/SKILL.md` gains *Sign-in and test accounts*, the one owner of the sign-in order: a valid session, then a test account the project documents, then a test account the agent creates through the project's own seed, console command, factory, or `tinker`. Creation is allowed only against a local target (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `*.test`, or a documented local instance), never bypasses authentication, uses a reserved test e-mail domain and the documented or a generated test password, never touches a real account, and is reported under **Cleanup**. Mandatory two-factor authentication without a documented test path, or any other failed source, is `Blocked` with the unblocking step. `agents/raphael.md` checks the UI in a real browser whenever the change reaches a page — a changed UI file, or a backend change a page shows — and skips the browser only for a change with no path to any page. It states that a local test account is runtime data, so its read-only boundary on tracked files holds. `skills/_shared/plan-route.sh` adds `raphael` at every tier for `--runtime-acceptance`, and `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` gains *The acceptance pass is chosen by the change, not by the tier*. `agents/splinter.md`, `docs/agents.md`, `README.md`, and `skills/e2e-testing/SKILL.md` follow. Content tests and planner self-tests pin the contract. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔁 **Changed**: `michelangelo` now runs on `opus` instead of `fable` on Claude Code. The Claude roster uses the Opus and Sonnet tiers only; the Codex adapter is unchanged.

- 📝 **Changed**: `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md` entries no longer name renamed or retired agents (`daedalus`, `athena`, `argos`) as current actors in their headings, triggers, and rules. The entry `daedalus-executes-final-merge-directly-no-specialist-owns-it` is now `splinter-executes-final-merge-directly-no-specialist-owns-it`. Dated examples keep the names used at the time.

- ✅ **Changed**: `/redesign-page` and `deliver-page-redesign` now show the redesign previews to the user and write code only after the user explicitly approves them. Before this change, the run went from the proposal straight to the implementation, so the user saw the design for the first time in the pull request. The invoking session now dispatches `michelangelo` for the design phase, shows every preview (the Read tool on Claude Code, the image viewer on Codex), and re-dispatches `michelangelo` with the user's feedback until the user approves. It records the approval in `APPROVED.md` and only then delegates the delivery route to `splinter`, without `--redesign`. `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` and `agents/splinter.md` state that an approved redesign is never replayed, so the approved proposal stays the specification `donatello` implements. The workflow text is shared by Claude Code and Codex. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🎯 **Added**: the *Acceptance-Criteria Test Contract* in `rules/code-testing/general.md`. A change is delivered only when every acceptance criterion is met, the code is the simplest design that meets the criteria, and the tests prove exactly those criteria with 100% coverage of the changed lines. Every criterion has a named test with the data the assignment states; every added or modified test proves a criterion or a failure mode the criteria or the change imply; coverage comes from those tests, and a changed line that no criterion-driven test reaches is either a missing failure-mode test or code to delete. The implementer's handoff carries a criterion → test table and the measured coverage. `rules/code-review/review-process.md` adds the reverse direction of the existing use-case check: a test that proves no criterion and no implied failure mode is a Moderate finding. `agents/donatello.md` applies the contract. A content test pins the three texts. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔁 **Changed**: `transition-to-in-progress.sh` now returns a JIRA issue from a Code Review status to In Progress when the issue's owner resumes work on the code, for example in a code-review fix round or a conflict resolution on the open pull request. Before this change, the helper refused every issue in a status containing `review` with exit 4, so an agent that changed code on an issue in Code Review either left the board saying the work waited for a reviewer, or moved the status with a raw `acli` call. Review detection is now synonym-aware, matching the same way `transition-to-code-review.sh` matches its own target: a status containing `review`, or an entry of `$JIRA_CODE_REVIEW_SYNONYMS` — so a board whose Code Review column carries no `review` substring is covered too. The return runs only when `assignee = currentUser()`; an issue in a review status that another user owns still exits 4 and is left untouched. A status that is Ready to Merge (contains `merge`, or an entry of `$JIRA_READY_TO_MERGE_SYNONYMS`) already means an approved code review sits behind the issue, so it is now refused unconditionally, before any ownership lookup, transition, or assignment — exit 4 whoever owns it; before this change the script moved such an issue to In Progress and reassigned it. The way back from Ready to Merge is `transition-to-code-review.sh`, which reverts the Code Review transition. The finished-status check still runs first and now also matches `canceled`, `merged`, `Hotovo`, a status containing `deploy`, and `Testování`/`testovani`. A failed ownership lookup on a review status exits 3 with the status and the assignment left untouched, and a fake `FAKE_ACLI_VERIFY=error` mode covers that path. `rules/jira/general.md` and `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` describe the return, the Ready-to-Merge refusal, and the exit codes. Script tests pin the owned return from review, the refused foreign return from review (including a configured synonym), the unconditional Ready-to-Merge refusal for the owner, a foreign owner, and a configured synonym, the refused finished issue across every spelling, and the failed ownership lookup. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🎫 **Changed**: `/prepare-issue-for-merge` and `verify-merge-readiness` now accept a JIRA issue key or JIRA URL as well as a GitHub issue or pull-request URL. Before this change, the skill stopped on a JIRA link because its input contract named GitHub URLs only, although its publication and cleanup steps already supported a JIRA ticket. On a JIRA source, the skill reads the assignment through `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/gather-issue-context.sh`, takes the candidate pull requests from `pullRequests[]` of the JIRA loader, keeps only open pull requests of the current repository, and runs `assert-current-repo.sh` on the resolved pull-request URL. The command, `splinter`'s prepare-only trigger, `README.md`, `docs/agents.md`, and `docs/skills.md` name the JIRA input. A content test pins the contract. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🎨 **Added**: the `/redesign-page <page URL>` slash command and the `deliver-page-redesign` skill it delegates to. The command redesigns one page of the running application end to end. The invoking session resolves the URL path to its route and views and captures desktop and mobile screenshots on the local instance. `splinter` then runs the full delivery route in thorough mode with the redesign stage, so the review and the browser walkthrough are in the plan on every tier: `michelangelo` writes the proposal and one preview per state, `donatello` implements it with the existing design system and opens the pull request, `leonardo` reviews it to convergence, and `raphael` verifies the page on the local instance of the pull-request head in its own interactive browser, on desktop and mobile viewports. No shared, staging, or production host receives a write. The skill states the acceptance criteria and the report shape, and it references `page-redesign`, `frontend-design-direction`, `design-system`, `frontend-patterns`, `frontend-a11y`, and `interactive-testing` instead of restating them. The workflow keeps the main layout shell and the business logic unchanged, and it never merges. Codex exposes no user-defined slash command, so Codex reaches the same workflow as `$deliver-page-redesign`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🌐 **Added**: a test that reaches a real DNS resolver or a raw network socket is now a **Critical** code-review finding. Before this change, `rules/code-testing/general.md` said only *"Avoid DNS lookups in tests."*, and the review's test-isolation gate covered HTTP and OS processes but not DNS. A consuming project showed the cost: production code called `dns_get_record()` directly, a namespace function override protected one namespace in one test file, and every other test performed real lookups. One test class spent 142 seconds on them. `rules/code-testing/general.md` *External Calls* now names the functions (`dns_get_record()`, `checkdnsrr()`, `getmxrr()`, `gethostbyname()`, `gethostbynamel()`, `gethostbyaddr()`, `fsockopen()`, `stream_socket_client()`, `socket_connect()`, `ftp_connect()`, `ldap_connect()`, and equivalents). It requires an injectable seam class dedicated to each capability in production code — one seam for DNS resolution, a separate seam for raw sockets — and a fake of each such seam bound by default in the base test case, and it rejects a namespace function override as a replacement. `rules/code-review/review-process.md` *Test isolation — no real HTTP, no real system processes* gains a third **Critical** gate, **Real DNS lookup or network socket**. The gate fires on a test that drives production code into such a function, and on every changed production line that calls one directly outside a seam dedicated to that capability, because the coverage rule guarantees a test executes that line. A first round of review found that this coverage requirement could not be met by the seam's own body: the seam's delegation line is itself a changed line calling the native function, so covering it meant a real lookup and not covering it meant a Coverage finding. `rules/code-testing/general.md` *External Calls* now states the fix: the seam class's body is a single call to the native function with no other logic, and that one delegation line is exempt from Coverage — excluded from the default run with a documented marker such as `@codeCoverageIgnore`, or covered only inside the project's explicitly isolated integration test group. `rules/code-review/review-process.md` cites that exemption instead of raising either gate. It hands HTTP-level calls to the outbound-HTTP gate and the validation of a resolved address to the SSRF section, so one line never gets two findings. `rules/laravel/laravel.md` *Testing* gains the Laravel form: a resolver seam for DNS lookups and a separate connector seam for raw sockets, each faked in `tests/TestCase.php`. A new content test pins the gate and the contract. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🎚️ **Changed**: the reasoning effort of three agents. `april` moves from `low` to `medium` on Claude Code, which matches its Codex adapter. `donatello` moves from `medium` to `high` on Claude Code and on Codex (`codex/agents/donatello.toml`). `raphael` moves from `medium` to `high` on Claude Code, which matches its Codex adapter. No model tier changes. `docs/agents.md` states the new values. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔓 **Fixed**: the `--allow-bundled-scripts` permissions never matched. The installer wrote `Bash(*skills/code-review-github/scripts/load-issue.sh:*)` and the JIRA twin. Claude Code reads a pattern that mixes a leading `*` with the `:*` suffix as a literal prefix, so the leading `*` never expanded, and Claude Code warned about both entries. The patterns now end with ` *`. The installer removes the two legacy `:*` entries from `~/.claude/settings.json` when it adds the new ones. `SECURITY.md` and `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md` cite the new form. The package owner requested the fix directly.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the always-on rules are cut by half again. They drop from **65 714 to 30 848 bytes** (34 471 with the installed `templates/CLAUDE.md`). Every session of every consuming project therefore sends about 8 700 fewer tokens before it reads the task. Codex benefits in the same way, because `AGENTS.md` applies a rule without `paths` to every task. Three moves carry the change, and each follows the seam c5d12e6 used for `tracker.md`: the sections move **verbatim**, a pointer section stays in the always-on file, and the moved file loads on demand. **(1)** `rules/git/general.md` keeps what every commit needs: branch context, the git rules, worktrees, pull policy, commit messages, cleanup, and tooling. *Issue Linking*, *Pull Requests* (with *Draft pull requests*), *PR Lifecycle*, and *Merging* (with the HOTFIX, `FAST`-tier, and dependency-only cases) move into a new `rules/git/pull-requests.md`. That file is scoped to `.claude/run/**` and its own installed path, and the size drops from 24 667 to 10 622 bytes always-on. The only edited sentence is the *PR Lifecycle* pointer to *Worktrees / Workspaces*, which now names `@rules/git/general.md` because that section stayed behind. **(2)** `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` moves *Compound Memory (per project)* into a new `rules/compound-engineering/memory.md`. That file is scoped to `.claude/run/**`, to `docs/memory/**` (reading the memory file itself attaches its protocol), and to its own installed path, and the size drops from 14 375 to 4 260 bytes always-on. **(3)** `rules/reports/general.md` becomes on demand, scoped to `.claude/rules/reports/**`. This reverses the c5d12e6 decision to keep it always-on, deliberately and on the record. Every publishing skill already names it directly or through `cr-wrapper-contract.md`. For an ad-hoc publish outside a skill, `rules/writing/general.md` gains the pointer *a run that publishes a report to a tracker reads and applies `@rules/reports/general.md` first*. It does not move `security/general.md`, which must be present exactly when external content arrives unannounced (#12). **References:** 20 section references to the git sections and 28 references to the memory sections across `skills/`, `agents/`, and `rules/` now name the new files. `verify-merge-readiness` and `cr-wrapper-contract.md` gain an explicit `Apply @rules/git/pull-requests.md`. Tests read both halves through the new `gitRuleContents()` and `compoundEngineeringRuleContents()` helpers. The scoping registries, the always-on list (six down to five), `docs/rules.md`, and the README Rules Overview record the three files. A new test fails when one of the three pointers disappears. **Deliberately not changed:** `rules/laravel/laravel.md` keeps `**/*.php`. It also loads in a non-Laravel PHP repository, but #274 scoped it that way so that a Laravel package under `src/` still gets it, and no path glob can tell a Laravel package from a plain PHP one. The package owner requested the change directly.

- ❓ **Added**: a question raised during a code review now gets a verified answer and a recommendation that fits the reviewed application. Before this change, a reviewer's question was handled like an instruction to change code, and no rule said how to answer it. `rules/code-review/general.md` gains *Answering a question raised during a review*. Every sentence of an answer comes from code, a test, or a command this run opened or ran on the checked-out head. A point that cannot be verified is labelled *Not verified*, together with the step that settles it. The recommendation first looks for how the project already solves the problem, follows the order reuse → extend the owning layer → framework or installed dependency → small new part, and never removes a security check. An answer that finds a defect also raises that defect as an ordinary finding. The answer has a fixed, human-readable shape: **Answer**, **Evidence**, **Recommendation**, **Not verified**. `process-code-review` treats a reviewer question as its own checklist item and never turns it into an unrequested patch. It publishes the answers in a new conditional `## Answers to reviewer questions` section of the one CR comment, re-checked against the final head, and leaves a question-only thread for the asker to resolve. The Reviewer Comment Fulfillment Gate in `cr-wrapper-contract.md` counts an open question as actionable and gains the *Answered* disposition. An answer whose evidence no longer holds on the current head is not fulfilled. No new GitHub write surface is added: the answers travel in the existing `cr-comment`. The package owner requested the change directly.

- 🔂 **Fixed**: a failed or retried publish no longer leaves duplicate comments. On a real JIRA ticket one TL;DR landed three times: the create succeeded, the helper could not read the new comment's ID and exited 3, and each retry created another copy. `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` now never creates a comment it cannot prove is the first one. It matches the author on the Jira account ID, which `jira_actor_account_id` in `jira-actor.sh` resolves through JQL `currentUser()`, so the match still holds when Jira Cloud hides `author.emailAddress`. A failed comment lookup, or a comment carrying this actor's marker whose author stays undecidable, now exits 3 without creating anything, so a rerun updates the comment an earlier attempt created or refuses. `skills/code-review-github/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` stops POSTing a new comment when its lookup fails, for the same reason. `rules/jira/general.md` states the contract for callers: one comment per actor per issue, holding the final result, and a JIRA MCP fallback updates that comment instead of adding a second one.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: every consuming project exceeded the **150 000-character total limit Claude Code now enforces across all always-loaded instruction files** — not only per file. A consumer reported `⚠ 13 instruction files add up to 395.5k chars, over the 150.0k-char total limit · largest: .claude/rules/code-review/core-analysis.md (107.5k), .claude/rules/code-review/general.md (71.3k), CLAUDE.md (54.5k)`. Always-loaded means the project `CLAUDE.md`, the user's memory, and every rule without a `paths:` key (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory: *“Rules without a `paths` field are loaded unconditionally”*). The eleven always-on rules this package shipped came to **344 317 bytes** on their own — **347 940** with the installed `templates/CLAUDE.md` — so a project was over the limit before a single line of its own instructions was counted. **The always-on total is now 69 337 bytes** (65 714 of rules plus the template), an 80 % cut. Three changes carry it. **(1) Five activity rules become on demand.** `rules/code-review/general.md`, `core-analysis.md` and `review-process.md` declare `paths: [".claude/rules/code-review/**"]`, `rules/jira/general.md` declares `.claude/rules/jira/**`, and `rules/refactoring/general.md` declares `.claude/rules/refactoring/**` — each is scoped to its own installed path, so it attaches exactly when an agent reads it, which is what every skill and agent running the activity is already told to do through its `@rules/…` reference; a new test fails the suite when one of them loses its last reference in `skills/` or `agents/`. `.claude/run/**` is deliberately **not** given to the code-review files: every orchestrated run touches that directory, the orchestrator included, and the glob would pull 225 kB of review rules into sessions that never review. **(2) `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` splits again, at the same seam kind #275 used.** The seven tracker-workflow sections — *Analyze every comment before you act on a tracker assignment*, *Fix the cause first, then repair the data it already wrote*, *Claim a tracker issue before working on it*, *Tracker status tracks the phase of work*, *Every pull request links back to its tracker issue*, *File deferred points as follow-up tracker issues*, *Label tracker issues, and keep the labels true* — move **verbatim** into a new `rules/compound-engineering/tracker.md`, scoped to `.claude/run/**` (the orchestrator's gather phase applies the comment analysis and the claim before any dispatch) and to its own installed path. The always-on file keeps Compound Engineering, Compound Memory with its read, slice and write protocols, What Not To Do, Blocked delegation is a hard stop, and Code Review Application (53 575 → 14 375 bytes, the new pointer section included), and gains *Tracker workflow — the companion file*, which names the seven sections and tells every run that reads, claims, updates, links, labels, or files a tracker item to read and apply `tracker.md` first. **No normative sentence changed or was lost**: the moved block is byte-identical, and every cross-reference inside it points within it.
