Every `@rules/compound-engineering/general.md *Section*` reference to a moved section across `skills/`, `agents/`, `rules/`, `docs/`, and `tests/` now names `tracker.md` — including five a section-name sweep could not see because the reference wraps a line, follows the section name, or names no section; the one in `file-deferred-moderate.sh` also still cited the retired name *Label newly created tracker issues*. **(3) A total-budget guard.** `tests/Installer/InstructionFileSizeTest.php` sums every rule without a `paths:` key — read from the frontmatter, so an unregistered new always-on rule still counts — plus `templates/CLAUDE.md`, and fails above **75 000 bytes**, half the limit: the other half belongs to the consuming project, whose own `CLAUDE.md` alone was 54.5k in the report above. `reports/general.md` (every published report) and `security/general.md` (the Untrusted Content Boundary, which must be present on exactly the runs where external content arrives unannounced — the #12 reasoning still holds for it) stay always-on. **This reverses the #45 decision, deliberately and on the record.** #45 made these activity rules always-on because no path glob names an activity, and concluded that their token cost was *“a content problem, not a frontmatter one”*. That held while the only limit was per file. The total limit is the owner decision that reopens it: a package whose always-on baseline is more than twice the whole budget cannot be fixed by trimming content, and scoping each activity rule to its own installed path names the activity after all — the moment of reading the file. `ruleExtensionAlwaysOnFiles()` drops to six, `ruleScopingGlobsAddedByTotalBudget()` registers the six new scopes, the consumer-path corpus gains the four `.claude/rules/…` paths the new globs match, and `docs/rules.md` and the README Rules Overview gain an *On-demand activity rules* group. No tracker issue exists; the package owner requested the fix directly.

- **Changed**: renamed the six agent identities to Splinter (`daedalus`), Donatello (`hephaestus`), Leonardo (`athena`), Raphael (`argus`), Michelangelo (`apollo`), and April O’Neil (`hermes`) while preserving their roles, tool permissions, model tiers, and handoff contracts. Added matching transparent Krang-style portraits, thumbnails, shared activity animations, an interactive preview, and a portable asset manifest. Agent assets now ship in distribution archives. Normal installer upgrades remove only verified, unchanged definitions under the previous names; customized definitions remain for manual review.

- 🧾 **Fixed**: the JIRA loader no longer loses what a comment says. `load-issue.sh` took every comment `body` from `acli jira workitem comment list`, which flattens the ADF itself and drops mentions, emoji, links, and the text of every list item — a decision written as a bullet vanished, and an orchestrator reported a Critical "acceptance criterion undecided" that the reporter had already decided. The body is now rendered from the ADF the issue view embeds, paired by comment `id`, with the flattened text as the fallback for a comment the view does not embed (disclosed on stderr). The ADF renderer keeps mentions, emoji, smart links, link targets, status and date values, `- ` / `1. ` list markers indented per nesting level, tasks, rules, tables, and expands; `descriptionText` uses the same renderer. `load-issue.sh` and `parse-comments.sh` now expose each comment `id`. Reported from a merge preparation of a JIRA task.

- 🧹 **Added**: `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/delete-owned-comment.sh`, the JIRA counterpart of `delete-owned-github-comment.sh`. It deletes one comment only when it is on the exact issue, is neither protected ID (final TL;DR, current CR evidence), carries this account's `_cr-comment:actor=<actor-digest>_` marker, and shares the author account ID of the protected final TL;DR this run published — the marker alone is copyable text and acli exposes no account ID for the current user. A visible author e-mail must match too. It re-reads the issue after `acli jira workitem comment delete` and reports success only when the comment is gone. The digest now comes from one shared `jira-actor.sh`, which `upsert-comment.sh` also sources. `hermes` merge-preparation consolidation, `verify-merge-readiness`, the orchestration rule, and `rules/jira/general.md` route JIRA cleanup through it, so the duplicates a failed `upsert-comment.sh` run leaves behind are removed after a verified publish instead of becoming a blocker. Reported from a merge preparation of a JIRA task.

- **Added**: `interactive-testing` verifies an assignment and its code diff through the testing agent's own interactive browser. It waits for user login when needed, checks persistence and responsive layouts, and reports failures and blocked scenarios with evidence. The target URL comes from the consuming project.

- 🔁 **Added**: reuse an existing Eloquent query scope before a new one is written. A scope is the reusable name of a condition, so a second scope for a condition the model already carries splits one filter into two definitions — they drift apart the moment one of them is corrected, and the caller reading the stale one keeps working while it returns the wrong rows. `rules/laravel/laravel.md` `## Database and Eloquent` now states the gate: search the model, every trait it uses, and its parent classes first, match on the **condition** rather than the name (`scopeActive()` and `scopeNotDeleted()` over the same column are one filter under two names), call the existing scope when it already expresses the filter, and widen it with the parameter the new call site needs when it almost fits — a near-copy under a new name is the violation. A condition no existing scope expresses still earns its own scope, so the rule collapses duplicates rather than filters. `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` gains the matching walk, *New Eloquent query scope added (Laravel)*, at **Moderate**, on every Laravel project and regardless of whether `pekral/arch-app-services` is installed, with the two literal Suggested Fix templates (call the existing scope, or widen it) and gating that hands an inline condition in an entry point to *Inline Eloquent / query-builder outside repository layer* and a feature-specific Repository method to *Repository scope*, so no line carries two findings.

- ✨ **Added**: three code conventions and the review walks that enforce them. `rules/php/core-standards.md` `## Structure` states that **a method called only from inside its own class is `private`** — `protected` only for a project subclass, `public` only for another class, a test never counts as a caller, and a method the framework, a vendor contract, a container, or a trait's users call keeps its wider visibility. `## Testing` states that **visibility is never widened for a test**: no `private` made `protected` / `public`, no test-visibility annotation, no test-only subclass re-exposing a member, and no reflection reaching a private member — the test goes through the public method, or the logic moves into its own class with its own public method. `## PHP Practices` states that **a local variable must do work** — a variable only returned, read once directly below, copying another value, or naming an argument is inlined, while a variable read twice, explaining a non-obvious expression, splitting a long one, fixing evaluation order, required by a language construct, or narrowing a type stays. `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` gains the matching walks: *Method visibility wider than its callers need* (**Moderate**), *Visibility widened for a test* (**Critical**), and *Unnecessary local variable* (**Moderate**), each gated so no method or line carries two findings. `rules/code-testing/general.md` points test authors at the visibility rule.

- ✨ **Added**: eight conventions the rule set did not carry, each measured against what it costs a reader. `rules/php/core-standards.md` gains `## Time` — every date call names its timezone, one zone for computing and storing, conversion only at the boundary, no SQL `NOW()`, and a zone on every value crossing a process boundary; `rules/laravel/laravel.md` gains the Laravel half, where `config('app.timezone')` is the single source and the explicit argument changes nothing at runtime and everything for the reader. `## Structure` gains the **size ratchet**: a file under 400 lines takes new code freely, a file over it must not gain net lines, a file over 800 is expected to leave the change shorter, and the same applies to a method pushed past ~50 lines — a new class the old one calls, never a `FooHelper` holding the overflow. `## String Emptiness Checks` gains the reason it exists — `''` is never the only empty case, a single space satisfies a *required* guard and stores as a value that renders as nothing, and `empty()` is not the substitute because it rejects the legitimate `'0'`. `rules/laravel/architecture.md` gains three contracts: every DTO extends `Spatie\LaravelData\Data` on a project that installs it, and a `Data` subclass is **never** a `readonly class` — PHP refuses to load it (`Readonly class … cannot extend non-readonly class`), so every fixer, analyser, and review stays green and the failure lands in whichever request first touches the DTO; a data-building class carries the `DataBuilder` suffix and no other, and builds one shape rather than two unrelated payloads; a rule set is a `ValidationRules`-suffixed trait in `app/Concerns/`, parameterised by the attribute path instead of forked per surface. `rules/code-review/general.md` gains three sections: **a `foreach` is never a finding** at any severity (the choice against a `collect()` chain is a style preference between two constructs with identical behaviour — the defect *inside* the loop keeps its own owner, and `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` *Collection pipelines are fluent* drops its `foreach` clause accordingly); **a review comment assigned to somebody else is left alone**, decided mechanically from an `@mention` that states in words who resolves it, a trusted author, and the acting account read from the tool — every ambiguity resolving toward *handle it normally*, and a security finding never settled by an assignment; and **published product documentation is a requirement the assignment need not restate**, reported on both surfaces (a Moderate finding on the pull request, a *Clarifying questions* entry on the tracker) because only the person who maintains the promise can decide which side is wrong. `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` *Label newly created tracker issues* becomes *Label tracker issues, and keep the labels true*: a label that stopped being true is corrected in the same action that adds the one that is, a label contradicting the diff is a **Moderate** finding with the exact `gh pr edit` that fixes it, and an item carrying **no** label is never a finding at all. Every new walk carries its severity and its gating, so no diff line produces two findings. Sourced from a consuming project's own guidelines and filtered to what holds in any project — the project's checker wiring was deliberately left out.

- 🛑 **Changed**: An EPIC tree is never created without a human approving it. Splitting a subject too broad for one PR used to create whatever the split produced under the L1 consent of the original request — including an **EPIC parent with sub-issues**, a structure somebody then has to own, re-triage, and close. That half is now **L2**: the decomposition presents one confirmation package (the parent's title and scope, one line per proposed sub-issue, the dependencies, the resolve order), reports `Breakdown proposed — awaiting approval`, and stops. Only an explicit approval in words permits the writes, and only for the pieces the package names — silence is not approval, the original request is not approval, and an approval does not carry to a changed split. Nothing is lost when the user does not answer: the proposed pieces are in the report. A **flat** set of peer issues is outside the gate and keeps its existing L1 consent, because that is a list of work the request actually asked for.

- ⚡ **Changed**: `athena` reviews the assignment, not the repository — and files nothing itself. The review's subject is what the assignment asked for and the diff that fulfils it. A defect that **predates** the change is no longer athena's to queue: it is not a finding, it does not gate convergence, and only a **Critical** one leaves the run at all, as a `critical out-of-scope findings` entry in the handoff. `daedalus` then judges the severity itself and either files one issue through `create-issue` or **drops the item**, saying so in its report — dropping is a legitimate outcome, because a backlog entry nobody decided to do is a cost every later triage pays again. Two things are deliberately unchanged: a security item is marked `do not file publicly` by athena and honoured by daedalus, so handing a vulnerability on never publishes it; and the three-round cap is restated as a **deadline rather than a budget** — never raise it, never restart the loop for another three, and never split one issue's review to work around it, while a Critical and a security-relevant Moderate stay hard blockers at round 3 exactly as at round 1. The speed comes from reviewing a diff instead of a repository, never from accepting a defect because the clock ran out.

- 🧭 **Changed**: `daedalus` routes a page redesign to `apollo`. `plan-route.sh --redesign` places the stage **before** `hephaestus`, because the proposal is the specification the implementation builds from — an implementer that ran first would be inventing the layout the stage exists to decide. The stage is **not gated on the tier**: a page needs a redesign because of what the task asks for, never because of how risky the change is, so it applies at `FAST`, `STANDARD`, and `CRITICAL` alike and composes with the security analysis rather than replacing it (a `CRITICAL` redesign analyses security, then designs, then implements). A post-implementation escalation never replays it — the redesign already ran, and a second one would hand the implementer a competing specification. A redesign request with **no** implementation ask stays an analysis-only run whose deliverable is the proposal itself, which makes `apollo` that branch's second specialist beside `athena`. An unregistered `apollo` stops the run with its remediation rather than letting `hephaestus` invent a layout.

- ☀️ **Added**: `apollo`, the sixth agent — the page redesigner. It owns `page-redesign` end to end: it reads the real view, maps every state the main screenshot hides, lays the content region out against the operator's task sequence rather than the field list, and hands back a developer-ready specification with one rendered preview per state. Two boundaries define the role and both are enforced by its tool grant rather than only by its prose: the application's **main layout shell is never touched** without an explicit order, and it **proposes rather than implements** — it holds `Write` for its own proposal, mockups, and previews under the path the caller named, and no `Edit` tool at all, so no file the application ships is changeable by it even by accident. It publishes nothing and never commits, pushes, or merges. Models: `fable` at `medium` effort on Claude Code, `astra` at `medium` on Codex. **Its portrait is the universal `placeholder.svg`**, not a painted namesake like the other five — `docs/agents.md` names the three files to replace together when the artwork exists. One naming hazard is recorded rather than left to be discovered: the retired `apollon` (test engineer and post-convergence reporter) is **not** this `apollo`, and the retired-agents table now says so.

- 🎚️ **Changed**: The Codex adapters declare their own model and reasoning effort as real config. Each `codex/agents/*.toml` now carries a `model` key and a `model_reasoning_effort` key — `athena` and `daedalus` on `sol`, `argus` and `hermes` on `luna`, `hephaestus` on `terra`, at `high` for the two roles whose depth decides a round (`athena`, `argus`) and `medium` for the rest. The adapter used to carry no `model` key at all and describe its default tier in prose, which left the choice to whatever the session happened to be configured with — the opposite of *declared per platform in the agent's own definition*. The tier block below the keys states the same two values, and a test pins both, so neither can move without the other. The escalated tier stays described rather than pinned: it is whatever the strongest model the session can select turns out to be.

- 🧭 **Added**: `page-redesign`. A skill for redesigning an existing page around the person who actually uses it — a warehouse, workshop, or shop-floor operator who is not an IT person and needs the software to get an order out. It reads the real view, maps **every** state the main screenshot hides (collapsed sections, tabs, modals, error and empty states, permission variants), lays out the content region against the operator's task sequence rather than the field list, and ships one HTML mockup and one rendered PNG per state through `scripts/render-previews.sh`, which drives the existing project-independent Playwright runner. Two boundaries are the point: the application's **main layout shell is never touched** without an explicit order, and the skill **proposes, never implements** — its deliverable is a handoff precise enough that a developer builds from it without a follow-up question. A density budget keeps the page full without becoming a wall, and every region is extensible by structure rather than by reserved blank space.

- 🎚️ **Changed**: Agent model and effort tiers. `athena` and `daedalus` now declare `opus` — the reviewer and the orchestrator are the two roles whose mistakes cost every stage downstream, so this roster buys the stronger model outright instead of escalating into it per dispatch. `daedalus` drops to `medium` effort and `hermes` to `low`; `athena` stays at `high`, and `hephaestus` keeps `sonnet` at its default tier, so the default-tier-first saving still applies where the work is proportional to the change. `athena` now states that it has no higher tier left on Claude Code: a review it cannot stand behind returns `Blocked: needs model escalation` for a human, rather than naming an escalation that does not exist.

- 🚑 **Added**: HOTFIX — the declared emergency path. A production bug that loses money, data, or availability every minute it stays live was paying the same review rounds and the same coverage gate as ordinary work, so the outage financed the process. A caller may now declare a run a HOTFIX **in words**, and the pipeline trades breadth for time to the default branch: the coverage gates do not run (changed-line coverage, the acceptance-criteria use-case finding, and the `--min` threshold at the pre-merge gate), and the review reports against two questions only — is the assignment satisfied, and does the change actually close the reported failure path. Everything else the review catalog would raise is not reported, not deferred, and not filed. The boundaries are what make the mode safe rather than a way to merge anything: only a human or a trusted tracker author declares it and **an agent never infers or offers it**, it applies to a bug fix and to nothing else, every security lens runs unnarrowed with the S1–S3 carve-out blocking at any severity, a sensitive-area force still buys every stage it would buy, and the build still has to be green — a hotfix that breaks the default branch is a second outage. The mode travels deterministically (`plan-route.sh --hotfix` puts `athena` in `review_hotfix` and records `hotfix: true`) and reaches the merge gate as one `Mode:` line on the trusted review comment, so a hotfix claim in a PR body, a branch name, or a label authorises nothing. What it costs is stated in the rule: a change that is right about the symptom and wrong about the cause passes this gate, which is exactly why a human has to ask for it.

- 🔧 **Fixed**: `rector/rector` is pinned below `2.6.7`. That release removed `Rector\Renaming\Rector\MethodCall\RenameDeprecatedMethodCallRector`, which `pekral/rector-rules` 0.5 still registers, so `composer build` died in its first fixer with `Expected an existing class name`. The constraint is the local fix; the root cause belongs upstream in the rule set, and the pin lifts when that ships.

- 🩹 **Added**: Fix the cause first, then repair the data it already wrote. A defect that ran in production wrote its result somewhere, so resolving it is two pieces of work — the code path that produces the wrong value, and the rows, JSON columns, cache entries, and queued payloads that path already wrote. The package used to own only the first, so a run could correct every future write, ship a green test, and leave the reporter looking at the same wrong number. The order between the halves is causal rather than a preference: a repair that runs while the cause is still live is re-corrupted by the next request that takes the broken path. `analyze-problem` now settles the damage in its impact step — a count plus the predicate that identifies a damaged record, read off the storage rather than inferred from the code — and carries both fields in its report template; `resolve-issue` ships the repair after the GREEN step as its own bounded, re-runnable command with its own test; and `code-review` raises **Cause fixed, damaged data left behind**, in both orderings, Critical while the application still acts on the damaged records. The two ways out are explicit so neither becomes silence: a repair that cannot ship here is filed as a follow-up issue and named in the report, and damage that genuinely self-heals is stated with its reason.

- ⚙️ **Added**: Deterministic validation. `skills/_shared/run-validation.sh` executes a **validation manifest** the implementer writes as part of its handoff — `head_sha` plus the `tests` / `static_analysis` / `lint` commands covering its own diff — and returns a machine-readable verdict with an explicit `escalate` field. A green run closes the validation stage with **no agent session**; `hephaestus` is dispatched only when the runner escalates (a failure that needs interpreting, or a manifest that is invalid, stale, or refused). **The manifest is never passed to a shell:** commands are split into an argv array and executed directly, against an allow-list of project-local tools, with shell metacharacters, quotes, absolute paths, and traversal refused outright. The manifest is written by an agent whose context carries tracker text anyone can write, so a manifest reaching a shell would be arbitrary code execution in the one step no human reviews. An empty manifest is `invalid`, never a pass: *nothing to run* and *I could not work out what to run* are indistinguishable to the reader, and the second must escalate.

- 🧭 **Added**: A deterministic route planner. `skills/_shared/plan-route.sh` turns the tier and a few flags into the run's stage list — which specialist, in which mode, at which model tier, and where the deterministic stages sit — and `daedalus` executes it instead of reconstructing the workflow from prose at every step. Four stage types keep the expensive thing visible: `agent` is an LLM dispatch, while `deterministic_validation`, `deterministic_classification`, and `deterministic_reporting` are scripts. A post-implementation escalation asks the planner what the raised tier owes and gets exactly the stages the lower tier skipped, never a replay of the implementation. The whole routing contract is now asserted rather than read.

- 🔁 **Changed**: Context-efficient orchestration is the default and has no flag. It was an opt-in *savings mode* a user had to ask for by name — but removing orchestration overhead skips no gate, changes no reviewer, and weakens no convergence criterion, so there was nothing for the opt-in to protect and every run that forgot it paid for nothing. The `## Savings mode` and `## Context pack` brief fields are withdrawn. `--verbose-orchestration` replaces the toggle as a **debugging** escape hatch: it adds narration and never a check, so a verbose run is never more correct — the rule says so explicitly, because otherwise every careful caller would enable it.

- 📉 **Added**: Bounded structured handoffs. `skills/_shared/check-handoff.sh` validates a handoff against its role's schema and a size budget (600 prose-equivalent words by default), and rejects one that inlines a diff or test output instead of referencing the artifact. A handoff is re-read by every later stage, so whatever it carries is re-tokenised once per stage — a three-round review used to pay for the same pasted evidence four times.

- ✂️ **Changed**: `daedalus` carries 20 % less always-loaded context. Three rare procedures moved to `skills/_shared/orchestration/` behind explicit loading triggers — the startup sweep (`stale-run-recovery.md`), the long-running-dispatch liveness check (`dispatch-liveness.md`), and prepare-only mode (`merge-preparation.md`) — taking the base prompt from 124 628 to 99 551 bytes. **No behaviour was removed:** the text is verbatim, each reference states the condition that loads it, and `daedalusContractText()` in the test suite folds the references back in so every existing content pin still asserts against the whole contract.

- 📝 **Changed**: The routine completion report is rendered, not written by a model. `skills/_shared/render-report.sh` builds it from the run's own structured data — what changed, validation, review, changed-file count, PR link, how-to-test — in the assignment's language. `hermes` is now dispatched only when the audience needs real writing: an announcement, changelog prose, a stakeholder note, or a language the renderer does not carry (it supports `en` and `cs` and refuses anything else rather than emitting English onto a tracker).

- 📊 **Added**: Persistent orchestration metrics and `ai-olympus stats`. Each completed run appends **counts only** to a local JSONL store outside the repository (`~/.ai-olympus/metrics.jsonl`, or `$AI_OLYMPUS_HOME`) — tier in and out, outcome, agent dispatches, model escalations, review rounds, finding counts. No source, diffs, prompts, issue text, branch names, or URLs are written, and the writer enforces that by validating every field and refusing an unknown flag. `vendor/bin/ai-olympus stats --last=7d|30d|all` summarises it and surfaces the three signals a single run cannot: the FAST escalation rate, the review zero-finding rate, and expensive-tier usage. Token fields stay optional — an absent number rather than a fabricated one.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: `agents/hephaestus.md` still told the implementer its pre-PR self-check runs `code-review` + `security-review` over its own diff and gates PR creation on the findings — the duplicate review that was removed from `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md`, and the direct opposite of the *never review your own work* boundary stated three lines above it in the same file. Two more copies sat in `docs/agents.md`, and `agents/daedalus.md`'s athena-not-registered branch named that pass as the security-analysis fallback, which would have reported a security review nobody performed. All four now defer to the skill that owns the pass, and the unregistered-athena fallback says plainly that no pre-implementation analysis runs and that the security review still reaches the PR through `code-review-github`. A test pins the **class** rather than the four sentences: no file under `rules/`, `skills/`, `agents/`, or `docs/` may phrase the self-check as running a review skill, while the negative statements that describe the removal keep passing.

- 🔁 **Changed**: The routing and model contracts apply on OpenAI / Codex, not only on Claude Code. The tiers are written as roles — *default tier* and *escalated tier* — and the rule owns only **when** a step escalates. **Which model a tier means is declared in that agent's own definition for that platform**: the `model:` frontmatter of `agents/<name>.md` on Claude Code, and a *Model tiers for this role on Codex* block in `codex/agents/<name>.toml` on Codex, including for the three roles that never escalate, which say so explicitly rather than leaving it unstated. A rule naming a vendor's model is wrong on the other platform the day it is written and wrong on both the first time a model is renamed. A session that cannot change the model for a single step applies what it can and records that in the escalation line — never an escalation that did not happen. Two mapping gaps are closed while the adapters are open: every Codex adapter and `AGENTS.md` now map a **bare** `skills/<path>` script reference (the classifier, and the pre-existing deterministic loaders) to `.agents/skills/<path>`, which `@skills/<path>` alone never covered.

- ✂️ **Removed**: The commit-splitting mandate. The package required one commit per phase and one per enumerated assignment point, ordered so each was independently cherry-pickable, with no commit shipping a symbol its own tree did not yet call — enforced through a commit-plan table written before the first line of code and reconciled against the history before the PR. None of it was ever a review criterion: the review reads the diff that will be merged, and the granularity of the commits under it changes nothing about what that diff does. `skills/resolve-issue/references/phase-planning.md` goes with the mandate it executed, and the PR `## Changes` rules it also owned move to `references/pull-request.md`, which owns the PR body. Three things survive because none is about granularity: a test and the change that makes it pass land in the same commit, the merged head is green, and a branch under review is not rewritten. What is lost is stated in the rule: a merged branch's commit list no longer maps onto the assignment's points, and a mid-branch commit is not known to be cherry-pickable.

- 🗑️ **Changed**: The filing bar now files exactly two things: a critical gap in the business logic the assignment asked for, and a critical security issue. It used to admit three broader categories, one of which — technical debt with a named consequence — covers every refactoring proposal an agent can argue for, so the backlog filled with work nobody had decided to do. Everything else is now ignored rather than filed or carried forward: refactoring follow-ups of any kind, architectural cleanups, debt, performance ideas with no breached limit, naming and cosmetics, nice-to-have work. A deferred assignment point below the bar stays visible in the PR `## TODO` and the final report, so a scope cut is still never silent — it is documented rather than scheduled. One consequence is named where it lands: a round-3 non-security Moderate that is a refactoring no longer becomes a sub-issue, so it is fixed in the loop or it blocks.

- ⚡ **Added**: Adaptive routing. Every `daedalus` run now classifies its task before it dispatches anything and routes it to one of three pipelines — `FAST` (implementer plus deterministic validation), `STANDARD` (plus `athena`), `CRITICAL` (plus the upfront security analysis where relevant, both agents on opus, `argus` when behaviour is observable). The pipeline used to be one shape for every task, so a README typo bought the same agent sessions, the same expensive models, and the same review passes as an authorization rewrite. `skills/_shared/classify-risk.sh` decides the tier: a shell script rather than another model call, because a router that asks an LLM how risky a task is adds an LLM call to save LLM calls and answers differently on every run. It prints its score and every signal that produced it, so a routing decision can be argued with. Auth / secrets, migrations / data-loss, and payments / billing each force `CRITICAL` on their own, and a lower explicit override is refused rather than applied. The tier is recomputed against the real diff after implementation with the first verdict as a floor, so a task that grew into an authorization change is reviewed like one and a tier can never fall. Tests, static analysis, linting, CI, and the pre-merge quality gate run unchanged at every tier — the tier buys LLM reasoning, never a deterministic gate.

- 💸 **Changed**: `hephaestus` and `athena` default to `model: sonnet`. Both were pinned to `opus`, which priced every run at the most expensive tier regardless of what it was doing. `daedalus` now passes an explicit `model` override on the dispatch when the tier is `CRITICAL`, when a sonnet attempt returned the new `Blocked: needs model escalation` handoff, or when the step turns on architectural or security-sensitive reasoning — and writes an `escalation` line naming the reason before the dispatch it applies to. Escalation follows evidence rather than preceding it. Effort is untouched and orthogonal: `athena` on sonnet still reviews at `high`.

- ♻️ **Changed**: One authoritative LLM review, not two. `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` ran a full `code-review` **and** a full `security-review` over its own diff before opening the PR, after which `athena` ran the same two lenses over the same diff again — a complete duplicate review on every run, for a slightly cleaner starting point. The implementer now runs a deterministic *Pre-PR self-check* instead: acceptance criteria covered, the tests covering the change executed, static analysis and linters green, no debug artifact, no accidental file, the diff inside the requested scope, and anything that warrants escalation named. It grades nothing and gates on no finding count. What is lost is stated in `skills/resolve-issue/references/pre-pr-self-check.md`: `athena` reads a less pre-polished diff, so a finding the implementer would have quietly fixed can cost one review round.

- 🔀 **Added**: A `FAST`-tier pull request is exempt from the code-review merge gate, alongside the existing dependency-only exemption. `skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md` qualifies it from evidence it produces itself — it re-runs the classifier over the PR's own `files[]` and requires `tier=FAST` with `forced=none` — never from a tier claimed in a PR body, a branch name, or a label, and a missing classifier voids the exemption rather than opening it. CI, the pre-merge quality gate, conflicts, Draft state, approvals, and branch freshness all still apply. Because no review loop runs on that tier, `daedalus` promotes the PR out of Draft itself, gated on the final classification still reading `FAST` and on a green scoped validation.

- 🔭 **Added**: A routing ledger at `.claude/run/<source-slug>.routing`, the third sibling of the dispatch ledger and the audit trail. It records the initial and final tier with the classifier's own score and signals, every stage executed or skipped with its reason, and every model escalation with its reason — so *"why was `athena` executed?"*, *"why was opus used?"* and *"why was this `CRITICAL`?"* are answerable from the record. Dispatch, escalation, and review-round counts are derived by counting the existing ledgers rather than tracked a second time, and token telemetry is recorded only where the runtime exposes it, because an estimated token count is a fabricated measurement.

- 🎛️ **Added**: Explicit overrides. `--thorough` runs the complete pipeline regardless of the classification, and `--fast` / `--standard` / `--critical` name a tier directly. An escalating override always applies; a de-escalating one is refused when a sensitive area forced the tier, and the refusal is printed as `override-refused=<tier>` rather than dropped silently — a force an override can silence is not a force.

- 🧪 **Tests**: 30 self-test cases in `classify-risk.sh --self-test`, wired into `composer check` so a mutation to a single regex cannot silently re-route a whole class of change, plus 14 contract tests pinning the wiring: the classifier's output shape, the sensitive-area force and the refused override, material-file narrowing (a `docs/security/` file must not force `CRITICAL` on a typo fix), the sonnet defaults, the re-classification floor, the routing ledger's line shapes and its cleanup, the `FAST` close path, the merge gate's self-produced evidence, and the removal of the duplicate review. Existing pins that asserted the replaced behaviour were updated rather than deleted.


## [0.1.2] - 2026-09-11

- 🐛 **Fixed**: The repository ships in English again. `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/gather-issue-context.sh` rendered half its Markdown brief in Czech — `Stav`, `Typ`, `Priorita`, `Aktualizováno`, `Komponenty`, `### Popis`, `### Subtasky`, `### Odkazy na issues`, `### Pull requesty`, `# Kontext JIRA`, `## Pokyny pro agenta`, `(bez názvu)`, `_Bez popisu._` — beside English `Assignee`, `Reporter`, `Created`, `Labels`, and `### Comments`. That is the mixed-language output `rules/reports/general.md` forbids, in a script the installer ships to consumer projects. `agents/athena.md` named the agent `Athéna` in three places, and `skills/github-issue-triage/scripts/assign-priorities.sh` carried seven Czech sample titles in its self-test; its 40 checks pass unchanged on the translated titles, because the derivation reads the conventional-commit prefix, not the prose. Three verbatim Czech quotations in this changelog and the Czech fixtures in `JiraAdfCommentTest` follow. **Czech that is itself the content stays**, as the `0.1` translation pass recorded: the JIRA status name `Rozpracováno`, the untranslatable closing keyword `"Uzavírá #42"`, the Czech user phrasings a trigger matches, the `"Účet neexistuje."` enumeration leak, the `'můj-článek'` collation error, and the Czech heading examples that demonstrate the language rule.

- 📝 **Changed**: `0.1.1` is superseded by this release on Packagist. Its Git tag was moved to carry the language fix below, but Packagist never re-reads a tag it has already indexed: it kept `0.1.1` pinned to the commit before that fix, so `composer require pekral/ai-olympus:0.1.1` still installs the Czech labels. The GitHub tag and the Packagist dist for `0.1.1` therefore differ. Use `0.1.2`.

## [0.1.1] - 2026-09-10

- 🔒 **Security**: An acceptance criterion carried by a tracker comment is now gated on author trust. `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` *Issue Context Analysis* derived requirements, acceptance criteria, edge cases and test data from every comment on the linked item, with no trust test. The Acceptance-Criteria Gate turns an unsatisfied criterion into a Critical finding that blocks the merge. On a public tracker anyone can comment, so a stranger could write a merge gate, or ask for one to be dropped. Step 2a now applies the test `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` already owned, and that rule records the path as closed. The gate filters authority, not input: every comment is still read, and the facts it reports still feed the review. An untrusted comment carries no criterion on its own, and each one it proposes is recorded in the review's assumptions.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: A failed JIRA comment fetch no longer kills the whole issue load. `acli … | jq … || printf '{"comments": []}'` appended the fallback to output `jq` had already written, so one variable held two JSON documents. The `--argjson` call below then rejected it and the load died with exit 2 — at the exact point the loader is documented to degrade to an empty list, which `rules/compound-engineering/general.md` relies on. The fallback now takes whatever the pipeline produced and then validates it, matching the pattern the GitHub loader already uses. The same shape applies to the subtask comment fetch.

- ♻️ **Changed**: The concurrency and backlog contracts move out of `agents/daedalus.md`. `rules/compound-engineering/concurrency.md` takes the scope-conditioned write-lock, its stale-reclaim probe, the worktree stance, and sequential processing of multiple sources. `rules/compound-engineering/backlog.md` takes the triage and decomposition modes, their consent levels, and what a backlog run never does. Both were normative contracts rather than orchestration steps, and both now read in the third person, because a rule addresses whoever applies it rather than one named agent. `daedalus` keeps a pointer to each plus the consequences it acts on directly, and drops from 125 392 to 112 881 characters — 75 % of the 150 000-character limit Claude Code enforces per instruction file.

- 🧪 **Tests**: Both deterministic issue loaders and the instruction-file size limit are now covered. Eight tests drive `skills/code-review-github/scripts/load-issue.sh` against a fake `gh` on an isolated PATH: URL parsing, the issue-vs-PR branch, the three exit codes, the `isDraft: false` special case, and the silent sub-issue degradation callers are told to disclose as unverified. Eight more drive the JIRA loader against a fake `acli`. No call leaves the machine. A separate guard measures every instruction file against the 150 000-character loader limit. That limit had lived only in a comment, after `rules/code-review/general.md` crossed it once and the whole code-review rule set went silently inactive.

- ⚙️ **Changed**: `hephaestus`, `argus` and `hermes` now run at `medium` reasoning effort. `daedalus` and `athena` stay at `high`. Effort was pinned to `high` roster-wide, which treated it as one dial for everybody. It is not: `daedalus` routes the run and `athena` is the roster's single reviewer, so a shallow pass there costs a round for every agent downstream, while the other three execute against a brief that has already been decided. The guard now pins the level each agent declares rather than one shared value. `max` stays banned roster-wide.

- 📝 **Documented**: `README.md` describes how agents hand work over: the shared task brief and its handoff log, the dispatch and audit-trail ledgers, blocking dispatch with no fan-out, the per-dispatch memory slice, and the untrusted-content boundary. The mechanism was implemented across three rule files and the `daedalus` definition, and a reader had to open the rules to find it.

- 📝 **Changed**: The installation examples in `README.md` and `docs/installation.md` pin `0.1.1`.

- 🔧 **Build**: Composer dependencies updated.

## [0.1] - 2026-09-09

- 📝 **Changed**: Use `composer build` for automatic PHP fixes in this repository. Preserve consumer instructions in `templates/CLAUDE.md` and keep repository maintenance guidance in the root `CLAUDE.md`. Per user request.
- 🐛 **Fixed**: Recognize `--disable-co-author-attribution` when CLI flags arrive concatenated. Per user request.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: Declare PHP `^8.3` for the Composer installer and test distribution installation on PHP 8.3, 8.4, and 8.5. Per user request.
- ⚡ **Changed**: Global Claude co-author attribution is now opt-in through `--disable-co-author-attribution`; default and automatic installation preserve global settings. Existing attribution preferences remain untouched. Per user request.
- 📝 **Changed**: Correct contributor setup and CI documentation, document version constraints and upgrades for the planned `0.1` release, and align installer settings documentation with its defaults. Per user request.

- ⚡ **Changed**: `/prepare-issue-for-merge` no longer stops on an issue that has no pull request. It used to require exactly one linked pull request and stop on none, so an unimplemented issue produced nothing at all — the one case where the user's ask (*bring this issue to a merge-ready state*) was fully actionable and the workflow declined it. `skills/verify-merge-readiness/SKILL.md` step 1 now branches on the resolved pull-request count: exactly one continues to the fingerprint decision, several stay a hard stop (guessing which one the issue means is never safe), and **none dispatches `daedalus`'s own end-to-end delivery path** — steps 4 to 6 of `agents/daedalus.md` *The end-to-end run*: the optional security-risk analysis, `hephaestus` for the implementation and its tests, then the `hephaestus` ↔ `athena` review-and-fix loop to convergence. The preparation then continues on the Draft pull request that path opens, and because the delivery loop already converged, step 2's fingerprint comparison reuses that review instead of buying a second CR round. Two hard stops guard the new branch: a closed source issue is never implemented, and a delivery path returning `Blocked` stops the preparation with that blocker rather than preparing an unconverged pull request. The skill's consent bullet states the widened authorization explicitly — the invocation authorizes the delivery path that opens the pull request, with implementation, its tests, and the Draft PR staying L1 exactly as in a normal `hephaestus` dispatch — and `agents/daedalus.md` *Prepare-only mode* notes that the mode takes the working-tree write-lock once it delivers, so it is no longer read-only in that case. The workflow still never merges. Pinned in `VerifyMergeReadinessWorkflowTest`. Per user request.

- ⚡ **Changed**: a **JIRA comment now opens with the acceptance verdict and carries nothing a developer would need** (#118). A run on a JIRA-sourced task published a 10 964 B comment to a ticket: method names, test file names, the head SHA, the diff fingerprint, the quality-gate result, three CI statuses, test and assertion counts, and per-line coverage, with a *Required information* section listing the SHA and the fingerprint as fields of their own. Rewriting it by hand into what the reader actually needs produced 3 063 B, so 72 % of it was noise for the one person who reads that ticket. **`skills/pr-summary/templates/pr-summary-jira.md` is rewritten** to a binding order — one status sentence, then `h2. Acceptance criteria` (one verdict sentence, then a bullet **only** for a criterion that is unmet, partially met, or awaiting a human's confirmation; satisfied criteria are never enumerated), then `h2. How to test`, then `h2. What changed` (three to five bullets of observable behaviour, in before → after form). The `Problem` / `Cause` / `Result` / `What I fixed` fields are gone from that template: `Cause` asked for a mechanism, and a mechanism is what invited the method names. The acceptance verdict stops being an optional `{embedded_blocks}` slot and becomes the first section, so the slot now carries the clarifying questions alone.
  **`rules/reports/general.md` gains a binding section**, *A JIRA comment is written for a non-technical reader*, listing the banned content item by item — class / method / function / variable / enum / file names, file paths and line numbers, commit SHAs, diff fingerprints, branch names, quality-gate results, CI status, test / assertion counts, coverage figures, severity labels, finding counts, rule references, code blocks, and internal layer names — with exactly two exceptions written as exceptions: a string the end user sees, quoted verbatim, and one pull-request link at the end. An item on the list is removed, never annotated. A comment fits within **3 000 characters**, shortened in `What changed` and never in `How to test`. The section states in as many words that the technical evidence does not disappear but **moves to the pull-request comment, which is where `@skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md` reads it** — without that sentence the next agent reads the rule as a loss of information and works around it in good faith.
  **`agents/hermes.md` enforces it**, because it is the roster's only publishing agent: it walks the body against the banned list before the write and removes what it finds, publishes as ADF, and then reads the comment back to confirm it carries real `heading`, `bulletList`, and `listItem` nodes. A single `paragraph` of flat text means the conversion failed, and that is a publication failure rather than a cosmetic one, so it returns `Blocked: publication unconfirmed`. The structural check needs the stored ADF, which the deterministic loader flattens to plain text, so hermes's Bash boundary grants one named raw `acli … comment list` **read** — a read, never a write.
  **Two paths that bypassed the technical / non-technical split are closed.** `skills/verify-merge-readiness/SKILL.md` published the head SHA, the diff fingerprint, and the gate result to whichever tracker the source was; on a JIRA source it now publishes the JIRA shape and leaves those four values on the pull request. `agents/athena.md` routed a standalone JIRA-sourced review's severity-sorted findings, counts, and code references straight onto the ticket; those now go to the linked pull request, while the JIRA helper publishes the non-technical summary — the tracker-matching routing from issue #691 is preserved, it just no longer carries the findings. `skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md` states the split as a constraint instead of implying it through its Scope list, and verifies it per run against what was published rather than what was intended. Two further paths follow the same shape: the linked-GitHub-issue mirror stopped being described as *guaranteed to match* the JIRA comment, because the two targets now render different structures from the same facts and copying one body onto the other would publish a shape that target's contract does not define; and `@skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md`'s own non-technical report, posted before any review runs, takes the JIRA shape on a JIRA source, with its risk areas and pre-existing-fix hints folded into the `How to test` steps they belong to (`references/tracker-follow-up.md`).
  **Scope: JIRA only.** `pr-summary-github.md` and `pr-summary-bugsnag.md` keep their `What changed` / `How to test` shape, and the GitHub-PR technical-CR English exception is untouched — the new section is gated against it explicitly, since the two destinations are disjoint and no comment is ever governed by both. `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` gains *The JIRA shape* and stops claiming every target renders the same structure; `skills/code-review/SKILL.md`, `skills/code-review-github/references/cr-wrapper-contract.md`, `skills/prepare-issue-context/SKILL.md`, and `agents/daedalus.md`'s reporting dispatch follow. Pinned in `CodeReviewContentTest`, `ReportsContentTest`, `JiraAdfCommentTest`, and `SkillsContentTest`, including an order assertion over the template's published body and a filled-in example measured against the 3 000-character cap; the four dropped field labels are asserted as absences that fail on the base branch. The secondary `upsert-comment.sh` exit-3 defect the issue reported is filed separately as #121 rather than fixed here. Per user request — *JIRA comments must read for a product manager, not a developer*.

- ⚡ **Changed**: every Czech passage in the repository is translated to English, so the package ships in one language. Translated in full: `docs/plans/*.md` (three planning documents), `agents/daedalus.md` (step 6a *Publish feedback back to the source*, seven `Blocked:` states, and the inline dispatch notes), `skills/laravel-security/references/audit-workflow.md`, `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/gather-issue-context.sh` (its rendered Markdown brief), `skills/github-issue-triage` (the label descriptions, in the script and on the repository's own labels), the `worktree-standing-authorization` entry in `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, and roughly forty verbatim assignment quotations in this changelog. `docs/marketing/launch-article.cs.md` is deleted — `launch-article.en.md` is its English twin. Every test that pinned a translated string is updated with it, and the `rules/security/backend.md` body digest is re-baselined after the four Czech glosses were dropped from the *Malicious Code & Supply-Chain Indicators* headings.
  **What deliberately stays Czech, because there the language is the content:** the example of a translated GitHub closing keyword (`"Uzavírá #42"`, which GitHub does not parse — that is the whole lesson), the JIRA status name `Rozpracováno`, the Czech user phrasings a rule matches on (`"úsporný režim"`, `"pročistit"`), the unsafe Czech locale string `"Účet neexistuje."` that demonstrates an enumeration leak, the `'můj-článek'` SQL literal that demonstrates a collation error, the bilingual-parentheses examples the reports rule exists to forbid, and the title of the externally-authored article `rules/sql/optimalize.md` cites.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the tests that pinned the prose of `README.md`. `ReadmeContentTest.php` and `SkillCatalogContentTest.php` go in full, along with the single-file README assertions in `CliRunTest`, `SkillsContentTest`, `IssueTemplateContentTest`, `ReportsContentTest`, `WritingContentTest`, `CompoundEngineeringContentTest`, `PluginMarketplaceTest`, and the README half of the `AgentsTest` roster check. They asserted that a document contained a sentence, so every edit to that sentence failed a test that described no behaviour — and the assertions were re-pointed at whichever file the prose had moved to rather than deleted, which is how a documentation test survives its own subject. **What is lost, stated rather than hidden:** nothing now catches a README that drifts from the code — a stale install command, a link to a moved page, a skill count that no longer matches `skills/`. **What survives:** the front-matter readability guard moves into `SkillsContentTest`, and the rules-overview completeness check moves with its table to `docs/rules.md`, where it still fails on a missing, duplicate, or phantom rule row. Both assert against the filesystem, not against a sentence.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the `ai-olympus resolve-next` subcommand and every class behind it. It picked the oldest unclaimed labelled issue from `gh issue list` and handed one prompt to `claude -p` / `codex exec`, which is a scheduler this package does not need to own — `cron` plus a one-line `claude -p` invocation does the same thing without a PHP class hierarchy, and the selection was never atomic, so overlapping ticks could pick the same issue anyway. Five classes go (`Cli`, `CommandResult`, `AgenticIssue`, `AgenticIssueResolver`, `AgenticOptions`) along with `InstallerFailure::issueListUnparsable()`, the `--label` / `--repo` / `--merge` / `--dry-run` / `--codex` switches, `tests/Installer/ResolveNextTest.php`, and the README *Unattended Runs* section. `bin/ai-olympus` loses the `proc_open` executor it carried solely for this command and now calls `Installer::run($argv)` directly — **the package ships no process-spawning code at all any more**, which restores the instructions-only invariant `SECURITY.md` states. `install` is the only subcommand left.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the README *Skill Catalog* and *Rules Overview* sections move into pages of their own, `docs/skills.md` and `docs/rules.md`. Together they were 150 of the README's 443 lines — two long tables a reader scrolled past to reach anything else, and neither is what someone opening the repository reads first. Each page is restructured rather than pasted across: the skill catalog gains a group index with per-group counts, and the rules overview is split by scope (always-on baseline, orchestration, PHP, Laravel, security, data & API, testing) with every rule linked to its own file instead of named in a code span. README keeps a three-line summary of each and links out. The completeness test that pinned every rule file to exactly one table row follows the table to `docs/rules.md` and still fails on a missing, duplicate, or phantom row.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the merge-preparation skill is renamed from `prepare-issue-for-merge` to `verify-merge-readiness`, so it no longer collides with the slash command of the same name. Claude Code lists a project command and a project skill in the same `/` menu, and `commands/prepare-issue-for-merge.md` and `skills/prepare-issue-for-merge/SKILL.md` therefore rendered as two identical-looking entries a reader could not tell apart. **The command keeps its name** — `/prepare-issue-for-merge <URL>` is the user-facing entry point and renaming it would break every invocation already in use. Only the skill it delegates to moves. The new name comes from the skill's own body: step 3 is *Prove merge readiness on the exact head*, the comment namespace it publishes into is `merge-readiness`, and the workflow verifies rather than merges (*It never merges the pull request*). **One user-visible consequence for Codex:** Codex exposes no user-defined slash command and reaches this workflow by mentioning the skill, so the Codex entry point changes from `$prepare-issue-for-merge` to `$verify-merge-readiness`. `README.md`, `docs/agents.md`, `docs/installation.md`, `agents/daedalus.md`, `agents/hermes.md`, `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md`, `skills/code-review/references/specialized-reviews.md`, and the `InstallerPath::resolveCommandsTargetDirectories()` doc comment follow the new skill path; `tests/Installer/PrepareIssueForMergeWorkflowTest.php` is renamed to `VerifyMergeReadinessWorkflowTest.php` and pins both halves — the command still names `@skills/verify-merge-readiness/SKILL.md` and `$verify-merge-readiness`, and the skill frontmatter carries the new `name:`.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the GitHub and JIRA CR comment publishers update the comment they already own instead of adding another one on every run. `skills/code-review-github/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` looks up the newest comment carrying `<!-- <namespace>:actor=<gh-login> -->`, PATCHes it, and POSTs only when no match exists; a failed lookup warns on stderr and falls back to a POST, so a transient API error costs a duplicate rather than a lost review. `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` gains the JIRA half: JIRA carries no hidden-comment syntax, so the helper appends the visible italic marker `_cr-comment:actor=<actor-digest>_`, resolves the account e-mail from `acli jira auth status`, finds the newest marker-carrying comment through `acli jira workitem comment list --json --paginate`, and applies the ADF to it with `comment update --body-adf`. The lookup filters on the comment's **own author** as well as on the marker, because the JIRA marker is visible text anyone can copy into their own comment and matching on it alone would overwrite a stranger's. The marker carries a 16-character SHA-256 digest of that e-mail rather than the address itself: the line is readable by everyone who can browse the issue, including an external customer on a Service Management project, and Jira Cloud hides `author.emailAddress` from its own API responses for exactly that reason. `acli` exposes no account ID for the current user, so the digest is what identifies the actor. That same hiding is why the author half of the lookup often has nothing to match on — the run then creates a second comment and names the degradation on stderr, rather than letting it read as a first run. An unresolvable e-mail, an `acli` error, or an unexpected JSON shape falls back to creating a comment, and a failed update deletes only a comment this run created, never one an earlier run published. Both helpers now report `action=created` or `action=updated` on stderr. This reverses the append-only behaviour a previous explicit request introduced (the entry below, *make the GitHub CR report always a new comment in the issue*), on a newer explicit request from the same owner; the older entry stays as history. **What is lost, stated rather than hidden:** the comment chain was the cross-run history, and update-in-place overwrites it, so only the current round's verdict is visible on the tracker. **What survives:** the `Reviewed revision:` and `Reviewed diff fingerprint:` header lines inside that one comment are what the incremental review scope gate and `merge-github-pr` actually read, and `process-code-review` passes the previous round's finding dispositions through its own loop state rather than off the thread. `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` *Cross-run history*, `skills/code-review/templates/review-output.md`, `skills/code-review-github/references/cr-wrapper-contract.md`, `skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md`, `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md`, `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md`, `skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md`, and `rules/jira/general.md` all describe the new behaviour. Bugsnag is unchanged: its Data Access API comment surface still posts a new comment per run.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: a JIRA comment could still reach the ticket as literal Wiki Markup — `h2.`, `{{code}}`, `* item`, `[label|url]` rendered as text instead of a formatted ADF document. Two causes, both closed. `agents/hermes.md` *Merge-preparation consolidation mode* and `skills/prepare-issue-for-merge/SKILL.md` hardcoded the **GitHub** publish helper and the GitHub `pr-summary` template for the source-issue TL;DR, so a JIRA-sourced run had no sanctioned path and improvised a raw `acli` write; both now select the helper and the template by the source tracker, and state that the deletion pass stays GitHub-only, so a JIRA source publishes the TL;DR and deletes nothing. `rules/jira/general.md` adds the two clauses that make the improvisation unavailable: a helper that exits 2 or 3 is never a licence to retry with a raw `acli` write (the JIRA MCP server with an **ADF** payload is the only fallback), and a workflow step naming the GitHub helper is re-routed on a JIRA source rather than followed literally. `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` is hardened against the failure mode that invited the improvisation: the created-comment ID is read from whichever envelope shape `acli --json` returns and validated as digits, and a failed `--body-adf` update now deletes the comment it created — so no unformatted comment is left behind — before failing with an explicit "do not fall back to a raw acli write". `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/wiki-markup-to-adf.php` additionally re-parses the span inside `*strong*`, `_em_`, and a link label, so nested `{{code}}` reaches ADF as its own mark instead of leaking into the rendered comment as literal markup. Covered by a new behavioural test that runs the converter and pins both routing halves.

- ⚡ **Changed**: a code review now decides *whether* another round runs at all, and publishes one comment instead of two. `rules/code-review/general.md` gains two canonical sections. *When another review round runs at all* narrows the re-review trigger from "the effective diff fingerprint moved" to two causes — the commits changed **business logic**, or the **assignment** changed (an edited tracker body, or a scope-refining comment from a trusted author). Neither changed — a fixer-output commit, a comment-, docs- or CHANGELOG-only change, a content-identical rebase — carries the converged verdict forward with the carry-forward and its reason recorded; an unclear classification counts as business logic and gets the round, and a new actionable reviewer comment, an open finding from the previous round, and every security gate are untouched. *One published comment per review run — a TL;DR, not a systematic report* retires the `cr-status` namespace: a converged run posts exactly one `cr-comment` per destination carrying the header block (with a new `Quality gate:` line), `## TL;DR` of what the loop changed, `## Functional Review`, and the conditional `## Deferred to sub-issues` / `## Pre-existing fixes` sections — no `## Technical Review` heading over an empty body and no per-walk confirmation. `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` now assembles that body and publishes nothing itself, `skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md` reads every value its gate needs off that one comment, and `skills/_shared/delete-owned-github-comment.sh` protects two markers instead of three. `agents/daedalus.md` takes a converged `athena` handoff plus a green gate straight to the tracker outputs and never dispatches a confirmation round; `agents/athena.md` states the convergence verdict as its own handoff field so that decision is deterministic. Per user request.

- ⚡ **Changed**: `commands/` now ships exactly one slash command, `prepare-issue-for-merge.md`, and the Composer installer delivers it. `commands/` used to reach a project through the Claude Code plugin marketplace only, so a Composer install left `/prepare-issue-for-merge` unavailable even though every skill and agent it delegates to was already installed; the installer now syncs `commands/` into `.claude/commands` alongside the existing rules, skills, and agents payloads. Codex gets no copy, because Codex exposes no user-defined slash command at all — its `SlashCommandItem` carries only built-in and service-tier variants — so the same workflow reaches Codex as the skill the command delegates to, mentioned as `$prepare-issue-for-merge` and loaded from `.agents/skills`; a `.codex` copy would be a file nothing reads. `commands/finalize-tasks.md` and `commands/install-rules.md` are removed. Removing `install-rules` costs the plugin-marketplace channel its only way to deliver `rules/` and `CLAUDE.md`, so `README.md`, `docs/installation.md`, and both launch articles now state that Composer is the only path that installs them instead of naming a command that no longer exists, and the `/finalize-tasks` row leaves the consent inventory in `rules/compound-engineering/orchestration.md`. Per user request.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: JIRA comments no longer send Wiki Markup directly through `acli comment create --body-file`, which stored headings such as `h2.` and emphasis markers as flat text. The canonical JIRA publisher now converts its constrained source markup to a valid Atlassian Document Format document before the first write, passes that ADF file to comment creation, requires the exact new comment ID from the response, and applies the same document with `acli jira workitem comment update --body-adf`. A new contract test executes the full flow with a fake `acli`, verifies both writes receive the rendered ADF nodes and marks, and proves that a missing ID fails closed instead of risking an update to another comment.

- ⚡ **Changed**: JIRA delivery runs now claim the issue before implementation by moving it to the project's In Progress status and assigning it to the account currently authenticated in `acli`. The guarded transition helper performs `--assignee "@me"` after a new status write and verifies the result with `assignee = currentUser()`; on re-entry it continues only when the authenticated user already owns the issue, so another run's claim is never stolen. A failed or unverified assignment blocks implementation. `resolve-issue`, the JIRA rule, the tracker phase invariant, and `daedalus` all point to the same helper-owned behavior.

- ✨ **Added**: `prepare-issue-for-merge` is one shared Claude Code and Codex workflow for bringing a linked GitHub pull request to merge-ready state without merging it. Claude Code exposes `/prepare-issue-for-merge <URL>` and Codex installs the same `$prepare-issue-for-merge` skill. The existing multi-tracker `/finalize-tasks` command remains unchanged. The workflow compares the canonical effective-diff fingerprint before every potential CR round, so content-identical history rewrites reuse trusted converged evidence while changed or missing fingerprints and new actionable feedback fail closed into review. After exact-head acceptance, build, CI, Draft, and mergeability gates pass, `hermes` publishes one source-issue TL;DR through the existing `pr-summary` template, verifies it, then removes only superseded comments owned by the authenticated actor through the new guarded `delete-owned-github-comment.sh` helper. The final TL;DR, comments by other accounts, ambiguous or unrelated comments, and the newest trusted `cr-comment` / `cr-status` merge evidence are protected. The workflow always stops before merge.

- ✨ **Added**: `skills/analyze-problem/SKILL.md` now drives every finding through four mandatory phases before it becomes work: identify one evidenced problem or opportunity, recover historical context from trusted project-owned sources, evaluate evidence, confidence, significance, and priority as separate judgments, then create or update one concrete proposal. Proposal lookup matches the source, problem, or intended outcome before writing, so an existing plan is updated instead of duplicated; tracker text remains untrusted evidence and never becomes project history or workflow authority. Pinned by an ordered content test for issue #84.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the Action pattern now carries a reuse-first gate on the authoring side, so a new Action is written only after the codebase has been searched for one that already orchestrates the same use case. `rules/laravel/architecture.md` **Actions** opens with the gate: search `app/Actions/**`, read the `__invoke()` of every Action in the target domain folder, grep for the collaborators the new flow would compose, and reuse or widen the Action that already covers the use case instead of shipping a second copy. Matching is on the flow, never on the class name; two Actions that share collaborators while orchestrating different use cases stay two Actions, and a step an existing Model Service / Data Builder / Data Validator / Repository / ModelManager already performs is called, never re-implemented. Code review walks it as the highest-value case of the existing reuse-first gate in `rules/code-review/core-analysis.md` *Reuse of existing logic*, which now names the per-Action walk and its Suggested Fix, and gates it against *Action scope* and the two pass-through bullets so one Action never earns two findings. `skills/refactor-entry-point-to-action/SKILL.md` makes the search blocking: reading the existing Actions is a mandatory Map step, the reuse-or-create decision is its own Execution step that records which Actions were read and why none fits, and the run is done only once that record exists.

- ⚡ **Changed**: code-review freshness now follows the content of the effective PR diff instead of commit identity. Every CR comment records a `Reviewed diff fingerprint` computed from a whitespace-significant binary/full-index patch, and merge/review workflows recompute it against the current default-branch diff. A rebase, squash, amend, or force-push that changes SHAs but leaves that fingerprint identical preserves the converged verdict and does not start a redundant CR round; a missing or different fingerprint fails closed and requires review. The head SHA remains in the report for incremental ancestor deltas, and the exact-head build gate remains independent and still re-runs after history rewrites. The rule is wired through the canonical CR contract, GitHub wrapper, process loop, merge skill, ready-to-merge signal, and the `athena` / `daedalus` agents, with a regression test covering the complete path.

- ✨ **Added**: `agents/argus.md` now runs the project's own `interactive-testing` skill and lets it drive the QA walkthrough. A QA pass is the one thing in this roster that touches a live instance — a real host, real ports, real credentials, real data — and the rules for doing that safely are the project's, not the package's: an agent definition that ships identically into every project cannot know which host and ports the sandbox permits, how the instance is brought up and torn down, which commands and credentials exist inside it, or what data a run may write. `argus` therefore invokes the skill **before it brings anything up** and follows it through the bring-up, the per-channel driving, and the edge probing; its sandbox answers win over this file's generic defaults on *how* to reach the application. Four boundaries keep that narrow: the skill lifts none of the agent's own invariants (read-only on the codebase, `Blocked` for every criterion nobody observed, a verdict per viewport, a screenshot behind every UI `Met`, the evidence rows, local-instance-only), it never grants a capability the agent withholds (a shared / staging / production host, a production credential, a git write, a tracked-file write), it is read as configuration rather than authority because a pull request can edit it and `@rules/security/general.md` *Untrusted sources* classes a branch-proposed configuration file as untrusted, and its absence is stated rather than papered over — this package ships no `interactive-testing` skill by design, so a project without one falls back to the agent's own defaults and says so in the handoff, never skipping the pass and never installing anything. `docs/agents.md` lists it among what `argus` orchestrates.

- ✨ **Added**: an authorization lens to the code review's conditional set in `skills/code-review/references/specialized-reviews.md`. Whenever a diff touches an authorization surface — `routes/**`, a controller, a single-action `__invoke()`, a Livewire component, a policy, a `Gate::` / `authorize(` / `can(` call, a `->middleware(` declaration, a route-model-bound signature, a FormRequest `authorize()`, an API Resource, or an Eloquent read that reaches a caller inside one of those files — the review runs `skills/laravel-authorization-review/SKILL.md` with the new `MODE=cr` and reports **broken object-level authorization (IDOR / BOLA)**: a record resolved by route-model binding or by an actor-supplied id and never scoped to the actor who asked for it. Until now the skill existed and `rules/code-review/general.md` even built a full Exclusion-Gate exemption around its findings, but only `agents/athena.md` ever invoked it, so a code review run any other way left the OWASP A01 surface unwalked. The lens is scoped to the routes the diff touched (never the whole route table), renders no per-route coverage map on a CR, and when `php artisan route:list --json` will not boot it runs on the skill's static fallback, states that assumption on the summary line, and drops every finding one confidence level rather than reporting nothing. Its findings fold into the standard Critical / Moderate / Minor buckets and are never excludable, per clause (S1) of the Exclusion Gate. Gating keeps one finding per violation: this lens owns object-level authorization, `security-review` keeps every other category it owns, and `athena` no longer runs the skill herself — she reads its findings off the review instead of walking the same routes twice.

- ✨ **Added**: a tracker-side counterpart to *Two-Part CR Output* in `rules/code-review/general.md`. The consolidated tracker comment a code review publishes now always states whether the assignment was met, in one of exactly three verdicts: every acceptance criterion is met (one explicit positive sentence), a named criterion is not met and what concretely is missing (the gap list the comment already carried), or the assignment states no explicit criteria plus the basis the implementation was judged on. The affirmative case is the one that used to vanish: `skills/assignment-compliance-check/SKILL.md` returned a skip status when every requirement was satisfied, so the wrapper embedded nothing, and `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` rendered no verdict of its own — which left a tracker comment where *"every criterion is met"* and *"nobody checked"* looked identical to the person who owns the ticket and decides whether the change ships. The verdict already rendered unconditionally on the pull request through `## Functional Review`; this carries the same result onto the surface the ticket owner actually reads. `assignment-compliance-check` now returns its block on every run that has a linked tracker and keeps exactly one skip status, `no linked issue — assignment compliance skipped`, which is a property of a missing destination rather than of a clean result. The block stays non-technical, stays off the GitHub PR comment, and still travels only through the single consolidated `pr-summary` comment per destination; the three `pr-summary` templates state that a clean CR run passes the block too, so an omitted slot now means a non-CR invocation and never a clean assignment.

- ✨ **Added**: first-class Codex support alongside Claude Code. The installer now writes repository skills to Codex's native `.agents/skills`, installs project instructions in `AGENTS.md`, mirrors the rule library to `.codex/rules`, and registers all five roles as project-scoped `.codex/agents/*.toml` adapters. The adapters load the same canonical `agents/*.md` role definitions from `.codex/agent-instructions`, translating Claude path and collaboration-tool conventions at runtime so the two harnesses do not maintain divergent workflow prompts. `--global` and `--prune-global` now cover both `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.agents/skills`. Unattended issue resolution supports `--codex`, which uses the documented `codex exec` command and Codex `$skill-name` invocations; Claude Code remains the default when the switch is absent. Claude-specific permission switches continue to modify only Claude settings.

- ✨ **Added**: a preferred invocation for a job's own test to *Jobs* in `rules/code-testing/general.md`. The test constructs the job with the payload it controls and runs it through `app()->call([$job, 'handle'])`, so the container resolves the `handle()` dependencies and the test builds no double just to satisfy the signature. This is also the production call path — the queue worker reaches `handle()` through `Illuminate\Bus\Dispatcher::dispatchNow()`, which calls `$container->call([$job, 'handle'])` — so the test runs the same wiring the worker runs. The rule bans the alternative it replaces: `$job->handle($mockRepository, $mockService)` forces a double per dependency, asserts the job against those doubles instead of against real behavior, and breaks the moment `handle()` gains, loses, or re-types a parameter. A collaborator that genuinely cannot run in the test is swapped at its container binding (`$this->app->instance(...)`, `Http::fake()`, `Process::fake()`, `Storage::fake()`), never at the call site, so the invocation reads the same either way; `dispatch_sync($job)` stays for the narrow case where the test is about the bus pipeline itself. The existing *assert the dispatch, never the payload* bullet is unchanged — it owns the caller's test, this one owns the job's. `rules/laravel/laravel.md` *Testing* carries the one-line Laravel pointer next to its `Artisan::call()` sibling, and `skills/create-test/SKILL.md` restates it where it already restates test placement, naming, and arrange-act-assert.

- ✨ **Added**: an **accept the HTTP request** ban to *Action Rules* in `rules/laravel/architecture.md`. An Action's `__invoke()` declares no `Illuminate\Http\Request` and no `FormRequest` parameter, and never reaches for the request from inside its body (`request()`, the `Request` facade, `app('request')`). This is the input-side mirror of the existing *Actions return plain domain data* ban and carries its **Critical** severity for the same reason: the controller owns client↔server communication in both directions, so the request stops where the response starts. A request parameter costs three things at once — it binds the use case to HTTP, so the same Action cannot run from a job, a command, a listener, or a test without faking a request; it hides the input contract behind a parameter named *a request*; and it reopens the trust boundary, because an Action holding the request reads `input()` / `all()` / `query()` past the payload the FormRequest just validated. The controller converts the request first — `$request->toDto()` on the endpoint's FormRequest, or `$request->validated()` mapped by a Data Builder — and passes that DTO.
