The report also names which target directory (never the specific orphaned files) carries the count when more than one target has orphans, and the source tree is now listed once per payload — reused by the copier, the pruner, and the orphan lookup alike — instead of re-walked once per target per concern (a PR #150 run-2 CR fix threaded the shared listing into `InstallerFileCopier::installDirectory()` too and deleted its now-redundant private listing method, closing the gap where only the pruner side had actually been deduplicated). The report's own wording was corrected in the same round: the orphan count sums across every target of a payload, so "N file(s) in target" (singular) was replaced with "across the target directories" to stop implying a single directory. A PR #150 code-review round additionally found `InstallerPruner`'s target-side walk still followed directory symlinks (a pre-existing flag, newly reachable on every default install once this report runs unconditionally) — a symlink placed under a target could make the count include, and `--prune` delete, files outside the target tree; the target walk no longer follows symlinks (empirically verified the escape is closed, and that `unlink()` on the resulting leaf symlink only ever removes the link itself, never its target). `Installer::reportInstallSummary()`'s parameter count crossed `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc`'s 4-parameter DTO threshold once the new orphan counter was added; it and the internal sync-count shape threaded through `runAllSyncs()`/`syncDirectories()` are now `InstallSummary` / `SyncCounts` value objects instead of positional parameters / array shapes. Note: the issue's part 1 point 1 (manually deleting the orphaned skills already installed in `~/.claude/skills`) is **deliberately not done** in this PR — an explicit owner decision, not an oversight — so the issue's own success criterion "`comm -13` between `skills/` and `~/.claude/skills/` returns empty" is **not met** by this change; that directory is a shared, mutable, per-machine path outside this repository's control, and this PR intentionally ships detection/reporting only, never an automatic delete against it. Verified (not taken from the issue's own snapshot, which is stale) immediately before this PR: exactly **2** orphaned entries remain in `~/.claude/skills` — `create-tracker-issue-from-cr-comments` and `humanizer` — precisely the two the issue itself flagged as "no owning commit found, needs a human decision"; every other formerly-orphaned skill the issue listed already has a matching source-tree entry. **(2) Per-role read filter fixed** — `@rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` *Per-role read filter* previously recommended `grep -A5 "^### " | grep -E "Role:.*(<role>|shared)"`, a fixed 5-line-window grep that silently missed almost every entry whose `Role:` field sits past line 5 of its block (most of them, once an entry carries any inserted `**Recurrence (#N)**`-style paragraph) — verified empirically: for `talos|shared` the old idiom returned 2 matching entries out of 33 that actually qualify. Replaced with a whole-block `awk` extraction that scans every line of each entry for a matching `Role:`, regardless of depth. The 5 entries that had never been tagged with a `Role:` at all (`agent-file-vs-registration`, `parallel-agent-publication-contract` → `daedalus`; `agent-new-mode-status-result-parity`, `cr-rule-severity-collision`, `agent-rename-sync-points` → `talos`, matching this project's own established role-tagging convention for near-identical existing entries) are now tagged; every one of the file's 49 entries carries a `Role:`. Pinned by a new fixture-based regression test (`tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`) proving the new filter finds a `Role:` at both a shallow and a deep offset, where the old fixed-window idiom would have missed the deep one. **(3) `MODE: bulk` sanctioned for `compact-project-memory`** — the skill's diff-scoped default (issue #98) is unchanged, but it now accepts an explicit, caller-supplied `MODE: bulk` input that treats every existing entry as the primary set for a one-shot pass (skipping only the "expand to related" step, which becomes not applicable — reported as `N/A`); every compaction rule, the deterministic loss-check, and all seven invariants apply unchanged. This is a **deliberate, scoped extension of the non-goal recorded in commit `21043c4` (issue #98 / PR #146)** for this one opt-in case — the default, unsolicited behavior is not a regression; `MODE: bulk` never triggers on its own. **(4) Bulk compaction applied once** — running the newly-sanctioned `MODE: bulk` over all 49 entries shrank `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md` from 94,609 bytes to **61,455 bytes** (a 35% reduction; the file no longer actively grows a write path removed in issue #77, so this is a one-time catch-up compaction, not a recurring maintenance pass). **Correction from an earlier draft of this change:** the loss-check run at compaction time compared only `#N` / commit-SHA / `### slug` references — three of the five token classes `compact-project-memory`'s own invariants require — so it was structurally blind to a dropped concrete pointer or a deleted counter-example, the exact class of loss it exists to catch; a PR #150 code-review round (`argos` + `athena`) diffed the pre- and post-compaction file directly and found **14 concrete-pointer tokens dropped across 13 entries** (8 with no trace left at all — e.g. `.cursor/rules/project.mdc`, `composer.json`, `getOldPascalConfig()`, `toContain()` — plus 3 counter-example items in one entry), invisible to the narrower check because the entry count (49 → 49) and the slug set stayed byte-identical. `compact-project-memory/SKILL.md`'s step-5 loss-check now explicitly names concrete-pointer and counter-example token classes (not just slug/SHA/issue refs) so a future compaction cannot silently repeat this.

  **Second correction (PR #150, run 2):** the CHANGELOG sentence in that first correction — *"Every dropped token was restored"* — was itself **not true**: the restoration was applied but never re-verified against the strengthened check it had just introduced. A second, independent CR round re-ran the per-entry loss-check for real, before = `origin/master`, after = the working copy, walking every one of the 49 entries: **36 genuine tokens across 20 entries were still missing**, including six real issue/PR references (`#16`/`#33`, `#71`/`#72`, `#69`/`#57`) and several concrete pointers with no trace left anywhere in their entry (e.g. `README.md` and `agents/hermes.md` dropped from `agent-rename-sync-points`; `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php`, `skills/code-review/templates/`, and the `agents/` prefix on three renamed-skill delegation targets dropped from `skills-tree-convention-removal-grep-full-tree`; `.claude/run/.daedalus-write.lock` dropped from `write-lock-staleness-needs-corroborating-evidence-not-bare-pid`). All 36 are now restored, pinned by a new regression test in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php` (`PROJECT_MEMORY.md restored the concrete pointers a first compaction pass dropped`) that checks each restored token in its own specific context, not as a bare token a coincidentally-numbered unrelated entry could already satisfy. Four tokens are **deliberately excluded** from the restoration as generic placeholders rather than pointers to a specific artifact — `agents/<name>.md`, `@skills/<removed>/SKILL.md`, `rules/**`, `.claude/rules/...` — plus the benign ordinal "mandate `#2`" (not an issue/PR reference); this exclusion is a conscious decision, not an oversight. Issue #148's own loss-check success criterion is therefore met **now**, on this second, independently re-verified pass — not on the first, unverified claim.

  **Issue #148's own size criteria remain NOT met** — restoring the 36 tokens grew the file further, to **62,486 B** against a ≤ 50,000 B target, and the largest entry is now 1,699 B against a ≤ 1,200 B target — the same deliberate trade-off as before, decided in favor of the invariant (no concrete pointer is ever dropped) over the byte target, now simply measured at its corrected, fully-verified size. The per-entry budget test (guarding the result, plus a companion test guarding that every entry still carries a `Role:` from the allowed dictionary) stays pinned at **1,700 B**, not ~1,200 B, for the same reason, justified inline in the test; both are proven by a red→green transition (the budget test failed against the pre-compaction file, confirmed passing after the compaction, and re-confirmed after both rounds of token restoration). **(5) `CLAUDE.md` now points to the memory file** — a one-line pointer (`rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc`'s own long-standing requirement, previously unmet) makes `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md` discoverable from the file every project already reads first. **(6) `daedalus` sweeps stale run artifacts at startup, fail-safe by construction** — before writing its own shared brief (step 2), `daedalus` now deletes a stale `.claude/run/*.md` brief or `.claude/worktrees/agent-*` entry only on **positive proof of death**: a `## PID` / `pid <N>` token found in the brief's header region (never inside a fenced tracker-payload quote, since the brief carries attacker-influenced tracker text) or a worktree's `locked` reason, matching `^[0-9]{1,7}$`, whose `kill -0` probe fails with "no such process" — never "not permitted", which means the process is alive under another user/namespace. Anything ambiguous — a missing PID, a malformed token, a live probe, or an EPERM probe — is preserved, never removed; an unlocked worktree entry is likewise left in place, since every CR worktree `argos`/`athena` create is now required to lock itself (`git worktree lock --reason "pid $PPID slug <slug>"`, under `.claude/worktrees/agent-cr-<slug>`) at creation. The one PID source used everywhere is the run's own stable `$PPID`, never `$$` (the ephemeral per-Bash-call subshell PID, empirically confirmed dead moments after any single tool call returns) — the same value the working-tree write-lock already records. The sweep itself now runs under a dedicated, short-lived `.claude/run/.daedalus-sweep.lock` so two runs starting at once can never interleave it, and the brief is written atomically (temp file + `mv`, `## PID` first) so no peer sweep can ever observe it mid-write. **Correction from an earlier draft of this change:** the first version of this sweep inverted the safety direction — a *missing* `## PID` was treated as safe to delete, `$$` and `echo $PPID` were offered as interchangeable, and no worktree-locking convention existed for the sweep to check against — a PR #150 code-review round caught all of this as fail-**unsafe** (a live peer's brief, and a live CR worktree, were both reachable for deletion) plus a shell-injection risk from an unvalidated `pid` token parsed from attacker-influenced content; this description reflects the corrected, fail-safe design, not the original one. This runs regardless of whether a prior run crashed or stopped `Blocked` before reaching its own step-7 cleanup, so orphaned scratch memory no longer silently accumulates. Pinned by algorithmic tests (brief and worktree halves, each with dead-PID / live-PID / no-PID / malformed-PID / fenced-block / EPERM fixtures) and a content-assertion test on the documented procedure.

  **Second correction (PR #150, run 2):** the fail-safe fix above still let a live brief resolve to `confirmed dead` two different ways, both reproduced without an attacker: `## PID` had no pinned single-line format, so a run that happened to write the ISO timestamp before the PID (a valid order under the old wording) had its **year** captured as the PID by the shipped `\s+`/`\b` regex, and a "header region" defined as "before the first fenced block" was not actually a trust boundary — one of the three real briefs on disk carries no fenced block at all, making its whole file the "header region". `## PID` is now pinned to a single, fixed-order line (`## PID <digits> <ISO-8601>`), read **only by its fixed file position** (the second line, immediately below the title) rather than by scanning for a fence, and parsed with a full anchor (`^## PID[ \t]+([0-9]{1,7})[ \t]`) that fails closed — never falls through to a look-alike line elsewhere in the file — on any other order or shape. The sweep's own mutual-exclusion lock had two further defects, also reproduced directly: on a project with no `.claude/run/` yet, `mkdir` on the lock's missing parent failed with ENOENT forever (not EEXIST), hard-hanging `daedalus` step 2 on every first run in a fresh checkout (now fixed with an explicit `mkdir -p` before the loop); and the lock carried no holder token at all, so its 50-attempt "steal" was unconditional and repeating rather than gated on the holder being confirmed dead — now a genuine holder file (`PID`/`SLUG`/`STARTED`, mirroring the write-lock) gates a one-shot steal, and the lock itself is keyed on the repository-wide common git dir (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`) rather than the current toplevel, since the worktree half of the sweep it guards is itself repository-global. Separately, the write-lock's stale-reclaim probe recorded an ISO-8601 timestamp but never read it back — a bare alive PID was trusted as proof of a live holder with no identity check at all, a weaker evidence standard than this project's own `write-lock-staleness-needs-corroborating-evidence-not-bare-pid` memory entry requires for that exact reclaim path; the reclaim now additionally computes the holder PID's own start time from its elapsed run time (`ps -o etime=`, portable across macOS/BSD and Linux) and requires it to fall at or before the recorded `STARTED`, treating a later start (proof the PID was recycled by an unrelated process) exactly like a confirmed-dead probe. `LC_ALL=C` is now prescribed on every `kill -0` probe in this file, closing a locale-fragility gap where a translated `strerror()` message could silently drop the `"not permitted"` substring the ESRCH/EPERM distinction depends on (and the PHP test mirror's own predicate is now aligned to match the identical phrase). One known, declared residual limitation remains: a genuine mid-run host-process reconnect (the recorded PID exits and the run resumes under a new one) still reads as confirmed-dead to the brief sweep, and no local, network-free PID check can tell that apart from real abandonment — mirroring the already-accepted DNS-rebinding gap in `attachment-download-urls-need-an-ssrf-host-guard`, this is noted explicitly rather than silently left open. Pinned by new algorithmic tests (the timestamp-first and own-field-malformed-with-a-look-alike-line-deeper-in-the-file fixtures the mis-parse needed; a confirmed-dead-gated steal-decision mirror; an identity-corroboration mirror) and new content-assertion tests.

  **CR worktree locking (`agents/argos.md` / `agents/athena.md`) hardened in the same round:** the path and lock reason both `argos` and `athena` write when isolating their review in a worktree were previously identical for both peers (derived from the same shared-brief slug with no per-agent discriminator), so the second of the two concurrent CR passes could not even create its worktree, and the standalone cleanup path (`git worktree unlock` + `git worktree remove`, unqualified) let whichever peer finished first tear down the *shared* path out from under the other — reproduced directly in an isolated scratch repository. Both agents now suffix their path and lock reason with their own name (`.claude/worktrees/agent-cr-<slug>-argos` / `-athena`, `git worktree lock --reason "pid $PPID agent <argos|athena> slug <slug>"`), and their standalone cleanup instructions now state explicitly that only the agent's own path is ever removed. `<slug>` — interpolated into both a shell command and a filesystem path with no format rule at all — is now required to match `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$` before either happens; a non-matching slug (verified in an isolated scratch repository to otherwise permit command substitution via `$(...)` in the lock reason, or path escape via `../` in the worktree path) means no worktree is created and the review continues in the shared tree instead. `<ref>` (the PR head branch) is replaced with `--detach <head-sha>`, since that branch is normally already checked out in the main tree during a PR review and `git worktree add <path> <branch>` fails on an already-checked-out branch (a pre-existing defect the new pinning test would otherwise have frozen in place). Pinned by extended content-assertion tests per agent.

  **Third fix (PR #150, run 3):** the second corrective round itself introduced two new regressions in the same ~18-line sweep-lock block (`agents/daedalus.md:95-111`), found independently by both `argos` and `athena`. The lock-theft gate (`! LC_ALL=C kill -0 "$pid" 2>&1 | grep -q 'not permitted'`) tested the exit code of **grep**, not of `kill -0` — a live concurrent run under the same UID prints nothing to stderr, so `grep` fails, `!` inverts that into "confirmed dead", and the lock is stolen from a live holder (reproduced directly against PID 11073, i.e. against a `daedalus` that was running at the time). The fix captures `kill -0`'s own exit code first (`rc=$?`) and only then inspects the captured message. The second regression: the "skip this sweep" branch (a `break` after finding a live or EPERM holder) still ran the unconditional write of the holder file, so the intruder overwrote a live neighbour's `PID` / `SLUG` / `STARTED` — the holder is now written only on the branch that actually acquired the lock (`skip=1` set immediately before the `break`, the write guarded by `[ -z "$skip" ]`). The holder PID read from the sweep-lock file also gained the same format validation (`case "$holder_pid" in ''|*[!0-9]*) holder_pid='' ;; esac`) as the other two PID sources in this file. The write-lock's identity corroboration (`ps -o etime=` against `STARTED`) had no fail-safe default for the case it cannot evaluate (a missing `STARTED` on an older lock, unparseable `ps` output) — such a state is now read as a live blocker and never as a reason to reclaim, exactly as the startup sweep reads a missing `## PID`; the summary in step 5, unchanged from `master` and newly contradicting that sentence (it spoke of reclaiming "when the probe fails", while an EPERM probe is a failure that means alive), now points at the full logic instead of carrying its own simplified restatement. **Systemic finding:** the PHP test mirror `daedalusPidConfirmedDead()` encodes the correct (`posix_kill()`-first) semantics, so the tests stayed green while the shipped shell did the opposite — the mirror tested a different implementation from the one the agent actually runs. The new mirror `daedalusSweepLockKillProbeConfirmedDead(rc, message)` works on the same two primitives as the shell (`kill -0`'s own exit code, and the captured message) instead of one opaque PID, and the corrected shell text is additionally pinned verbatim in the test (`toContain` plus `not->toContain` on the old shape), so the same class of regression cannot return without changing a test. The fixed position of `## PID` (exactly the second line of the file) was a no-op in practice — 0 of the 3 real briefs on disk satisfied it, because natural Markdown puts a blank line after an H1 — so the read now scans the first 5 lines (still a positional check, never a content one) instead of a single fixed line, which stays safely above `## Gathered context`, where the attacker-influenced payload lives. The one remaining lost pointer (`skills/code-review-github/scripts/load-issue.sh`) in `daedalus-executes-final-merge-directly-no-specialist-owns-it` was restored as well, having stayed dropped even after the second recovery round. Pinned by extended algorithmic and content-assertion tests in `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php` and `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`.

  Per issue #148.

- ⚡ **Changed**: `daedalus` now **repairs** an unmet code-review merge gate instead of escalating it. The prohibition on merging without a converged review was already in place — `agents/daedalus.md` *"You never merge anything without a code review from the CR agents"*, mechanically enforced by `@skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md`'s *Converged code review on the final diff* gate (locate the CR comment, require `criticalCount + moderateCount == 0`, require its `updatedAt` to be at or after the head commit's `authoredDate`) — but the two halves met badly in one case: when the **merge step itself** reported the gate unmet, the run ended as a blocked stop, so a merge the user explicitly asked for was abandoned merely because the review was missing rather than because anything was wrong with the change. That refusal is now classified as a **missing prerequisite `daedalus` owns**: it dispatches the step-6 review-and-fix loop on the current diff (`argos` for quality, `athena` in parallel whenever the diff carries a security surface or the CR comment claims `security: owned by athena`), lets `talos` resolve every surfaced Critical / Moderate finding through `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md`, and re-enters the merge once the loop reports 0 Critical + 0 Moderate — re-running the gate against the **new** head commit, since the fixes themselves move the head and would otherwise re-stale the review that had just converged. The same treatment covers an unmet *delegated security coverage* gate (a `security: owned by athena` token with no `athena` comment behind it — exactly the state a security pass that dies mid-run leaves). Escalation is reserved for a repair loop that cannot converge (`maxIterations`, an unresolvable finding, or a blocked dispatch); an absent review is never a reason to merge without one. Pinned by a new test in `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php`.

- ✨ **Added**: a new **`compact-project-memory`** skill (#98) shrinks the token footprint of `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md` after every write — editing only the entries the write actually touched, never the whole file. The touched range is derived deterministically from `git diff HEAD -U0 -- <file>` (falling back to the immediately preceding commit, `git diff HEAD~1 HEAD`, only when the working tree is clean) — verified empirically before writing the skill that a bare `git diff` alone shows unstaged changes only and silently misses a change that was staged but not yet committed. Up to **3** demonstrably related entries (matched by overlapping `Trigger:` keywords, a shared `Example:` path, or an explicit cross-reference) are pulled in per run, and every excess candidate is listed as "not compacted this run" instead of silently dropped. Compaction shrinks `Rule:`/`Example:`/`Trigger:` prose to the per-entry budget in `@rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` *Entry format* and collapses stacked `Recurrence`/`Update` paragraphs, while a deterministic loss-check — extracting every slug, URL, issue/PR number, and `path[:line]` token from the entry before and after — guarantees the after-set is a superset of the before-set; any entry that would drop a fact has its edit reverted and reported instead of published. Every entry keeps its slug, `Role:`, and `Added:` date; a merge is allowed only when one entry is a strict subset of another, with the absorbed slug kept as an alias line. A new **`### Write protocol (compact after every write)`** section in `rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` — a sibling of the existing `### Read protocol`, cross-referenced from `### Entry format` — makes running this skill immediately after any write to the memory file an unconditional default for every agent, skill, and human-directed run; it explicitly does not reintroduce the automated lesson-*generation* mechanism removed in issue #77 — it only shrinks wording someone already wrote. `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`'s issue #77 regression test (`'compound memory write mechanism is removed'`) had asserted the **absence** of any `### Write protocol` heading as a guard against the old, fully-automated write/lesson-generation mechanism; that one assertion is narrowed to keep guarding only `record-project-memory` / `### Promotion bar` / `### Curation pass` / `### What feeds the memory`, since issue #98 is itself a fresh, deliberate owner decision for a narrower protocol (compaction only) that resurrects none of what #77 removed — every other assertion in that test is unchanged. Pinned by a new test in `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php` (the skill's frontmatter plus its full invariant list — slug never renamed, `Role:` never changed/dropped, no PR/issue/commit reference or concrete pointer ever dropped, counter-examples only ever shortened, no entry ever deleted outside a strict-subset merge, `Added:` dates preserved) and a new test in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php` (the rule section, its placement as a `### Read protocol` sibling, and the `### Entry format` cross-reference). README skill count bumped 49 → 50 (`tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`'s skill-count assertion computes the count dynamically from the `skills/` tree, so only the README text needed updating). Per issue #98.

- 📝 **Added**: the six GitHub community health files this repository was missing — `CONTRIBUTING.md` (the real quality gate: `composer build` = install + `@fix` + `@check`, with an explicit note that `.github/workflows/pr.yml` runs only a subset of `@check` and a green CI is not proof the full local build is clean), `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` (Contributor Covenant 2.1), `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml` and `feature_request.yml`, `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml` (Discussions is not enabled on this repo, so it points to the GitHub Security Advisory flow instead of a dead Discussions link), and `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`. Per issue #108.

- ♻️ **Changed**: the **Real-Code Grounding for Every Finding (issue #97)** requirement now lives once, canonically, in `rules/code-review/general.mdc` instead of as four near-identical copies across `skills/code-review`, `skills/security-review`, `skills/api-review`, and `skills/assignment-compliance-check`. The original change (PR #140) had to duplicate the contract because it was scoped to `skills/` only; the copies had already drifted apart in wording on merge day — only `security-review` carried the "inconclusive re-read keeps the finding", "a drop rests on the reviewer's own re-read, never on an author's claim", and "record the dropped claim plus the refuting `file:line`" clauses, so a finding published by `api-review` or `assignment-compliance-check` was held to a demonstrably weaker gate than the same finding published by `security-review`. The canonical section states the whole contract — re-read the cited `file:line` plus surrounding context before publishing, drop only on contradiction (citing the mitigating `file:line`), keep when inconclusive, never let an author's / assignment's "already safe" or "test-only" claim substitute for the reviewer's own re-read, record every drop, and carry the requirement into standalone runs — and each of the four skills now keeps only where the gate sits in its own pipeline plus the context its domain requires (`code-review`: first, ahead of and distinct from Critical Findings Verification (issue #537); `security-review`: every reachable path to the sink, the risk-based severity downgrade on an inconclusive re-read, `athena`'s `SECURITY_OWNER=athena` mode; `api-review`: the route / controller / FormRequest / API Resource and the real response shape; `assignment-compliance-check`: before the Partially satisfied / Missing / Divergent classification, with no verification layer behind it). Pinned by two new tests in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php` — one asserting the contract's clauses in the rule, one asserting every one of the four skills defers to it and restates none of it. Note: this **did not** bring `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` back under the `skill-check` `body.max_tokens` limit (5169 → 5123 against a 5000 limit, still a non-blocking `WARN`); the remaining excess sits in `## Output Rules` and `### Specialized Reviews`, which are unrelated to issue #97 and were deliberately left untouched. Follow-up to issue #97.

- ✨ **Added**: Laravel rules now mandate the native `Illuminate\Support\Facades\Image` facade (Laravel 13.20, built on Intervention Image v4) for image processing — resizing, cropping, format conversion, quality control, and simple effects (#118). A new `## Image Processing` section in `rules/laravel/laravel.mdc` sits ahead of the existing **New Feature Implementation** package waterfall (Spatie → community package → custom) for images: `intervention/image` used directly, another image package, or raw GD/Imagick calls are justified only when a transformation the native facade does not expose is genuinely required. The section documents the facade's image-source entry points (`$request->image()`, `Image::fromPath()` / `fromStorage()` / `fromUrl()` / `fromBytes()` / `fromBase64()`), which of the five resizing methods (`cover()` / `contain()` / `scale()` / `resize()` / `crop()`) to reach for, the immutable/lazy transformation pipeline (each call returns a new `Image` instance; nothing runs until an output call), and the queued-job gotcha (an `Image` instance cannot be serialized and must be stored first, consistent with the existing **Queue and Jobs** rule). Verified against the article the issue linked (https://laravel-news.com/a-practical-guide-to-laravels-first-party-image-processing) rather than guessed. Pinned by a new test in `tests/Installer/LaravelRulesContentTest.php`. Per issue #118.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the loop-gate checks in `@skills/resolve-issue/references/quality-gates.md` *Loop gate vs. final gate (issue #65)* now **reuse a green CI result instead of re-running a check locally** (#124), mirroring the "Reuse CI results when available" pattern already established for the coverage gate (`@rules/code-review/general.mdc` *Validation & Coverage Gate*). A CR pass on the first version of this change (`argos` + `athena`, consolidated) found the reuse mechanism itself exploitable and tightened it before merge: the verdict is now read exclusively from structured GitHub Actions data (`gh run view --json jobs`, matched per step by name), never from `statusCheckRollup[]`'s merged `context`/`state` — that projection cannot distinguish a real Actions check-run from a forged legacy `repo:status` commit status — and never by parsing the human-readable run log. Reuse is refused outright whenever the current commit's `.github/workflows/**` or `composer.json` `scripts` differ from the base branch: a `pull_request`-triggered workflow runs the definition committed on the PR's own ref, so a softened step (e.g. `continue-on-error: true` added to it) reports green under an unchanged name, and GitHub reports that suppressed failure's `conclusion` as `"success"` — identical to a real pass — so this diff check is the only defense against that vector. `security-audit` (`composer audit`) is excluded from reuse entirely and always runs locally: its inputs (`composer.lock`, `vendor/`) are untracked in this repository, so an exact-SHA match does not pin what it reads, and independently its verdict is time-variant (a live advisory-database query), so a past-green run says nothing about today. The staleness guard requires a clean tree and the CI run's actually-checked-out SHA — not the workflow's nominal trigger SHA, since a `pull_request` event may check out a merge ref — to exactly match the current commit, states plainly that a dirty tree disables reuse for every check with no per-check carve-out (the loop's own "apply a fix, then gate" order means this reuse fires only on an iteration that changed nothing since the cited CI run), and requires `test:coverage` reuse to apply the same coverage threshold as the local gate. A non-green conclusion (`skipped`, `neutral`, `cancelled`, `timed_out`, `stale`, `action_required`, or missing) is never treated as a pass. A check CI never runs at all is never reused either: in this repository `skill-check`, `composer-normalize-check`, and `shell-self-tests` are part of `composer.json`'s `@check` but absent from `.github/workflows/pr.yml`'s steps — named here as this repository's own example, not a portable list. The reuse is scoped strictly to the **loop** gate — the **final gate** (the full `composer build` before push/PR) always runs in full regardless of CI status, and `@skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md`'s own *Pre-push quality gates* section now states explicitly that its own gate **is** the final gate the reuse mechanism never applies to, even though both sections share that title. `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md`'s *Pre-push quality gates* section now cites the new subsection and restates the loop-only scope, the clean-tree condition, the non-green catch-all, and the `security-audit` exclusion inline. Pinned by an updated test in `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`. Per issue #124.

- ✨ **Added**: a new **`pr-deploy-planner`** skill (#131) — a read-only advisory that analyzes the current branch's commits and proposes (1) a dependency-safe logical grouping with a suggested deployment order, so a large PR can ship in smaller, safer increments instead of one big deploy, (2) for each group, whether its commits should be squashed into one commit for the cleanest readable history (with a suggested `type(scope): description` message when so, grounded in the linked issue's own requirements), and (3) rename suggestions for commit messages that are too generic or inaccurate to explain the change (`wip`, `fix`, `updates`). It never rewrites git history itself — no `git rebase`, squash, amend, or force-push — a human decides whether to act on the proposal (e.g. via `@skills/git-workflow/SKILL.md`'s rebase mechanics). PR discovery resolves the current branch name first so a detached HEAD never binds an unrelated PR, and, once found, resolves the base against the remote (`git merge-base origin/<baseRefName> HEAD`) instead of a possibly stale local ref; every field loaded from the PR (`title`/`body`/`comments`/`reviews`/author names, not only diff lines) is treated as untrusted data, and quoted commit/PR text in the published report is escaped, length-capped, and stripped of bidi/zero-width/C0 control characters before rendering. `@rules/reports/general.mdc` now names this skill's report as a second sanctioned English-language exception (alongside the technical-CR-comment exception), since its payload is itself a set of proposed commit messages. Standalone and callable on demand; it is **not** wired into the existing `daedalus` / `talos` / `argos` orchestration pipeline. README skill count bumped 48 → 49; `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`'s skill-count assertion is computed dynamically from the `skills/` tree, so only the README text needed updating. Per issue #131.

- 📝 **Changed**: the CR **Database Analysis** section's `Suggested Fix` now renders a concrete SQL / query-builder rewrite, index DDL, or batch-operation snippet — not just a fix-category label — for every finding, grounded in `@rules/sql/optimalize.mdc`'s existing rewrite pattern library. The canonical rule (`rules/code-review/general.mdc`), the `code-review-github` / `code-review-jira` Output Rules, and the shared template (`skills/code-review/templates/review-output.md`) all carry the tightened contract. While tracing every occurrence of the section, the three per-wrapper CR templates (`skills/code-review-github/templates/pr-comment-output.md`, `skills/code-review-jira/templates/github-output.md`, `skills/code-review-bugsnag/templates/github-output.md`) turned out to omit the `## Database Analysis` heading entirely even though their own `SKILL.md` prose already mandated it — the same class of drift fixed for `code-review-bugsnag`'s Summary line in issue #60 — so all three gained the section, positioned before `## Architecture` exactly like the shared template. Per issue #132.

- 📝 **Changed**: Laravel architecture rules replace the subjective "primarily serve a single model" test for `Service`-suffixed classes with a mechanical one — any `Service`-suffixed class must `extend BaseModelService` unconditionally (when `pekral/arch-app-services` is installed); a structural public-method count (excluding the constructor and framework/vendor-interface-required overrides) decides only the *remediation*: more than one public business method on a single model → make it a proper `BaseModelService` subclass, exactly one → convert wholesale into an Action. `@rules/code-review/general.mdc`'s **Only-Laravel-and-arch-layers class inventory** bullet gained a worked Faulty Example (`InvoiceAttachmentSizeService`) and an explicit "single source of truth, never two findings" note against its `@rules/laravel/architecture.mdc` CR Severity Rules restatements, plus an explicit gating clause against the sibling Moderate "Action-fit" bullet so a compliant `BaseModelService` subclass with as few as one public method is never flagged. Per issue #126.

- ✨ **Added**: an **opt-in savings mode** for the `daedalus`-orchestrated pipeline (#119) that removes five concrete sources of token overhead **without changing the process or the quality bar**. Off by default, activated only on an explicit user request and recorded once in the shared brief (`## Savings mode: on`) — a run with it on and the identical run with it off must converge with the same Critical/Moderate finding count, the same two independent reviewers, the same convergence gate, and the same PR/review/feedback artifacts. The five mechanisms: (1) a new, unconditional (not gated by the flag) `@rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` *Orchestrator turns must end in a result or a hard blocker, never a narrated plan* rule stops `daedalus` ending a turn with an undispatched plan; (2) a shared `## Context pack` + disjoint invariant split lets `argos`/`athena` stop independently re-deriving the same diff/AC/invariants and reporting the same defect twice; (3) a `## Build gate cache` keyed by a **tree** hash of the working tree (`git rev-parse "$(git stash create)^{tree}"`, mixed with non-tracked build inputs such as `composer.lock` so an ignored-file-only change still changes the key) lets the full `composer build` gate reuse a passing, this-run-provenanced result across intermediate steps instead of re-running on a near-identical tree — wired into `resolve-issue`'s final gate, `process-code-review`'s Finalization, and `apollon`'s scoped-validation full-build branch, and **never** allowed to skip the mandatory full run on the exact final head SHA before merge (`merge-github-pr`'s existing billing-exception invariant is preserved, not weakened); (4) a CR pass running in the optional isolated read-only worktree (no `vendor/`, cannot execute tests) stops asserting an executed coverage verdict and defers it to `apollon`, the sole authoritative owner of the real, executed number; (5) `daedalus` records `## Orchestration mode: thin` for a linear pipeline with no remaining branching decision, trimming its own restatement between dispatches without skipping, reordering, or removing a single dispatch step. `docs/agents.md` *Savings mode* documents the per-mechanism token-reduction rationale (the "no reduced review depth" proof required by AC2, since this repo has no live multi-issue token-benchmark harness). Pinned by new tests in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`, `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php`, and `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`. Per issue #119.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: `README.md` no longer documents stale or incomplete installer facts (#102). (1) The `Why This Package` bullet dropped the stray `(v0.9.1)` suffix — a version number does not belong in a feature bullet, and it already contradicted the planned first public release (`0.1.0`); the README no longer carries any version number outside the badges. (2) `Available Commands` now lists all five `InstallOptions` flags — `--prune` and `--allow-subagent-writes` were missing even though `src/Installer.php`'s own `--help` output documents both. (3) The `CLI Switches` table gained the missing `--allow-subagent-writes` row (the *"If `talos` reports `Blocked`"* note a few paragraphs down already told readers to use the flag, without the table ever defining it) describing exactly what `InstallerClaudeSettings` writes — scoped `Edit`/`Write` allow entries for the project tree in `.claude/settings.local.json`. (4) The `Rules Overview` table gained the three rows a full-tree `rules/` listing (not just the two the issue named) turned up missing: `api/general.mdc`, `php/dependency-selection.mdc`, and `php/examples/named-arguments.md`. Both completeness gaps are now pinned by new tests instead of relying on manual review to keep the README and the source of truth (`InstallOptions`, `rules/`) in sync: `readme rules overview table lists every rule file exactly once with no phantom rows` (`tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`) and `readme documents every InstallOptions flag in both the command list and the CLI switches table` (`tests/Installer/CliRunTest.php`, deriving the expected flags from `InstallOptions`'s constructor via reflection so a future flag addition fails the test instead of silently drifting again). Per issue #102.

- ✨ **Added**: a **dependency-only pull request no longer needs a code review** to be merged — a new *Dependency-only pull requests* section in `@rules/git/general.mdc` *Merging* plus the matching *Dependency-only PR exemption* gate in `@skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md`. A PR that changes nothing but dependency versions carries no application logic a reviewer could act on, so the review buys no safety and only stalls routine upgrades (Dependabot, Renovate, `composer update`). The exemption is deliberately narrow and always qualified from evidence — **never** from the PR title, the branch name, or the author being a bot: (1) **every** `files[].path` must be a dependency manifest or lockfile (`composer.json`, `composer.lock`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`) — one file outside that set voids it for the whole PR; (2) the manifest diff must be a **version bump of an already-present package** — an added or removed `require` / `dependencies` entry is a dependency-selection decision (`@rules/php/dependency-selection.mdc`) and keeps the full gate; (3) **CI must be green**, because a bump fails at runtime, not at read time, so the test suite is the evidence that carries the merge. Everything else is unchanged: no conflicts, not a Draft, approvals present, branch up to date, and the existing billing exception stays the sole CI relaxation. The delegated-security-coverage gate is vacuous here (no review ran, so no delegation was ever claimed) and applies unchanged on every PR that does carry a CR comment. The merge report must record the exemption and the changed files that prove it. Pinned by a new test.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the **`metis` problem-analysis subagent is removed from the roster** — `agents/metis.md` and its avatar are deleted, and every reference to it is unwired. `daedalus` keeps orchestrating, but its analysis phase is now **security-only**: step 4 dispatches `athena` (security-risk analysis) when the task carries a cyber-security question and otherwise goes straight to `talos`, whose `resolve-issue` specificity gate already handles an under-specified assignment. The two run-modes `metis` owned exclusively are gone with it — the pre-implementation `metis ‖ athena` parallel analysis (#66) collapses to the single `athena` dispatch, and the **decomposition / EPIC** route no longer exists as an agent step: a subject too broad for one PR is now **reported back with its separable pieces** so the user splits it (`create-issues-from-text` / `create-issue` stay available as skills, unchanged) and re-runs per piece. A general (non-security) analysis-only request is answered with `Blocked: the roster has no agent for general analysis` plus the remediation *run `@skills/analyze-problem` in the top-level session* — `daedalus` stays read-only and never analyses itself. Also updated: `athena`'s registration fallback (the pre-implementation analysis is skipped rather than re-routed, `talos`'s pre-PR self-check still runs `security-review`), the `Role:` vocabulary in `@rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` (`daedalus | talos | argos | apollon | shared`), `analyze-problem`'s memory read filter, the six-agent roster in `README.md` / `docs/agents.md` / the social-preview asset, and the `Role: metis` entries in `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md` (re-tagged `shared`, with the lessons kept). The removal is pinned by a new test. Per user request — *remove the Metis agent from the codebase completely*.

- ✨ **Added**: `merge-github-pr` now honours an **explicit "merge anytime" request** inside the *GitHub Actions billing exception*. When the caller's instruction explicitly demands the merge proceed regardless of CI availability (*"merge anytime"*, *"merguj kdykoliv"*), confirmed billing / account-limit entries are ignored **without** requiring the green local `composer build` — the explicit instruction stands in as the auditable authorization the substitute evidence would otherwise provide. The waiver is strictly **billing-only**: the conservative detection (annotations must name a billing / spending-limit cause) still runs, a general "merge this PR" request does not trigger it, every other gate (converged code review, verified security coverage, non-Draft, no conflicts, no real CI failure, approvals, up-to-date branch) applies unchanged, and the merge report must record that the local build was waived by the explicit request. Pinned by a new test.

- ✨ **Added**: `security-review` is now **stack-aware for known CVEs and targets the attack surface** (#83). **(1) Known-CVE awareness** — when the diff adds/bumps a dependency or touches a component with published advisories, it is checked against CVEs **relevant to the project's own stack** (determined from `composer.json` / `package.json`), via `composer audit`, the GitHub Advisory Database / NVD, and `@skills/security-threat-analysis` for a full remediation walk. The rule explicitly **rejects a hardcoded cross-ecosystem CVE list** that could never fire on the stack (the originating request named two WordPress CVEs — wp2shell CVE-2026-63030 / CVE-2026-60137 — which a Laravel/PHP project never executes; hardcoding them would be dead weight, so the awareness is scoped to what the project actually runs instead). **(2) Attack-surface targeting** — the review spends its budget on the changed lines an attacker can reach or influence (user input, auth, injection sinks, secrets, rendered-back data) rather than sweeping evenly; this is **prioritization, not exclusion** — a data-flow trace showing attacker-controlled input reaching an off-surface line pulls it fully back into scope. Pinned by a new test. Per issue #83.

- ⚡ **Changed**: `daedalus` now runs the pre-implementation **`metis` and `athena` analyses in parallel** when a task genuinely mixes security with substantial general design (#66), instead of dispatching them one after another. Both are read-only analyses of the **same single source**, take no write-lock, and neither depends on the other's output — so they parallelise with the same machinery as the `argos ‖ athena` CR pass: two `Task` invocations in one message, both reading the shared brief and appending under the per-brief append lock, with `daedalus` as the barrier that waits for **both** `Analysis done` and `Security analysis done` before dispatching `talos`. The change is explicitly scoped to parallelism **within one source** and does not touch *Sequential processing of multiple sources* (which still forbids fanning out across different sources). When only one analysis applies, only that one is dispatched. Pinned by a new test. Per issue #66.

- ⚡ **Changed**: review/fix loops now use a **cheap diff-scoped gate per iteration and one full build at the boundary** (#65), instead of a full `composer build` on every iteration. A new *Loop gate vs. final gate* section in `@skills/resolve-issue/references/quality-gates.md` splits the two: a **loop iteration** runs only the changed-files fixers / checkers / coverage (the project's `check:changed` / `test:coverage:diff` equivalents) and **skips the dependency/skill reinstall** (`ai-olympus install --force`, `composer install`) — nothing the loop does changes them, so reinstalling each iteration was pure latency; the **final gate** runs the full `composer build` (install + full-suite `--min=100`) once before push, and also whenever the change is broad (shared/core/config surface or >~10 files, the `apollon` high-risk heuristic). `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` wires both halves — loop gate in the Review loop, full final gate in Finalization. Crucially the lightening **never removes** the boundary build: it is the gate that guarantees a merge never lands with a broken project (**issue #75** is preserved, not weakened). Pinned by a new test. Per issue #65.

- ✨ **Added**: code-review output must **state only verified facts, never assumptions** (#74) — a new *Output Rules — Truthful reporting* section in `@rules/code-review/general.mdc`, referenced from every CR wrapper via `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md`. The review is the input to a merge decision, so a confident-but-wrong line is worse than an omitted one: it authorizes a merge the evidence does not support. The rule governs the **whole** output, not just `## Findings` — findings state only what was reproduced (not "this could break"), counts are the real surviving counts for the head commit, **a check is reported as run only when it ran** (a delegated `security: owned by athena` records a delegation, not a delivery, and must link the delegate's real output — the merge gate verifies it exists), uncertainty is labelled rather than laundered into fact, and no `file:line` / test result / coverage figure / rule citation is fabricated. A review that publishes an unverified claim as fact is treated as non-converged and must be corrected before the merge gate consumes it. Pinned by a new test. Per issue #74.

- ✨ **Added**: code review now flags **explanatory comments and documentation that only restate the code** (#53) — a new *Explanatory comments and docs that restate the code* bullet in `@rules/code-review/general.mdc` Core Analysis Walk-through, enumerated in `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` so every CR wrapper inherits it. It flags, on touched lines, a comment narrating a self-evident statement, a docblock that only repeats the typed signature, or a `.md` / class docblock added solely to describe what the code does — the fix is to delete it and, where the code was unclear, make it clear (rename, extract, DTO) rather than annotate it. Three carve-outs stay because the code provably cannot carry them: comments / ADRs explaining *why* not *what* (rejected alternatives, non-obvious trade-offs), domain glossaries, and navigation markers (the thin `@see` / `@rules/...` reference strings forming reader paths). **Minor** for a redundant comment; **Moderate** for a doc file that narrates behaviour, since it drifts into a second, lying source of truth. Gated against Simplicity First so the two never double-report. Pinned by a new test. Per issue #53.

- 📝 **Changed**: `resolve-issue`'s body is back under the skill-check token limit (#59) — the long-standing `body.max_tokens` WARN (estimate had drifted past 5000, and the repository-ownership guard's JIRA/Bugsnag additions pushed it higher) is resolved by extracting three self-contained procedures to `references/` — `deferred-follow-up.md` (the deduplicate/file/verify/cross-link filing steps), `pre-existing-issue-handling.md` (the categories, commit conventions, and per-type coverage workflow), and `phase-planning.md` (the commit-plan step-by-step) — each replaced in the body by a short summary plus a pointer, and by compacting the *Reopened task* deep-pass and the step-6/7 problem-analysis prose. No procedure changed; the byte-pinned fragments the content tests assert now live in the reference files, and the tests read them there. `## References` lists the three new files. Per issue #59.

- ✨ **Added**: code review now flags a **translation key referenced but defined in no locale** (#37) — a new *Referenced translation key must exist* walk in `@rules/code-review/general.mdc`, next to the existing *Translation completeness* walk. The completeness walk already covered a key present in the default locale but missing from another, and a hard-coded literal where analogous code uses `__()`; it did **not** cover a call like `__('user.profile.saved')` whose key exists in **no** locale file. Laravel does not fail on that — `__()` returns the key string verbatim, so the user is shown the dotted identifier as if it were a sentence, and it slips past every gate (the code compiles, nothing was added so parity is clean, and the call does go through `__()`). The new walk resolves both key layouts (dotted `lang/{locale}/{file}.php` and full-sentence `lang/{locale}.json`), refuses to guess at runtime-assembled keys, and carries an explicit scope boundary so it never double-reports with the completeness walk. **Moderate**, **Critical** on a primary-flow surface. Pinned by a new test in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php`. Per issue #37.

- 📝 **Changed**: `git-workflow`'s **Conflict resolution** section now leads with intent rather than commands (#50), adapting the approach from [mattpocock/skills — resolving-merge-conflicts](https://github.com/mattpocock/skills/blob/main/skills/engineering/resolving-merge-conflicts/SKILL.md). The section was a bare command list; it now walks the actual decisions a conflict forces — **find the primary source of each side** (commit / PR / issue) before touching a marker, **preserve both intents where compatible** and record the trade-off where they are not, and **never invent a third behaviour** in a merge commit that no reviewer sees against either parent. Two traps are named that the old list glossed: **`--ours` / `--theirs` are inverted under `rebase`** relative to a merge (reaching for them from muscle memory mid-rebase drops a branch's own work), and **aborting is a decision about the merge, not an escape from a hard conflict** — correct for a wrong base/branch/scope, wrong as a way to hand the same conflict to the next person with less context. Pinned by a new content test in `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`. Per issue #50.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: `code-review-github`'s *Omit empty sections* rule now matches its sibling wrappers (#68) — the GitHub wrapper still said *"Only the header block … and the final `Summary` line are always rendered"*, omitting `## Functional Review` from the always-rendered set even though `code-review` and `code-review-jira` had been tightened to include it. The sentence now reads *"The header block …, `## Functional Review`, and the final `Summary` line are always rendered"*, matching both siblings, and the pinning test in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php` is updated to the new wording. Per issue #68.

- ⏪ **Restored**: the **`daedalus` orchestrator and `metis` analysis subagents are back**, together with the whole orchestration apparatus that was stripped from the specialists — reverting the removal in `bb53d3d` and the five-agent-roster doc rewrite in `695eb91`. `agents/daedalus.md`, `agents/metis.md`, and their avatars return; the `## Shared task brief` read-then-append protocol, the working-tree write-lock, the parallel-dispatch model with its per-brief append lock and barrier, and the read-only CR parallel-review worktree sections come back to `apollon`, `argos`, `athena`, `talos`, and `hermes`; `rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc`, `rules/git/general.mdc`, the `analyze-problem` / `create-issues-from-text` / `pr-summary` references, `README.md`, and `docs/agents.md` return to their daedalus-orchestrated form, and the `AgentsTest` / `CompoundEngineeringContentTest` assertions with them. **The one deliberate merge:** `agents/argos.md`'s security agenda now carries **both** the `SECURITY_OWNER=athena` skip-the-duplicate rule (added in #82) **and** the daedalus parallel-handoff consolidation rule — they compose rather than conflict, since in a daedalus-orchestrated `argos ‖ athena` run argos both skips the redundant inline security pass and consolidates athena's findings from the shared brief. The `scripts/resolve-and-review.sh` pipeline template is left in place — it is a print-only helper that co-exists with the agent orchestration rather than replacing it. Per user request — *bring back the daedalus agent that was deleted earlier*.

- ✨ **Added**: a **shared repository-ownership gate** so no skill can act on a GitHub issue/PR belonging to another repository. `skills/_shared/assert-current-repo.sh` compares the reference against the current checkout and exits `4` on a mismatch, `5` when ownership cannot be proven; every non-zero exit is a hard stop and the deterministic loader's "exit 2/3 → fall back to the MCP server" convention explicitly does **not** apply — there is no fallback for an ownership verdict (code `3` is left unused for exactly that reason). Wired into the nine skills that act on a GitHub reference: `merge-github-pr` (which had no repository check at all despite merging), `process-code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `code-review-bugsnag`, `prepare-issue-context`, `tester-cookbook`, `analyze-problem`, and `resolve-issue` (whose prose gate now points at the shared script instead of restating it). Deliberately **not** wired into `security-threat-analysis` (reads `github.com/advisories/<GHSA>` as an external threat source), `code-review`, `pr-summary`, and `assignment-compliance-check` (their reference comes from the checked-out branch or an already-validated wrapper, never from the caller); a test pins both lists so the split stays deliberate. Ownership is resolved from **every** remote through `git remote get-url --all` / `--push --all`, which applies `insteadOf` / `pushInsteadOf` and returns the URL git would actually contact — so the fork workflow (`origin` = fork, `upstream` = the repo the PR lives on) and `pushurl` setups resolve correctly, unlike a raw `remote.origin.url` read. The `gh repo view` fallback applies **only** to a checkout with no remotes at all: consulting it after a parse failure would let gh's own parser silently repair the verdict and hide the gap. Two review rounds (`argos` + `athena`) drove the parser to a single canonical implementation shared by the reference and remote sides, closing path traversal (literal, percent-encoded, and double-encoded), an `@` in the path being mistaken for userinfo, and an SSH host alias honoured over https (`github.com-evil.example` is registrable). Behaviour is proven by a `--self-test` in the script itself covering every remote URL form git accepts, both traversal families, the look-alike hosts, the fork / insteadOf / pushurl setups, and each closed bypass (the `scan-attachments.sh` precedent) — and **`composer check` now actually runs it**, via a new `shell-self-tests` script that also covers `scan-attachments.sh`'s own long-dead self-test; both previously passed while nothing executed them, so a mutation flipping `exit 4` to `exit 0` left the build green. Per user request — *adjust the skills so they always check that a GitHub issue belongs to the current repository*.

- ⚡ **Changed**: the CR pipeline no longer runs the **security pass twice**. All four wrappers (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `code-review-bugsnag`) skip their always-run `@skills/security-review/SKILL.md` step when the caller sets `SECURITY_OWNER=athena`, which `argos` does whenever `athena` is dispatched alongside it. The always-run set is strictly sequential, so the duplicate pass was pure latency rather than extra coverage — `argos` runs measured 14–19 minutes against `athena`'s ~12. The flag is **opt-in and fail-safe**: unset, the inline pass runs exactly as before, so coverage can never drop because a caller forgot to set it, and a run without `athena` is unchanged. A skipped pass is declared on the summary line (`security: owned by athena`) so a PR comment never implies a review that did not happen there. Agent `effort` is deliberately **not** lowered — `argos` and `athena` are the merge gate, and issue #64's own analysis keeps them at `max`. Pinned by a content test. Per user request — *CR takes longer than it should; try adjusting the agents so they hold the same quality but run faster*.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: the merge gate now **verifies a delegated security review actually arrived** instead of trusting the delegation token. `security: owned by athena` records that the inline pass was skipped — it says nothing about whether the delegate ever reported, so a PR could pass the hard `0 Critical + 0 Moderate` gate with **zero** security coverage while being positively marked as covered. Not hypothetical: a security pass that dies mid-run (API error, session limit, cancelled agent) leaves exactly that state, and it occurred during this PR's own review. `merge-github-pr` now locates athena's security comment and applies the same `updatedAt` staleness rule as the code-review comment, and the Summary token must carry the URL of that comment so a token without a link is itself the visible gap.

- ⚙️ **Changed**: the **GitHub Actions billing exception now substitutes evidence rather than waiving it**. A billing / account-limit failure means the jobs never executed, so it carries no information about the code — treating it as a red build blocks every merge for a reason unrelated to quality. The exception is therefore no longer gated on the merge being "explicitly requested"; it requires a **green local `composer build` on the exact head commit**, recorded in the merge report, plus confirmation from the run's annotations (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/check-runs/<id>/annotations`) that the jobs genuinely never started — a real failure and a never-started job are indistinguishable in the checks list. Without passing substitute evidence the exception has no effect. It never converts *we could not measure* into *it is fine*; it allows a different, equally strict measurement. Per user request — *if GitHub Actions has a billing problem, ignore it as long as the local build is fine*.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: two Minor follow-ups from the PR #80 code review on `scripts/resolve-and-review.sh` (print-only script behaviour unchanged). (1) Step 2 of the printed plan now prints the **tracker-matching argument** — `code-review-jira <KEY>` and `code-review-bugsnag <error>` resolve the linked PR themselves, so they no longer show the `<PR>` placeholder that only fits `code-review-github <PR>`; the `README.md` and `docs/agents.md` pipeline steps are resynced to match. (2) `tests/Installer/ResolveAndReviewScriptTest.php`'s "…in order" test now actually asserts the `1 → 2 → 3 → 4` step order via a positional regex instead of only checking token presence.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the **`daedalus` orchestrator and `metis` analysis subagents are removed** — the Claude Code harness now orchestrates the specialists itself, so a dedicated orchestrator/analysis agent is no longer needed. `agents/daedalus.md`, `agents/metis.md`, and their avatars (`assets/agents/daedalus.png`, `assets/agents/metis.png`) are deleted, and the whole daedalus-only orchestration apparatus is stripped from the remaining specialists (`argos`, `athena`, `apollon`, `talos`, `hermes`): the `## Shared task brief` read-then-append sections, the `.claude/run/.daedalus-write.lock` working-tree write-lock, the parallel-dispatch model with its per-brief append lock and barrier, and the read-only CR `## Parallel review worktree` sections all go — each agent is now a **standalone specialist** the harness dispatches on demand. On top of #77's write-side removal, `rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` re-homes the surviving **read** protocol from the daedalus gather phase onto the orchestrating harness and narrows the role vocabulary to `talos | argos | apollon | shared`, and its *Temporary-file hygiene* section drops the daedalus brief / append-lock / write-lock bullets. `rules/git/general.mdc` drops the `(daedalus)` merge-path mention, and the `analyze-problem` and `create-issues-from-text` skills plus the `pr-summary` caller example lose their daedalus/metis references. `docs/agents.md` and `README.md` are rewritten around the five-agent roster and a harness-driven end-to-end run. A new **`scripts/resolve-and-review.sh`** pipeline template guarantees the canonical order — `/resolve-issue` → `/code-review-<tracker>` → `/process-code-review` → (`/merge-github-pr` only with `--merge`) — by validating the input, detecting the tracker, and **printing** the exact skills to run in order (it never invokes `claude` or any tracker CLI). `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php` and `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php` drop the daedalus/metis/brief/worktree/parallel assertions. Per user request — *Delete the daedalus and metis agents … harness mode will orchestrate it itself and use the agents … I want a script for this procedure*.

- ⚙️ **Changed**: **`apollon` now runs on `sonnet` at the lowest effort** (`effort: low`) instead of `effort: max`. Its work — authoring and running scoped tests — does not need maximum reasoning depth, so it runs fast and cheap. The `every agent … effort to max` content test now carries `apollon` as the single documented exception; every other agent keeps `effort: max` (issue #40). Per user request — *the apollon agent will use the sonnet model at the lowest possible effort*.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: the **write side of the compound-memory mechanism** is removed (#77) — `skills/record-project-memory/` (the skill that appended distilled lessons to `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md`) is deleted entirely, along with every hook that invoked it: the per-agent write loop in `agents/daedalus.md` step 7 (now "Report to the user, then clean up the brief"), the `### Compound memory` step + the PR-opt-out reference + the `## Done when` bullet in `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md`, and the "Record durable lessons" step in `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md`. `rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` drops its write-protocol sections (`### Promotion bar`, `### Curation pass`, `### Write protocol`, `### What feeds the memory`) and trims its frontmatter `description`, the intro paragraph, and the `## What Not To Do` / `## Code Review Application` bullets that assumed a write side — but keeps the read side untouched: every agent's step 0, `analyze-problem`, and `prepare-issue-context` still read `docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md`, and the *Temporary-file hygiene* exception "Memory files are NEVER deleted" still protects it from cleanup. **`docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md` itself is untouched** — its accumulated lessons stay in the tree as a hand-curated, historical artifact, the same way `CLAUDE.md` is. This package has no external users yet (pre-1.0, everything under `[Unreleased]`), so the removal ships as a clean hard removal with no deprecation shim. README skill count drops 49 → 48 and its Rules Overview row is resynced; a new regression test in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php` pins the absence of every `record-project-memory` reference. Per user request — *Delete the logic that writes data into docs/memory/PROJECT_MEMORY.md*.

- 🐛 **Fixed**: `code-review-bugsnag`'s Summary line was the only one of the four CR wrappers missing the `assignment conformance:` token (#60) — a drift left over from the Two-Part CR Output work (#56 / PR #61), which added the token to `code-review`, `code-review-github`, and `code-review-jira` but explicitly deferred the Bugsnag wrapper as future follow-up. `skills/code-review-bugsnag/templates/github-output.md`'s Summary line now renders `assignment conformance: {conformant | N gap(s) | no linked issue}` in the same position as the JIRA template (byte-identical to it), and its `## Functional Review` blockquote now points at "the `assignment conformance:` token on the Summary line below" instead of the stale "already run for this review" wording — matching the other 3 templates. `rules/code-review/general.mdc`'s *Two-Part CR Output* invariant no longer carries the now-obsolete "except `@skills/code-review-bugsnag`" exception clause: all four wrappers render the Summary-line token uniformly. A new pinning test in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php` locks in the fix. Per issue #60 (a `metis` plan for #56 whose remaining unimplemented item was this one drift; the rest of #60's original plan was superseded by #61's already-merged, alternate two-part design and intentionally left untouched).

- ✨ **Added**: new **Storage relocation / migration completeness** code-review category (#55) — flags a diff that redirects the read/write path for an existing kind of data to a different storage (DB table switch, filesystem disk switch, cache-store switch, cross-technology move) while data already sitting in the old location is left unmigrated. Requires a data migration / backfill command, a dual-read fallback, or an explicit PR-description waiver retiring the old data. Lives in `@rules/code-review/general.mdc` as a new Core Analysis Walk-through bullet (Critical when previously-readable data becomes unreachable, Moderate when a fallback bounds the exposure) with a 3-way gating clause against the existing **New storage reuse analysis** and **Backward-compatible data / storage changes (issue #38)** bullets so no two ever fire on the same line; enumerated in `@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` so every CR wrapper inherits it, and pinned by a new content test in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php`. Per user request — *Adjust the CR skills so that when the logic moves data into another storage, the migration of the old data into the new storage is checked.*

- 📝 **Changed**: `pr-summary` **outputs are now terse by default — caveman-style prose compression** (#51), modeled on the core `caveman` skill (https://github.com/JuliusBrussee/caveman). A new **Terse output style (issue #51)** Constraint bullet in `skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` governs every sentence the skill authors into the rendered comment — the `Summary of changes` headline/paragraph and the `How to test` steps on both templates: drop filler words, pleasantries, hedging, and articles (in languages that have them); fragments are OK; prefer short synonyms; never invent new abbreviations (standard acronyms like DB/API/HTTP stay fine); no decorative tables, emoji, or causal arrows; compress the style, never the language (`@rules/reports/general.mdc` unchanged); never name or announce the style in the rendered comment (the JIRA footer's generator attribution stays, as traceability, not a style announcement). **Auto-clarity carve-outs** keep full, explicit sentences for the `{assignment_verdict}` banner (must-not-be-missed information), security warnings and destructive/irreversible `How to test` steps, and any spot where compression would blur order or meaning. **Never compressed:** the `Authors` and `Available behind` metadata lines, the `{embedded_blocks}` slot, pre-authored test steps passed by the caller, and the templates' fixed footers. The GitHub template's `Summary of changes` paragraph shrinks from 3–5 sentences to 1–3 short sentences or fragments; both templates' `How to test` placeholders now call for short imperatives with exact toggle names / values / URLs verbatim. A new pinning test (`pr-summary output style is terse — caveman-style prose compression (issue #51)`) in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php` locks the new Constraint bullet and placeholder wording. Per user request — *Analyse https://github.com/JuliusBrussee/caveman and adjust the pr-summary skill so its output resembles that of https://github.com/JuliusBrussee/caveman*.

- 📝 **Changed**: `class-refactoring`'s **`MODE=cr` read-only lens now has a complete per-bullet contract** (#52) — no Refactoring Guidelines / Laravel Context bullet can silently fall outside the CR report anymore. The general `## Modes` statement is generalized from the literal verb list "apply / extract / split / consolidate" to "apply, extract, split, consolidate, collapse, replace, remove, move, or any other verb that would touch code", closing the gap where bullets phrased with "Collapse", "Replace", "Remove", or "move" were formally uncovered. Eight bullets that previously relied only on that general statement now carry their own explicit `MODE=cr` sentence — matching the pattern already used by the four that had one (Query performance, Runtime efficiency, Simplicity First, Pass-through Actions): **Speculative interfaces**, **Business Logic Layers**, per-row batch-query loops, **DRY**, data-modification consolidation, **Method parameter count (>4 → DTO)**, Repositories' feature-specific query methods, and **Livewire / Blade view splitting** (whose apply-mode "tests committed before the layout refactor" mandate directly conflicted with the cr "never author tests" rule — now resolved by reporting missing feature-test coverage as a finding instead, mirroring the Test Coverage Gate's own cr note). Every appended sentence only maps the bullet's existing mandate onto cr-mode output (a written finding) instead of applying the change — no apply-mode wording was deleted or reworded, and no CR wrapper skill needed a change since all four already invoke `class-refactoring` with `MODE=cr`. Per user request — *On any CR, also run the class-refactoring skill in read-only mode so the report additionally carries the items that skill defines.*

- 📝 **Changed**: the **agent pipeline is faster without lowering the quality bar of merged output** (#62). Three optimisations from the pipeline analysis land together: **(R1)** the implementer pre-PR self-check in `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` no longer iterates its own `code-review` + `security-review` convergence loop — the full-diff review runs exactly **once**, fixed findings are re-verified in a targeted way (finding's code path + pre-push quality gates), and a new **PR gate** requires every surfaced Critical / Moderate finding to be resolved (0 Critical + 0 Moderate) before the PR may be created (an unresolvable finding stops the run as Blocked); full-diff convergence stays owned exclusively by the authoritative post-PR `argos` ‖ `athena` ↔ `talos` loop. **(R2)** the review-loop safety cap in `skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` (and the daedalus orchestration docs) drops from `maxIterations = 5` to `maxIterations = 3` — a non-converged run still escalates to the user and the merge gate still requires 0 Critical + 0 Moderate, so the lower cap only surrenders hard cases to a human sooner. **(R3)** `daedalus` dispatches the **pre-convergence** `apollon` scoped validation only for **high-risk** changes (shared / core / config surface, more than 10 files, or security impact — the same broad-change heuristic `apollon`'s scoped mode already uses); the post-convergence `apollon` pass stays mandatory for every run. Wording synchronised across `agents/daedalus.md`, `agents/talos.md`, `agents/apollon.md`, `docs/agents.md`, and `README.md`; pinned by updated/new content tests in `tests/Installer/SkillsContentTest.php`, `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php`, and `tests/Installer/AgentsTest.php`. The remaining analysis recommendations (R4 effort right-sizing — conflicts with the issue #40 `effort: max` mandate, R5 lighter in-loop `composer build`, R6 parallel read-only analyses) are deferred to follow-up issues. Per user request — *The goal is the highest possible quality of the delivered task* + *Open the PR only at 0 Critical / Moderate*.

- ✨ **Added**: agents and skills that create a new tracker issue now **always select the single most relevant label from the target system's existing labels** and apply it (#54). New section `## Label newly created tracker issues` in `rules/compound-engineering/general.mdc` (placed between *File deferred points as follow-up tracker issues* and *What Not To Do*) is the single home for both the principle and the per-tracker mechanics — unlike its two neighbouring sections, this contract has four call sites, so the mechanics live in the rule itself instead of one executor skill. The heuristic loads the target tracker's full label list first (GitHub: `gh label list --json name,description --limit 200` — the CLI defaults to only 30 labels; JIRA: labels carry no description, so candidates are harvested via `acli jira workitem search --jql "... labels IS NOT EMPTY ..."` and matched on name only; Bugsnag inherits the GitHub mechanics since it files as a GitHub issue), excludes three generic non-content classes (workflow/state/structural, verdict/triage, audience — recognized by name/description, never hardcoded to this repo's own label set such as `Resolve_by_AI`, `duplicate`, `good first issue`), and picks the single best-matching content candidate (`bug`, `enhancement`, `documentation`, …) by comparing name + description against the new issue's title + body. When nothing fits, the issue is created **without** a label rather than forcing a wrong one or inventing a new one — the rule's only sanctioned exception to "never create a new label" is the pre-existing structural `EPIC` label from `create-issues-from-text`'s *EPIC parent & sub-issues*. The new mechanism is purely additive to the existing `EPIC` / `Resolve_by_AI` (backlog + claim) / `ready for review` label logic, which is unchanged. Four call sites now carry a one-line reference to the rule: `skills/create-issue/SKILL.md` (step 3), `skills/create-issues-from-text/SKILL.md` (step 3), `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` (*Deferred-item follow-up issues*, step 2's GitHub/JIRA/Bugsnag bullets), and `agents/metis.md` (step 3, publishing the analysis plan issue). Pinned by a new content test in `tests/Installer/CompoundEngineeringContentTest.php`. Per user request — *When an agent or a skill creates an issue in any system, it always picks the most relevant label and applies it to the issue.*

- 📝 **Changed**: the **new static-analysis / linter suppression** Moderate code-review rule now explicitly covers the `@`-prefixed PHPCS annotation spellings (#41). Both canonical locations — `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc` PHP Practices (*Do not introduce new static-analysis / linter suppressions*) and `@rules/code-review/general.mdc` Core Analysis Walk-through (*New static-analysis / linter suppression introduced*) — now enumerate `// @phpcs:ignore` / `// @phpcs:disable` alongside the bare forms; PHP_CodeSniffer honors the `@`-prefixed spelling identically (verified against the vendored PHP_CodeSniffer 4.0.1 — the tokenizer strips the leading `@` before matching annotations), so the spelling must not slip through CR pattern-matching. A new lock-in test in `tests/Installer/CodeReviewContentTest.php` pins the shared fragment byte-identically in both rule files and additionally pins the previously-unpinned Moderate severity declarations of the rule pair. Per user request — *when new code carries something like `// @phpcs:ignore`, report it as a Moderate finding, because those errors should be fixed, not ignored*.

- 🗑️ **Removed**: **Cursor and Codex editor support, and the whole `--editor` flag concept, are removed** (#16) — this package is not published (Packagist 404, no git tags), so this breaking change ships without a deprecation window; the recommended next release is **0.10.0**.
