- ✨ **Added**: `skills/refresh-claude-md/SKILL.md` — a new maintenance skill adapted from the [ECC `codebase-onboarding` skill](https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC/blob/main/skills/codebase-onboarding/SKILL.md), narrowed to a single job: keep the project `CLAUDE.md` correct. It triggers **only** when `CLAUDE.md` is stale or missing (drifted tech stack / build-test commands / directory layout / conventions, or an explicit "refresh/regenerate/update CLAUDE.md" request) and otherwise reports "no refresh needed" instead of rewriting the file. Execution follows the ECC four-phase shape — **Reconnaissance** (Glob + Grep over manifests, framework fingerprints, entry points, directory snapshot, tooling, tests; Read only to resolve an ambiguous signal), **Synthesize** (tech stack, real commands extracted from `composer.json` / `package.json` scripts and `Makefile` — never invented, directory-to-purpose map, conventions from recent `git log`), **Merge** (enhance, never blindly replace — every human-authored section is preserved verbatim and only the auto-generated sections are updated in place; new files use Tech Stack / Code Style / Testing / Build & Run / Project Structure / Conventions), and **Validate** (run every documented build/test command to confirm it works, then `composer build`). Constraints respect the package's "`CLAUDE.md` is never overwritten once it exists" guarantee, keep the file scannable and under ~100 lines, link to `@rules/**` instead of pasting rule content, and flag unknowns ("Could not determine X") rather than speculate. Frontmatter and body follow repo conventions (`license: MIT`, `metadata.author`, `Use when` description, `skill-creator` Layout A — `Constraints` / `Use when` / `Execution` / `Output` / `Done when`); `composer skill-check` scores the file 100/100 with 0 warnings. README skill count bumped 32 → 33 and the skill added to the **Issue Resolution & Delivery** catalog beside `skill-creator`. Per user request — adapt the ECC codebase-onboarding skill into a `refresh-claude-md` skill that runs only when `CLAUDE.md` must be updated.
- ✨ **Added**: `skills/article-writing/SKILL.md` — a new content-writing skill adapted from the [ECC `article-writing` skill](https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC/blob/main/skills/article-writing/SKILL.md). It guides long-form content (blog posts, guides, tutorials, essays, newsletters) toward an authentic human voice rather than generic LLM output: **Core Rules** lead with the concrete thing (artifact, example, output, number, screenshot, code) and explain after, not before; **Banned Patterns** delete hollow phrasing ("rapidly evolving landscape", "game-changer", engagement-bait closers, biography padding, AI throat-clearing); **Voice Handling** is self-contained — when the user supplies voice examples the skill derives a short voice profile (rhythm, register, point of view, formatting habits) instead of depending on a separate `brand-voice` skill, and falls back to a sharp operator voice otherwise; **Structure Guidance** tailors openings and pacing per medium (technical guide / essay / newsletter); a **Done when** quality gate checks sourced claims, removed AI transitions, voice match, and medium-appropriate formatting. Frontmatter was aligned to repo conventions (`license: MIT`, `metadata.author`, `Use when` description) and the body to the `skill-creator` layout (`Constraints` / `Use when` / `Done when`). README skill count bumped 31 → 32 with a new **Content & Writing** catalog section. Per user request — adapt the ECC article-writing skill into this package.
- ✨ **Added**: Laravel 13 feature guidance in `rules/laravel/laravel.mdc` (issue #551). The **Queue and Jobs** section gains two bullets steering bulk dispatch toward `Bus::bulk()` — a single call that enqueues many jobs without the overhead of a tracked batch (bulk notifications, imports, email campaigns, mass background tasks) — and reserves `Bus::batch()` for cases that genuinely need progress tracking, completion/failure callbacks, or cancellation, so reviewers reject per-job `dispatch()` loops where one bulk call suffices. A new **Scheduling** section codifies attaching structured metadata to scheduled commands with `withAttributes()` (tag, priority) for monitoring, logging, and alerting, keeps that metadata declarative at the schedule definition site, and cross-references the existing `onOneServer()` multi-server guard. The **Migrations** section gains a bullet steering foreign-key existence checks toward `Schema::hasForeignKey()` over manual `information_schema` lookups or `try`/`catch` wrapping, so migrations stay readable and safe to re-run. Because every Laravel skill applies these rules by reference (`@rules/laravel/laravel.mdc`), the guidance propagates to every Laravel CR / resolve flow without per-skill edits. One Pest lock-in test in `tests/InstallerTest.php` fixes the three additions in place. Closes #551
- ✨ **Added**: Malicious Code & Supply-Chain Indicators contract across the security rules and review skills (issue #549). `rules/security/backend.md` grows a new top-level **Malicious Code & Supply-Chain Indicators** section that flags four high-signal attacker patterns on every diff line — **silent remote fetch** (`curl -s` / `wget -q`, especially `curl … | sh` / `| bash` / `| php`; require allow-listed, version-pinned, checksum-verified HTTPS and never pipe a network response into a shell), **disabled TLS validation** (`curl -k` / `--insecure`, `wget --no-check-certificate`, `CURLOPT_SSL_VERIFYPEER => false` / `CURLOPT_SSL_VERIFYHOST => 0`, Guzzle `'verify' => false`, `verify_peer => false`, `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0`; pin the CA bundle instead of disabling the check), **suppressed error output** (`2>/dev/null` / `&>/dev/null` on a security-relevant command, the PHP `@` operator, `error_reporting(0)`, empty `catch {}`; handle or log through the project's error sink), and **hidden file + detached background process** (writes to `/tmp` / `/var/tmp` / `/dev/shm` or dot-prefixed names combined with `&` / `nohup` / `setsid` / `disown` / `at` / `cron` / detached `proc_open` — the dropper-plus-persistence pattern; use `tempnam()` with `0600` and route background work through the queue / scheduler). `rules/security/frontend.md` and `rules/security/mobile.md` add parallel mirror sections covering the cross-cutting subset for Node / Electron / build-tooling (TLS-off agents, `postinstall` `curl … | sh`, swallowed `fetch`/`XHR` errors) and mobile (trust-all `TrustManager` / `HostnameVerifier` / `URLSession` trust, `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads`, disabled pinning, silent-download-plus-background-job, swallowed keystore / pinning errors). `skills/security-review/SKILL.md` adds a new **Malicious Code & Supply-Chain Indicators** Core Check and `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` adds a parallel **Malicious code & supply-chain indicators (issue #549)** Core Analysis bullet — both walk application code, shell / deploy / CI scripts, `composer.json` / `package.json` script hooks, and installer hooks, raising one finding per match with severity **Critical** when the indicator maps to active RCE / MITM / persistence (silent fetch piped to a shell, TLS off on a credential request, both halves of the dropper pattern) and **High / Moderate** otherwise, each carrying the Suggested-Fix replacement (allow-listed / checksum-verified / logged / queued equivalent). `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` step 12 now cites the rule so the implementing agent never introduces these constructs before the PR opens. Six new Pest lock-in tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` lock the contract across the three security rule files, the two CR skills, and the resolve-issue skill. Per user request — *adjust every skill and rule to check for security risks: a silent curl, ignored TLS validation, suppressed error output, a hidden file in /tmp, and a process started in the background*. Closes #549
- ✨ **Added**: `skills/cleanup-local-branches/SKILL.md` — a new skill that prunes dead **local** Git branches in two categories: **gone** (branches whose upstream was deleted on origin, detected via `%(upstream:track)` == `[gone]` after `git fetch --prune origin`) and **stale** (branches with no origin counterpart — empty upstream **and** no `git ls-remote --heads origin <branch>` row — whose last commit is older than a six-month cutoff). The skill never touches origin, never deletes the current branch or the protected set (`main` / `master` / `develop` / `development` / `production` / `staging` / `release/*` / `hotfix/*`), always prints a deletion preview (branch, category, last commit date, integration status, planned action) before deleting. Integration status is computed by patch identity (`git cherry` against the default branch) so squash- and rebase-merged branches are recognized as integrated rather than mislabeled unmerged; an autonomous run deletes every `gone` branch (the upstream deletion ended its lifecycle) and, for the `stale` category, deletes only integrated branches while keeping not-integrated ones (their local-only commits never reached origin) and reporting the manual `git branch -D` command. One Pest lock-in test in `tests/InstallerTest.php` fixes the skill contract in place. Closes #550
- ✨ **Added**: API design contract standard + review lens that runs on every CR (issue #552). New rule `rules/api/general.mdc` codifies HTTP API design as a consumer-facing contract across the article's seven points — **API as a Contract** (five pillars: consistency, predictability, discoverability, stability, security by default; no internal-DB-structure leak across the boundary), **Resource-Oriented REST** (nouns not verbs — `/users/{id}/orders`, never `/getUser` / `/createUser`), **Correct HTTP Methods & Idempotence** (GET safe, PUT/DELETE idempotent, POST creates), **Idempotency Keys for Critical Operations** (`Idempotency-Key` header so a retry can't double-charge), **Precise HTTP Status Codes** (200/201/202/204 + 400/401/403/404/409/422/429, with the 401-vs-403 contract), **Validation at the Trust Boundary** (schema → business → authorization, in FormRequest / Data Validator, deferring error-text safety to `@rules/security/backend.md`), and **Business Impact**; a `## CR Severity Rules` section stratifies each violation Critical / Moderate / Minor. New read-only skill `skills/api-review/SKILL.md` (+ `templates/review-output.md`) is the focused review lens for that rule — it self-scopes to diffs that touch an HTTP API surface and emits findings with the standard reproducer fields (Faulty Example / Expected Behavior / Test Hint / Suggested Fix). `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` wires both in: the rule is added to **Constraints** and to the **Strict rule compliance** rule-file enumeration, and the skill is added to **Specialized Reviews → Always run** so every CR (and the `code-review-github` / `code-review-jira` wrappers that delegate to it) walks the API contract. Closes #552
- ✨ **Added**: UI Redesign Lens in `skills/analyze-problem/SKILL.md` that fires only when the analyzed problem is a UI / UX redesign or a new user-facing flow (detected from layout / screen / page / dashboard / form / wizard / modal / widget / navigation / look-and-feel / accessibility mentions in the assignment, issue, or comments) and is skipped for backend / infra / performance / tooling problems. When the lens fires it fixes the design direction of the Recommended Solution and the Non-Technical Explanation against six rules: **Simple** (minimum surface that solves the user's job; speculative knobs and decorative chrome are rejected the same way speculative code is rejected by `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc`), **Intuitive** (primary action unambiguous, affordances match platform conventions, no hidden gestures), **Readable for humans** (clear visual hierarchy, plain-language copy, WCAG AA baseline unless the assignment de-scopes accessibility), **Modern** (current conventions of the framework / design system the project already uses, no retired patterns), **One-click default** (single-click completion is preferred; a confirmation step is allowed only when the action is destructive / irreversible / financially material / legally significant / affects a third party, and the Recommended Solution must name which reason justifies the extra click), and **Wizard fallback when multi-step is unavoidable** (every step states its purpose and position — *Step 2 of 4*, the user can move back without losing data, save-and-resume is mandatory when the flow exceeds three steps, each step validates inline with field-level errors per `@rules/security/backend.md` / `@rules/security/frontend.md` *Safe Validation & Error Messages*, the final step shows a commit-summary). The verdict is recorded in the Recommended Solution under fixed subheadings (*Simplicity*, *Intuitiveness*, *Readability*, *Modernity*, *One-click vs wizard decision*) so the lens output is scannable. Silent relaxations are forbidden — when the assignment authorizes a deviation, the rule cites the assignment passage. One Pest lock-in test in `tests/InstallerTest.php` fixes the lens contract in place. Per user request — *a UI redesign should hold to a simple, intuitive, readable, modern UI; a one-click solution is preferred, and an intuitive wizard for multi-step purposes*.
- ✨ **Added**: Safe Validation & Error Messages contract on every user-facing string the diff touches (issue #540). `rules/security/backend.md` grows a new top-level **Safe Validation & Error Messages** section that codifies the wording rules for validation messages, exception messages, HTTP error bodies, log lines surfaced to humans, and every locale entry behind `__()` / `trans()` / `Lang::get()` / `@lang`: no identity / account enumeration on auth / password-reset / sign-up / change-email / account-lookup flows (one generic message, one response shape, one latency profile across *not found / wrong password / account locked / email not verified / 2FA required / user disabled*); no authorization granularity leaks (generic `404 Not Found` for both *missing* and *forbidden*); no stack traces / file paths / framework versions / fully-qualified class names / DB table or column or index names / SQL fragments / queue or cache driver identifiers / feature-flag keys in the response body; no verbatim echo of the rejected user input (reference the field by name, never `"Email 'X' is invalid"`); no password / token policy leak beyond the rule the user can read (no proximity hints, no missing-character-class enumeration, no fragment echo); no timing / shape side channels; translation parity across every shipped locale; and specificity preserved on the safe surfaces (schema-level validation like `"The age must be at least 18."` stays explicit because it does not leak existence). `rules/security/frontend.md` adds a parallel section that mirrors backend wording on the client, forbids client-side existence pre-flights (`/api/users/check-email` style), forbids unescaped attacker input in the message DOM, and strips stack traces / SDK errors before display. `rules/security/mobile.md` adds a parallel section that bans native crash dialogs surfaced to the user, forces the app's own fallback view on WebView errors, scopes verbose logging to debug / staging builds, and locks translation parity across `strings.xml` / `Localizable.strings` / locale JSON. `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` adds a new mandatory Core Analysis bullet **Safe validation & error texts (issue #540)** that walks every user-facing string in the diff (`FormRequest::messages()` / `attributes()`, exception messages reaching JSON / Inertia / Blade responses, Notification subject and body, Mailable bodies, flash messages, API error envelopes, Filament notifications, every `__()` / `trans()` / `Lang::get()` / `@lang` / `t()` / `i18next.t()` call across every locale shipped by the project) and raises one finding per match against the five wording-attack patterns plus translation drift; severity is **Critical** on auth / password-reset / sign-up / authorization surfaces (directly exploitable for enumeration) and **Moderate** elsewhere, with a **Suggested Fix** that rewrites the string to the generic, non-enumerating equivalent in every shipped locale. The check sits **above** the existing Translation completeness check and is independent of it — wording findings fire even when the key is present in every locale. `skills/security-review/SKILL.md` adds a parallel **safe validation & error texts (issue #540)** sub-check under **Data Exposure**, severity **Critical** on enumeration / authorization surfaces and **Medium** elsewhere. `skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` step 12 now cites `@rules/security/backend.md` *Safe Validation & Error Messages* (and the frontend / mobile equivalents) explicitly and requires the rule to be applied to every locale shipped by the project, so the implementing agent fixes the wording before the PR opens. Six new Pest lock-in tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` lock the contract across the three security rule files, the two CR skills, and the resolve-issue skill. Per user request — *Analyse every translation and make sure none of them helps an attacker find a security flaw or any other kind of hack*. Closes #540
- ✨ **Added**: Critical findings verification on every CR run (issue #537). `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` grows a new mandatory **Critical Findings Verification** step that runs after every preceding analysis step (Core Analysis, Highest-Priority Fast Track, Named Arguments Review, Specialized Reviews, Refactoring & Tech Debt, Validation incl. the Coverage gate) and **before** the Output assembly. For every **Critical** finding aggregated within the skill's run, the step invokes `@skills/analyze-problem/SKILL.md` inline and feeds it the finding's `file:line`, risk/impact, **Faulty Example**, **Expected Behavior**, **Test Hint**, **Suggested Fix**, the surrounding code context (enclosing method / class + any helper the fix points at), and the issue context already loaded under **Issue Context Analysis**. The outcome is **binary**: when **Verified Facts** and **Probable Root Cause** in the returned analysis back the finding, the Critical entry stays verbatim in the report (reproducer fields + severity + Suggested Fix unchanged); when the analysis refutes the finding or names a different root cause whose mitigation already exists in the diff, the entry is dropped entirely. Silent downgrades from Critical to Moderate or Minor on the basis of this verification are explicitly forbidden — the regular Strict-rule-compliance stratification stays in charge of any genuine severity reassessment. Moderate and Minor findings are **not** subject to this verification — they pass through unchanged. The verification eliminates false-positive Critical blockers from the published comment so reviewers never spend time on a Critical the analysis itself could not stand up. Per user request — *when a critical problem is found, the analyze-problem skill runs and verifies whether it is real; when it is, the critical item stays in the report to be resolved*. Closes #537
- 📝 **Changed**: CR comments report **only items that still need action** — clean / "OK" status confirmations are dropped across the GitHub PR comment, the JIRA comment, and the linked-GitHub-issue mirror. The `## Architecture` section now renders **only when the Laravel architecture walk produces at least one finding** — the previous literal status line `Status: walked, 0 findings` is removed entirely, in line with every other CR section that is dropped when empty (issue #530 follow-up). `@skills/assignment-compliance-check/SKILL.md` now returns a markdown block **only when at least one Critical gap exists**; on clean assignments it returns the new status `no critical gaps — assignment compliance block omitted` and the CR wrapper embeds nothing in the consolidated `pr-summary` comment (no "Verdict: clean" line, no "What is satisfied" list, no "Open questions for the reviewer" list — those subsections are removed from the template altogether). On the GitHub PR comment, the wrapper surfaces the new skip status on the summary line so reviewers can still see the check ran without polluting the body. Updated surfaces: `skills/assignment-compliance-check/SKILL.md` (description / Constraints / Required approach step 5 / Output Format / Done when), `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` (Core Analysis architecture report contract + Output Rules), `skills/code-review-github/SKILL.md` (Run Reviews + Output Rules + Linked-issue consolidated summary), `skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md` (Run Reviews + Output Rules + JIRA consolidated comment + Linked GitHub issues mirror), and the three CR output templates (`skills/code-review/templates/review-output.md`, `skills/code-review-github/templates/pr-comment-output.md`, `skills/code-review-jira/templates/github-output.md`). Lock-in tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` were updated for the new wording and a new test locks the clean-assignment skip / `What is satisfied` removal contract. Per user request — *report only the things worth changing; do not report the things that are fine*.
- ✨ **Added**: explicit scope for the `app/Concerns/` namespace (issue #531). `rules/laravel/architecture.mdc` grows a new **Shared Concerns (Traits)** section that designates `app/Concerns/` as the canonical home for all globally shared and reusable logic — typically traits, but also small reusable helpers and value objects — and forbids domain-specific code in that directory. Placement requires three conditions: globally applicable (consumed across two or more unrelated domains), domain-agnostic (no concrete model / aggregate / feature-flow knowledge), and reusable as-is (no per-domain branching). The existing **Validation Rules (Traits)** section is now framed as one specific worked example of this broader rule. `### CR Severity Rules` grows two **Critical** entries (domain-specific code parked in `app/Concerns/` and reusable trait logic scattered outside `app/Concerns/`) and one **Moderate** entry (single-use trait in `app/Concerns/` with no second consumer). `rules/laravel/laravel.mdc` adds a parallel **Shared Concerns** section and a **Layer Responsibilities** bullet so the core rule set carries the same scope. `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` adds `**Shared Concerns (Traits)**` to the mandatory Laravel architecture walk-through so every CR run audits the namespace and flags both directions of misuse. Closes #531
- ✨ **Added**: mandatory standalone Laravel architecture walk on every CR run (issue #530). `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` Core Analysis grows a new **Architecture conformance (Laravel) — mandatory standalone walk-through** bullet that walks every section of `@rules/laravel/architecture.mdc` against the diff **regardless of which files the diff touches** — including helper-only, route-only, config-only, migration-only, seeder-only, test-only, or even docs-only diffs — because architecture is also a verdict on **where** new code *should* have lived. The walk covers **Business Logic Layers** (seven allowed homes — `Actions / Model Services / Repositories / ModelManagers / Data Validators / Data Builders / Eloquent models`), **Actions / Action Rules**, **Model Services / Repositories / ModelManagers**, **DTOs / Data Modification (DRY) / Data Builders / Validation Rules (Traits) / Data Validators**, **Controllers / Resource Controllers / Single-Action Controllers / Livewire / Custom Helpers**, and inherits severity from `CR Severity Rules`. When the `pekral/arch-app-services` Composer package is installed, the walk additionally cross-checks the diff against the package's published README examples; when the package is not installed, the README cross-check is ignored. The published CR comment **must** carry a `## Architecture` section on every Laravel project — with findings under Critical / Moderate / Minor sub-headings when any exist, or with the literal status line `Status: walked, 0 findings` when the walk is clean — so the reviewer can see the check ran rather than wondering whether it was skipped. On non-Laravel projects (`laravel/framework` not in `composer.json` `require`), the section is omitted entirely. Updated surfaces: `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` *Core Analysis* + *Output Rules*, `skills/code-review-github/SKILL.md` *Output Rules*, `skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md` *Output Rules*, and the three CR output templates (`skills/code-review/templates/review-output.md`, `skills/code-review-github/templates/pr-comment-output.md`, `skills/code-review-jira/templates/github-output.md`). Five new Pest lock-in tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` lock the new contract across SKILL files, output rules, and templates. Closes #530

## [0.8.3] - 2026-06-01

- 📝 **Changed**: CR coverage reporting is **short by default** (issue #528 follow-up). The coverage gate still runs on every CR / pre-push check, but the published CR / tracker comment renders the `## Coverage` section, the `Coverage:` header line, and the `coverage …` slot in the summary line **only** when there is something the reader must act on — uncovered changed lines (Critical findings) or unavailable / non-runnable coverage tooling (Critical finding). When every changed line is at 100% coverage and the tool ran successfully, all three coverage surfaces are dropped; the Counts line carries the clean signal. Updated surfaces: `rules/code-testing/general.mdc` *Coverage* section, `rules/php/core-standards.mdc` *Testing* bullet, `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` *Output Rules* + *Coverage gate*, `skills/code-review-github/SKILL.md` *Output Rules*, `skills/code-review-jira/SKILL.md` *Output Rules*, and the three CR output templates (`skills/code-review/templates/review-output.md`, `skills/code-review-github/templates/pr-comment-output.md`, `skills/code-review-jira/templates/github-output.md`). Sibling skills (`create-test`, `create-missing-tests-in-pr`, `process-code-review`, `class-refactoring`) verify coverage internally but never publish a Coverage section themselves, so they were left unchanged. Six new Pest lock-in tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` lock the new contract across the rule files, skill files, and templates. Per user request — `when coverage is 100%, there is no need to mention it`.
- ✨ **Added**: code-review test-organization gate — `rules/code-testing/general.mdc` grows a new **Test Organization** section that requires each new test file to mirror the production class namespace (`App\Service\Billing\InvoiceCalculator` → `tests/Service/Billing/InvoiceCalculatorTest.php`), use the `{ClassName}Test.php` (or `{ClassName}{Scenario}Test.php` for extracted scenario files) naming pattern, route cross-cutting tests under intent-named directories (`tests/Feature/<flow>`, `tests/Contract/<vendor>`, `tests/Integration/<area>`), and ensure every `it()` / `test()` description matches the scenario the body actually asserts (no generic placeholders, method-named descriptions, or stale descriptions left after the assertions were rewritten). `rules/code-review/general.mdc` references the convention and `skills/code-review/SKILL.md` adds a dedicated **Test organization (issue #528)** bullet to **Core Analysis** with severity matrix (**Moderate** default, **Critical** when the misplacement hides regressions because the test never runs under the runner's discovery glob or when a mismatched description would make future maintainers change the wrong test) and a **Suggested Fix** template that specifies the corrected path / file name / description string instead of a vague "rename it". `skills/create-test/SKILL.md` and `skills/create-missing-tests-in-pr/SKILL.md` instruct creators to emit tests that satisfy the new gate so re-review converges in one pass. Five new Pest tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` lock the rule text and skill text in place. Closes #528
- ✨ **Added**: `rules/laravel/laravel.mdc` gains seven new rule blocks aligned with the *Performance Bible* — index / `EXPLAIN` / composite-prefix guidance in **Database and Eloquent**; no full-model dispatch + scalar-identifier dispatch + lightweight job constructors in **Queue and Jobs**; no `app()` / `resolve()` / `make()` in loops, singletons for stateless services, lazy resolution, lightweight constructors in **Dependency Injection**; selective middleware + fast-failing ordering + no DB / orchestration / external calls in **Middleware**; and three brand-new sections — **Stateless Runtime** (disposable servers, shared session / cache, `Storage` disks, `onOneServer()`), **Caching** (Redis vs APCu, mandatory TTL, Repository / Service caching boundary, identity-scoped keys), **Long-Running Runtime Safety** (Octane / RoadRunner / Swoole compatibility, no per-request state in statics / singletons, worker recycling). Five new Pest tests in `tests/InstallerTest.php` lock each block in place (the three new top-level sections share one combined test). Closes #525
- 📝 **Changed**: every skill that triggers a code review now delegates the CR invocation to a **subagent** (via the harness `Agent` tool, `subagent_type: "general-purpose"`) when subagent dispatch is available, falling back to in-line invocation only when it is not. Concretely: `autoresolve-oldest-github-issue` dispatches both `code-review-github` (step 4) and `process-code-review` (step 5) to subagents; `resolve-issue` dispatches `code-review` (Code quality and review loop) and `security-review` (Testing) to subagents; the CR wrappers `code-review-github` / `code-review-jira` dispatch each independent sub-review (`assignment-compliance-check`, `code-review`, `security-review`, `class-refactoring` `MODE=cr`, and the conditional `mysql-problem-solver` / `race-condition-review` / `refactor-entry-point-to-action` `MODE=cr`) as **parallel subagents in a single fan-out**; `process-code-review` dispatches the loop CR (quiet mode) and the final publishing CR to subagents; `class-refactoring` and `refactor-entry-point-to-action` dispatch their After Completion `code-review` to a subagent. Each subagent prompt must (a) name the skill and any `MODE=cr` flag, (b) pass the PR URL / branch context, (c) state the publishing contract (quiet vs publish), and (d) require the subagent to return its findings as the canonical markdown block. The dispatch isolates the multi-skill review output (assignment-compliance block, security findings, refactoring proposals, mysql / race-condition specialist findings) from the orchestrator's context window so long PRs and 5-iteration convergence loops do not exhaust context. Per user request — run CR skills as subagents whenever possible.
- 📝 **Changed**: both refactoring skills (`class-refactoring`, `refactor-entry-point-to-action`) now state the pre-flight **Test Coverage Gate** more explicitly: refactoring may not edit a single line of production code until tests for the target lines reach 100% coverage in a dedicated `test(scope): cover <area> before refactor` commit, and the assertion logic of those tests **must remain unchanged** through the refactor commit. Expected return values, expected exceptions, expected persisted state, and expected emitted events stay byte-for-byte the same — an assertion that would have to change is a behavior change, not a refactor, and must be split into its own commit. Mechanical renames forced by the refactor itself (namespace move, constructor argument shape forced by an extracted DTO) are the only allowed test edits and must be flagged in the commit body. `refactor-entry-point-to-action` previously had no explicit Test Coverage Gate step and step 8 said "Add or update tests for the refactored flow" — both are aligned with the contract: a new step 3 enforces the gate, and step 9 forbids assertion-logic changes inside the refactor commit. The refactor itself rewrites code **strictly per the applied rules** (`@rules/refactoring/general.mdc`, `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc`, `@rules/code-testing/general.mdc`, and the Laravel architecture / livewire / filament rules on Laravel projects). Per user request — keep test assertions stable through the refactor and rewrite production code strictly per the architecture rules.
- 📝 **Changed**: every code-review skill that publishes to GitHub (`code-review-github`, `code-review-jira` for the technical PR comment + linked-GitHub-issue mirror, `pr-summary` for the GitHub target, `process-code-review` for the resolved-items status comment) now posts a **fresh GitHub comment on every CR run** instead of editing the prior comment in place. `skills/code-review-github/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` was rewritten as append-only: it still appends the hidden marker `<!-- <namespace>:actor=<gh-login> -->` (default `cr-comment`, `cr-status` for `process-code-review`) for per-actor traceability, but it no longer searches for a prior marker-carrying comment to PATCH — every invocation calls `gh api .../comments -X POST` and reports `action=created` on stderr. SKILL.md sections, output templates (`pr-comment-output.md`, `code-review-jira/templates/github-output.md`), and the MCP fallback contract are updated to drop the "edit in place" narrative and the `updateIssueComment` call path; the fallback path now uses `addIssueComment` so failures still produce a new comment. JIRA-side upsert behavior is unchanged (`skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh` still edits the marker-anchored JIRA comment in place). Per user request — make the GitHub CR report always a new comment in the issue.
- 📝 **Changed**: `process-code-review` now ingests **unresolved GitHub reviewer threads** directly from the CR, not only the structured findings published by the review skills. Because `load-issue.sh` returns general `comments[]` / `reviews[]` but never the line-anchored review threads nor their resolved state, the skill fetches them deterministically via the GraphQL `reviewThreads` connection (`isResolved` / thread `id` / line-anchored comments; GitHub MCP fallback when `gh api graphql` is unavailable). The checklist is built from both sources: every thread with `isResolved == false` (human reviewer **and** bot) becomes a checklist item, and every `isResolved == true` thread is skipped. Free-form reviewer threads are **exempt** from the mandatory reproducer workflow — instead of requesting a CR rerun, the skill derives intent from the comment text and applies a minimal best-effort fix (with a regression test at its discretion). After the fixes are committed and pushed, each thread whose finding was actually fixed is marked resolved via the GraphQL `resolveReviewThread` mutation (threads that were rejected / deferred stay unresolved with the reason recorded in the `cr-status` report). The in-conversation completion report now reports the count of reviewer threads resolved / left unresolved. Per user request — process unresolved CR comments from GitHub reviewers.
- 📝 **Changed**: every code-review skill (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`) now runs the refactoring skills as a **read-only CR lens** when the diff is a refactoring. Both `class-refactoring` and `refactor-entry-point-to-action` gain an explicit `MODE` input — default `apply` keeps the existing behavior (modify code, write the pre-refactor coverage commit, run fixers / checkers, chain the After Completion review), while `MODE=cr` makes them **read-only**: never modify code, never author tests, never stage / commit / push, never run fixers / checkers, and never chain `code-review` / `process-code-review`. In `MODE=cr` the `class-refactoring` Test Coverage Gate becomes a read-only audit (sub-100% target lines are reported as a finding instead of triggering test authoring) and the output is refactoring opportunities as markdown only. The CR skills now invoke `class-refactoring` with `MODE=cr` (the existing always-on DRY lens) and, when they detect the PR is itself a behavior-preserving refactoring per `@rules/refactoring/general.mdc`, **additionally** invoke `refactor-entry-point-to-action` with `MODE=cr` to surface the entry-point → Action proposals. Both feed the **Refactoring (DRY / tech debt)** (in-scope) and **Refactoring proposals** (out-of-scope) sections of the review — no code is ever changed by the review track. Per user request — run refactoring skills from code review without applying changes.
- 🗑️ **Removed**: the bespoke diff-scoped coverage runner (`bin/test-coverage-diff`, `bin/coverage-diff-check.php`, `src/CoverageDiffCheck.php`, `src/CoverageDiffCheckFailure.php`, and their Pest tests), the `bin/test-coverage-diff` entry from the `composer.json` `bin` array, and the `composer test:coverage:diff` script — the runner did not work reliably. The CR / pre-push coverage gate in `code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `process-code-review`, `create-missing-tests-in-pr`, `create-test`, `class-refactoring`, `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc`, and `@rules/refactoring/general.mdc` keeps the **scoped-to-changed-files** requirement but now verifies it with the coverage tooling the project already provides (a Phing coverage target, a Composer `test:coverage` / `coverage` script, or `vendor/bin/pest --coverage-clover` / PHPUnit `--coverage-clover`) instead of the deleted binary. New rule across the same gates: any auto-generated coverage report file must be deleted as soon as it has been read so it is never accidentally committed (kept in `.gitignore` as a second line of defence). Closes #521

## [0.8.2] - 2026-05-22

- ✨ **Added**: new Agent skill `autoresolve-oldest-github-issue` — single deterministic entry point that picks the oldest open GitHub issue (optionally filtered by label, default `Resolve_by_AI`, configurable via `LABEL`; empty string disables the filter) and chains `@skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` → `@skills/code-review-github/SKILL.md` → `@skills/process-code-review/SKILL.md` → `@skills/merge-github-pr/SKILL.md` against the resulting pull request. Preflight verifies `gh auth status`, GitHub remote, and switches to `main` with the latest pull before delegating. The CR / process / merge steps run against the **PR URL** captured from `resolve-issue` (not the issue URL) so the deterministic loaders and `merge-github-pr` pre-checks (`mergeable`, `mergeStateStatus`, `statusCheckRollup[]`, `reviewDecision`) operate on the right node. The chain stops with a documented blocker — no force-merge, no retry outside the contract of the delegated skill — when `resolve-issue` does not produce a PR, the PR has merge conflicts, `process-code-review` cannot drive Critical + Moderate findings to zero within its iteration cap, or the merge pre-checks fail. Useful for scheduled / batch invocation when the operator wants the oldest backlog item processed end-to-end without re-orchestrating four separate slash commands.
- 📝 **Changed**: `rules/php/dependency-selection.mdc` is now also referenced from `code-review/SKILL.md` and `security-review/SKILL.md` so the *Code Review Application* contract (missing selection note = Critical finding, archived / abandoned / branch-pinned adoption = Critical finding on the spot, single-maintainer adoption without bus-factor flag = Moderate finding) is actually walked during the technical PR review. Without these Apply lines the rule existed on paper but the CR Strict-rule-compliance loop never enforced it because it only scans rules the skill itself Applies. The rule's Compatibility gate bullet 4 now also distinguishes *red CI* (disqualifies the candidate) from *no CI configured at all* (does not disqualify but downgrades the Test-surface scoring signal to zero and must be recorded in the selection note as a quality risk). A concrete rendered example of the selection note (Laravel project picking `spatie/laravel-data`) was added under the placeholder template so agents can match the contract on first try. Closes the CR follow-up to PR #515.
- ✨ **Added**: new shared rule `rules/php/dependency-selection.mdc` codifies how every skill must choose a Composer dependency before recommending one. The rule defines an **Activity gate** (last commit + last release ≤ 12 months, not archived on GitHub, not abandoned on Packagist, at least one Composer-installable tagged release, responsive issue tracker), a **Compatibility gate** (PHP constraint, framework constraint, OSI-approved license, green CI on the default branch), and a **Selection process** that enumerates 2–3 candidates, tabulates the gate results, scores survivors on adoption / docs / tests / maintainer diversity / type-safety, and recommends the highest-scoring survivor with a documented audit trail. When **no** candidate passes the gates, the rule mandates a blocker report to the user instead of silently relaxing the criteria — agents do not adopt inactive libraries. Code Review Application section escalates a missing selection note to a **Critical** finding and any adoption of an archived / abandoned / branch-pinned package to a **Critical** finding on the spot. The rule is referenced from `resolve-issue`, `class-refactoring`, `composer-update`, and `security-threat-analysis` — every flow where the agent currently proposes a new Composer dependency. Per user request — frequently-updated-and-active library selection.
- 📝 **Changed**: every code-review skill (`code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `pr-summary`, `process-code-review`) now publishes its tracker comments through a **single-comment upsert helper** keyed by the current actor identity **and** a marker namespace, so follow-up CR runs **edit one comment in place** instead of stacking new comments on the PR, the linked GitHub issue, or the JIRA ticket. New `skills/code-review-github/scripts/upsert-comment.sh <NUMBER|URL> <BODY|-> [<MARKER_KEY>]` resolves the GitHub login via `gh api user --jq .login`, appends the hidden marker `<!-- <MARKER_KEY>:actor=<gh-login> -->` (default `cr-comment`) to the body, lists existing comments with `gh api --paginate` (`jq -s` slurp across pages so the marker lookup is global, not page-local), and either PATCHes the marker-carrying comment or POSTs a fresh one. Companion `skills/code-review-jira/scripts/upsert-comment.sh <KEY|URL> <BODY|-> [<MARKER_KEY>]` resolves the acli actor, **appends** the invisible anchor `{anchor:<MARKER_KEY>-actor-<slug>}` at the bottom of the body (top-of-body prepend rendered an empty paragraph above `*Authors:*`), lists comments, and either edits the existing one (via `acli jira workitem comment edit`) or adds a new one — exit code 4 signals the calling skill to fall back to the JIRA MCP server (`editJiraIssue` / `addCommentToJiraIssue`) on acli builds that lack comment-edit support. CR wrappers leave `MARKER_KEY` at the default; `process-code-review` passes `cr-status` so its resolved-items follow-up owns a **second** per-actor slot (alongside the CR comment) that converge loops edit in place, replacing the previous *"Replying to code review from {date}"* quoting recipe. The `Previous CR Status` template section is dropped (history is preserved by the tracker's edit history on the upserted comment) and replaced by a `Last updated` header line. Pre-existing CR comments published before this convention are left untouched — the first marker-scoped run creates a clean comment alongside them, no migration / deletion is performed. `gh api` body payloads use `-f` (string field) instead of `-F` (typed inference) so markdown bodies that happen to start with `true` / `false` / a number stay strings; API error envelopes are no longer redirected to `/dev/null` so failures land in the calling skill's logs. Tracking: per user request — single-comment-per-actor consolidation.
- 🐛 **Fixed**: `bin/test-coverage-diff` is now exposed as `vendor/bin/test-coverage-diff` via the package's `bin` array in `composer.json`, so any consuming project gets the diff-scoped coverage runner immediately after `composer require pekral/ai-olympus`. The bash script also resolves its companion `coverage-diff-check.php` via the script's own physical location (with `readlink`-aware symlink chain), so the runner works correctly when invoked through Composer's `vendor/bin` symlink rather than only from the package root. CR skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `process-code-review`, `create-missing-tests-in-pr`, `create-test`) and `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc` Coverage gate updated to put `vendor/bin/test-coverage-diff` first in the discovery order and to forbid creating a new `composer test:coverage:diff` script in a consuming project. Closes #488
- ✨ **Added**: new opt-in installer flag `--allow-bundled-scripts` that whitelists this package's bundled scripts in `~/.claude/settings.json` so Claude Code stops asking for confirmation on every run. The flag is no-op unless `--editor=claude` or `--editor=all` is set and a `HOME` / `USERPROFILE` is resolvable. Scope is narrow per Keesan12's *trusted-script provenance* observation on the issue thread — only the two `load-issue.sh` loaders shipped under `skills/code-review-{github,jira}/scripts/` are added, never a blanket `bin/` or `*` allow. New `Pekral\AiOlympus\InstallerClaudeSettings` class encapsulates the read / merge / write flow: existing entries in `permissions.allow` are preserved, non-string entries are dropped, the merge is idempotent (re-running the installer reports `0` newly allowed permissions), unrelated keys (`theme`, `permissions.deny`, etc.) survive untouched, malformed JSON raises a clear `InstallerFailure`. Help text and `README.md` *CLI Switches* table document the flag. 17 new Pest assertions cover the merge / idempotency / failure / no-op paths. Closes #487
- 🐛 **Fixed**: tighten `@rules/reports/general.mdc` after the initial #490 implementation produced bilingual mixed comments in the *"<translated severity> (Critical)"* style. The rule now (1) bans mixing two natural languages inside the same tracker comment as the headline constraint, including bilingual parenthetical glosses, and (2) introduces a single, narrowly scoped *Exception — technical CR findings on the GitHub PR* — the technical PR comments from `code-review-github` / `code-review-jira` / `process-code-review` / `security-review` / `security-threat-analysis` / `resolve-issue` stay in canonical English (machine-greppable severity labels, structured field labels, rule references), while the **non-technical** mirror published on the linked GitHub issue / JIRA ticket via `@skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` (and `assignment-compliance-check` / `tester-cookbook` comments) still follows the assignment language. Each CR-wrapper skill's `Apply @rules/reports/general.mdc` clause is updated to cite the exception. Closes #494
- 🐛 **Fixed**: every skill that publishes a report to an issue tracker (GitHub PR / issue comments via `gh`, JIRA comments via `acli`, Bugsnag-linked GitHub mirrors) now writes the report in the **language of the source assignment** — i.e., a Czech issue / JIRA description produces a Czech comment, an English one produces an English comment. The previous *"All output must be in English"* / *"All output posted to GitHub must be in English"* / *"GitHub output must be in English"* / *"All CR output must be written in English"* constraints in `code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `process-code-review`, `security-review`, and `security-threat-analysis` are replaced by an `Apply @rules/reports/general.mdc` reference. The same rule is referenced from `pr-summary`, `assignment-compliance-check`, `resolve-issue`, `test-like-human`, `tester-cookbook`, and `prepare-issue-context`. New shared rule `rules/reports/general.mdc` carries the canonical statement (assignment-language detection via the deterministic loaders, severity-label translation, code identifiers / PR titles / commit messages stay verbatim per `@rules/git/general.mdc`, in-conversation status may stay in English). Closes #490

## [0.8.1] - 2026-05-18

- 🔧 **Changed**: PHPStan, Pint, PHPCS, and Rector now also analyse the `bin/` directory (added to `phpstan.neon` paths, both `phpcs-check`/`phpcs-fix` and `pint-check`/`pint-fix` Composer scripts, and `rector.php` paths). Closes a CR gap where `bin/coverage-diff-check.php` shipped as 79 lines of production PHP without any static-analysis coverage; regressions in the CLI wrapper now fail the CI quality gate instead of slipping through to runtime.
- ✨ **Added**: new `Pekral\AiOlympus\CoverageDiffCheckFailure` named-constructor exception class (extends `RuntimeException`, with `::invalidCloverXml(string $reason)` factory), mirroring the existing `InstallerFailure` convention. `CoverageDiffCheck::findGapsInClover` now throws the specific exception instead of a generic `RuntimeException`, so consumers can catch the actual error type rather than relying on string matching the message. Pest tests updated to assert the new exception class.
- ✨ **Added**: new Agent skill `prepare-issue-context` — front-loads the data and codebase context an implementing agent needs to act on an assignment without hallucinating. The skill loads the issue via the deterministic loader, extracts every scenario from the *Jak otestovat* / acceptance-criteria section (trigger, literal inputs, expected outcome, edge constraints), maps each scenario to a concrete code path (entry point, business-logic layer, persistence shape, boundary integrations), enumerates the records the scenarios depend on, seeds the **development** database via the project's existing seeder / factory mechanism (scratch namespace, never staging / production), and runs a one-shot reproduction so the bug is observable on the seeded state before any code is written. Three modes: `resolve-issue` (default — full seed), `tdd` (seed only the scoped RED scenarios), `cr` (audit-only — no inserts, the dev-DB state is queried instead). Returns `ready` or `blocked: <count> open gap(s)` with categorised gaps (mapping / data / behavioral / external-dependency). Now invoked as a mandatory pre-flight from `@skills/resolve-issue/SKILL.md` (before *Problem analysis*), `@skills/test-driven-development/SKILL.md` (before the first RED), and the CR skills (`@skills/code-review/SKILL.md` — first specialised review, surfacing the gap count in the PR comment summary and escalating behavioral gaps to Critical findings).
- 📝 **Changed**: code review skill (`@skills/code-review/SKILL.md`) gains a **Translation completeness** Output rule. Auto-detects whether the project ships translations (Laravel `lang/` / `resources/lang/`, Symfony `translations/`, JSON / PO / MO locale files, `__()` / `trans()` / `Lang::get()` / `@lang` / `t()` / `i18next.t()` / `useTranslation()` / `$t(` / `$tc(` call sites). When translations are in use, every user-facing string added or modified by the diff (Blade / Livewire / Filament / Vue / React labels, validation messages, Notifications, Mailables, FormRequest `messages()`, enum `getLabel()`, log / exception strings surfaced to humans) must exist in **every** locale shipped with the project, and hard-coded literals on surfaces that elsewhere use `__()` / `t()` are flagged. Severity defaults to Moderate, escalated to Critical on primary-flow surfaces or when CI asserts translation parity. The section is skipped entirely when none of the i18n signals match. Suggested Fix carries the literal translation entry for the missing locale (or a `TODO(<locale>)` marker when the agent cannot translate confidently).
- 📝 **Changed**: every skill that publishes a non-technical comment to an issue tracker now delegates the comment to `@skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md`. `test-like-human` no longer authors its own JIRA / GitHub comment template; it hands raw scenario results to `pr-summary` and the published comment carries the uniform *Authors / Available behind / Summary of changes / How to test* contract with the executed scenarios folded into `How to test`. `code-review-github` and `code-review-jira` already delegated the linked-issue summary to `pr-summary` and now explicitly pass the PR / git authorship and the test-parameter-gating context through to it. Specialised non-technical reports (`assignment-compliance-check`, `tester-cookbook`) keep their bespoke structure but adopt the same `Authors` + `Available behind` metadata lines. Technical PR comments (CR findings on the GitHub PR) remain exempt and continue to carry the full detail programmers need.
- 📝 **Changed**: every tracker-facing comment **must credit the real change author(s)** instead of the agent / CR identity. `@skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md` resolves authors from `git log --pretty='%an <%ae>' base..HEAD` plus PR `author.login` + `commits[].author.login` (GitHub handle preferred for GitHub targets, JIRA display name preferred for JIRA targets). The pr-summary templates (`pr-summary-github.md`, `pr-summary-jira.md`), the `assignment-compliance-check` template, and the `tester-cookbook` comment now expose the dedicated `Authors` line. When authorship cannot be determined, the line says *"unknown — git history did not yield a recognisable identity"* rather than being silently dropped.
- 📝 **Changed**: every tracker-facing comment **must surface test-parameter gating**. When the diff hides the change behind a feature flag, ENV switch, query-string parameter, request header, A/B variant, admin toggle, or allow-list, `pr-summary` adds an `Available behind` line naming the gating toggle and the value required to reach it, and the first `How to test` step enables / supplies that toggle. `assignment-compliance-check` and `tester-cookbook` carry the same conditional line. The line is omitted entirely when the change is reachable for every user unconditionally.
- 📝 **Changed**: code review skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`) require `@skills/mysql-problem-solver/SKILL.md` to run **whenever the diff touches database operations** (raw SQL, Eloquent / query-builder calls, eager loads, model scopes, ModelManager / Repository methods, migrations, seeders, DynamoDB / NoSQL access). The skill is moved from "Always run" to a mandatory conditional trigger, and its findings appear in the published GitHub PR comment under a dedicated `## Database Analysis` section (before `## Coverage`) — never silently folded into the Critical / Moderate / Minor buckets. The JIRA non-technical comment (produced by `pr-summary`) stays plain-language and does not carry this section. `templates/review-output.md` reflects the new section.
- ♻️ **Refactored**: extracted the diff-scoped coverage logic out of `bin/coverage-diff-check.php` into a first-class `Pekral\AiOlympus\CoverageDiffCheck` class under `src/`. The `bin/` entry-point is now a thin CLI wrapper that loads Composer autoloader, parses arguments, and delegates to the class. Adds Pest tests covering every code path of `parseUnifiedDiff` (data provider with 8 cases), `findGapsInClover` (covered / uncovered / non-stmt / out-of-diff / sorted-deduped / absolute-path / nested `<package>` / malformed XML / missing-project-root), and `discoverChangedLines` (current-repo smoke + temp-repo untracked-file fixture). `bin/test-coverage-diff` now also picks up untracked PHP files via `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`, and the PHP helper diffs them through `git diff --no-index /dev/null <file>` so they participate in the changed-line map. Bug-fix: `bin/test-coverage-diff` previously aborted under `set -euo pipefail` because `php -m | grep -q` triggered SIGPIPE in `php -m`; the driver-detection step now captures `php -m` into a variable first.
- ✨ **Added** / 📝 **Changed**: CR coverage gate is now **diff-scoped only**. New `bin/test-coverage-diff` shell entry-point + `bin/coverage-diff-check.php` Clover parser, wired up as Composer script `composer test:coverage:diff`. The shell entry-point auto-picks PCOV when loaded and falls back to Xdebug in coverage mode, and auto-detects Laravel projects (artisan + `laravel/framework` in `composer.json` → covers `app/` + `src/`) versus non-framework projects (covers `src/`). All CR / pre-push skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `create-missing-tests-in-pr`, `create-test`, `process-code-review`) and the shared `rules/php/core-standards.mdc` Testing section require the diff-scoped command during code review and explicitly forbid substituting the full-suite `composer test:coverage` / Phing `coverage` / `pest --coverage --min=100`-on-whole-suite as a fallback. The published `## Coverage` section in CR PR comments now reports the diff-scoped command and result (or "diff-scoped tooling unavailable" with reason). Full-suite coverage remains the release / CI gate.
- 📝 **Changed**: review skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `security-review`) carry an explicit **read-only** constraint that bars every git write operation (`git add`, `git commit`, `git push`, `git reset`, `git checkout -- …`) on top of the existing "never modify code" rule. Branch switching and `git pull` to read the latest diff remain allowed; publishing is limited to PR / linked-issue comments via `gh` and to JIRA ticket comments via `acli`. README skill descriptions for `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, and `security-review` now surface the read-only guarantee to match `code-review`.
- ✨ **Added**: new Agent skill `security-threat-analysis` — analyzes a specific security threat from a referenced source (CVE, GHSA, vendor advisory, blog post, or write-up) and produces a remediation report that is both human-readable and directly actionable by an AI agent. The skill loads the source, classifies the threat (OWASP/CWE, severity, attack vector, preconditions), matches it against the current project (lockfile versions for dependency CVEs, grep hits for pattern-based threats, framework/web-server settings for configuration threats), drafts an ordered remediation playbook with exact files and commands, and adds a verification step (manual check + automated test) that proves the threat is closed. Complements `@skills/security-review/SKILL.md` (whole-project audit) without duplicating it (#482)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: `tester-cookbook` skill rewritten for a **short, dev-team-report-focused** output. The verbose cookbook structure (*What changes from the user's point of view* / *Setup before testing* / *Test scenarios* / *Regression* / *Edge cases* / *What to check after the run* / *What to report back to the dev team*) is collapsed to two sections: an **optional** *Brief steps to reach the result* and a **required** *What to report back to the dev team*. The Constraints (forbidden technical vocabulary, no code identifiers, no credentials, concrete inputs, Wiki Markup pre-publish validation) and the load-JIRA / load-PR / code→UI mapping / publish steps are unchanged; technical content stays off the JIRA comment exactly as before (#480)
- 📝 **Changed**: code review skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`) now **delegate the non-technical issue-tracker summary to `@skills/pr-summary/SKILL.md`** instead of authoring their own ad-hoc template. The JIRA non-technical comment on the originating ticket and the mirrored GitHub-issue comment are both produced by `pr-summary` — uniform *"Summary of changes + How to test"* output that non-technical project managers understand and can act on. The obsolete `code-review-jira/templates/jira-output.md` template (CR status / counts / key risks / testing recommendations format) is removed; technical findings still go directly on the PR comment (#472)
- ✨ **Added**: new Agent skill `tester-cookbook` — turns a JIRA task and its linked pull requests into a click-by-click manual-testing cookbook for an internal QA tester. The cookbook strips every code identifier (class names, ENV keys, queue/job names, enum cases, status codes, Bugsnag references) and maps each PR-level change to its visible UI label, delivered email/SMS, report column, or account-balance movement. The result is posted as a single JIRA Wiki Markup comment via `acli` (JIRA MCP server fallback). Pre-publish validation rejects any forbidden vocabulary or Markdown syntax (#476)
- 📝 **Changed**: SQL review rule and CR skills now require **reusing existing schema indexes before adding new ones**. `rules/sql/optimalize.mdc` ships a new *Reuse existing indexes first* section: during code review of new or modified SQL / Eloquent / query-builder code, locate the current schema (migrations, model `$table` metadata, `SHOW INDEX`) and flag every query that bypasses an existing covering index (column order mismatch, function on indexed column, projection outside the index). The Suggested Fix must rewrite the query (column re-ordering, SARGable rewrite, covering projection); a new index is proposed only when no existing index can cover the query and EXPLAIN confirms the gap. `code-review`, `code-review-github`, and `code-review-jira` carry the new bullet in Core Analysis / Run-conditionally DB-changes; `mysql-problem-solver` prefers query rewrite over new-index proposals in Diagnose / Propose Optimizations (#468)
- 📝 **Changed**: `pr-summary` skill rewritten for a **short two-section, human-readable comment** — only `Summary of changes` (one-line headline + one explanatory paragraph in plain language) and `How to test` (ordered tester steps). The skill now picks the output template by target tracker: `templates/pr-summary-github.md` (GitHub Markdown) for PR comments and `templates/pr-summary-jira.md` (JIRA Wiki Markup) for JIRA issue comments, so the JIRA UI renders the comment formatted instead of as raw text. Categories, breaking-changes, and testing-notes sections were removed in favour of plain-language business context that still names the relevant integration / payload / area so a developer can locate the change without reading the diff (#465)
- 📝 **Changed**: `assignment-compliance-check` now publishes the Assignment Compliance report as a **dedicated comment on the originating issue tracker** (GitHub issue via `gh issue comment`, JIRA ticket via `acli` with Wiki Markup conversion). The report is **no longer embedded** in the PR code-review comment — the PR comment carries technical findings, the issue tracker carries assignment compliance. CR wrappers (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`) surface the publish status (`posted to issue #N` / `no linked issue — assignment compliance skipped` / `failed to publish …`) in their PR comment summary line (#464)
- 📝 **Changed**: `process-code-review` is now a **convergence loop**. Iterations 1…N–1 invoke `code-review-github` / `code-review-jira` in **quiet mode** (no PR comment, no JIRA comment, no linked-issue mirror — findings returned in memory only), apply Suggested Fix snippets via the existing reproducer-extraction workflow, and re-run the review. The loop exits only when `criticalCount + moderateCount == 0`. The final report is published exactly once after convergence; a `maxIterations = 5` safety net stops runaway loops and surfaces residual findings to the user for manual triage. Both `code-review-github` and `code-review-jira` honour the quiet-mode caller instruction at their Post Results / Publish Results step
- 📝 **Changed**: GitHub PR comment templates (`code-review-github/templates/pr-comment-output.md`, `code-review-jira/templates/github-output.md`) rewritten for **scannable + AI-parseable** output — header carries Status / Counts / Coverage / Issue-tracker summary on one line each, every Critical / Moderate finding follows a stable `Location / Rule / Impact / Faulty Example / Expected behavior / Test hint / Suggested fix` key=value block (severity icons 🔴 / 🟠 / 🟡 for at-a-glance scanning), Refactoring and Refactoring proposals share the same compact shape. Code examples remain in `php` fenced blocks so reproducer fields stay machine-extractable
- 📝 **Changed**: canonical JIRA comment template (`@skills/pr-summary/templates/pr-summary-jira.md`) and `rules/jira/general.mdc` now mandate **JIRA Wiki Markup** for every JIRA comment so the JIRA UI renders the comment formatted instead of as raw text. The rule file ships a conversion cheatsheet (h2./h3. headings, `*bold*`, `_italic_`, `{{inline}}`, `{code:php}…{code}`, `* / # bullets`, `[label|url]`, `{quote}`) usable by every JIRA-touching skill
- 📝 **Changed**: `code-review` skill tightened to **strict rule compliance**. Two new mandatory Core Analysis steps: (1) **Strict rule compliance** — walk every numbered/bulleted rule from every Apply'd rule file (`@rules/php/core-standards.mdc`, `@rules/code-review/general.mdc`, `@rules/refactoring/general.mdc`, `@rules/code-testing/general.mdc`, plus on Laravel projects all `@rules/laravel/*.mdc`) against every line in the diff and raise one finding per violation with the rule reference; (2) **Architecture conformance (Laravel)** — walk every section of `@rules/laravel/architecture.mdc` (Business Logic Layers, Actions, Model Services, Repositories/ModelManagers, DTOs, Data Validators, Controllers, Livewire, Custom Helpers, …) against the diff. Default severity for any unexcused rule violation on touched lines is **Critical** unless the rule file's CR Severity Rules subsection assigns lower. The blanket "do not review formatting / linting" exclusion was narrowed to fixer-handled output (Pint / PHPCS / Rector) only — every rule violation a fixer does not catch must be flagged
- 📝 **Changed**: code review skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`) now mandate publishing a non-technical summary (overall status, key risks in plain language, testing recommendations, link back to the PR) to **every linked issue** in the originating tracker — `code-review-github` posts to each `closingIssues[]` GitHub issue via `gh issue comment` after the PR comment, and `code-review-jira` mirrors the existing JIRA non-technical summary onto any GitHub issues the PR also references. The canonical `code-review` rule explicitly states the wrapper skills must propagate the summary
- 📝 **Changed**: Laravel architecture rules and the `class-refactoring` skill now treat Eloquent models as a 7th allowed home for business logic — but **only** for simple, self-contained domain methods (own-attribute predicates like `$user->isActive()`, computed values from already-loaded data, simple state derivations). Anything that needs external services, repositories, model managers, new database queries, persistence side effects, or multi-entity orchestration must move to one of the other six layers (Action / Model Service / Repository / ModelManager / Data Validator / Data Builder). CR severity table flags model methods that cross this boundary as **critical** (#453)
- ✨ **Added**: new Agent skill `assignment-compliance-check` — plain-language verification that the PR implementation satisfies the linked issue's business requirements. Returns an in-memory **Assignment Compliance** markdown section (no file is written) that the invoking CR skill (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`) embeds verbatim into the published CR comment, so Critical functional gaps surface alongside the rest of the review (#457)
- ✨ **Added**: new Laravel rule `laravel/dynamodb.mdc` covering DynamoDB query safety — scan prevention for every query-builder change, required key-targeted access patterns (`GetItem` / `Query`), GSI usage, review checklist, and Tinker-based testing/debugging when `laravel/tinker` is available (#447)
- 📝 **Changed**: code review and security-review skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`, `security-review`) and their output templates now require a **Suggested Fix** code snippet alongside Faulty Example / Expected Behavior / Test Hint for every Critical and Moderate finding (Critical/High for security). The snippet must comply with `@rules/php/core-standards.mdc` and, on Laravel projects, `@rules/laravel/architecture.mdc`. `process-code-review` reads the snippet and applies it directly when resolving the finding
- 📝 **Changed**: code review skills (`code-review`, `code-review-github`, `code-review-jira`) and the shared `code-review/general.mdc` rule now require reviewers to flag newly added or modified logic that duplicates behavior already present elsewhere in the codebase — reuse the existing implementation to keep logic unified across the application (#440)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: CR skills now systematically review all open PRs per issue instead of only one (#227)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: JIRA skills now consistently prefer `acli` console tool via shared `jira-operations.mdc` rule (#228)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: CR no longer flags custom named static constructors (`fromModel()`, `fromRequest()`) on Spatie DTOs as issues (#230)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: CR skills now include mandatory regression analysis to verify changes don't break existing functionality outside ticket scope (#233)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: `code-review` skill now enforces English-only output — Czech Deliver/Communication sections translated (#235)
- 🔧 **Changed**: move shared rule files from `rules/skills/` to `rules/`, update all skill references (#238)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: `create-missing-tests-in-pr` and `test-like-human` skills now use shared `github-operations.mdc` / `jira-operations.mdc` rules instead of inline preferences (#237)
- 🐛 **Fixed**: all mock rules now prefer partial mocks (`makePartial()`) over full mocks (#241)
- ✨ **Added**: new Agent skill `skill-creator` for scaffolding new SKILL.md files that follow project conventions and pass `skill-check` validation (#432)
- ✨ **Added**: new Laravel rule `laravel/queue-debouncing.mdc` covering safe queue debouncing, urgency separation, replaceable-work design, and review/testing requirements (#431)
- 📝 **Changed**: testing rules now explicitly mandate Pest syntax (`it()` / `test()`) for all new test files; PHPUnit class-based tests are no longer allowed for new tests (#430)
- 📝 **Changed**: rules and CR skills now require 100% test coverage for every changed code path, mandate discovering the project's coverage command (Phing → Composer) before running it, and require a `## Coverage` section in every published CR comment — never push a CR report without the coverage result (#429)
- 📝 **Changed**: Laravel queue rules require a `deduplicationId(): string` method directly on the job class when using SQS FIFO / an SQS deduplicator package — the job is the source of truth for its own deduplication identity (#428)

- 📝 **Changed**: Laravel testing rules and test-writing skills require Eloquent rows in tests to be created only via model factories (#147)

- ✨ **Added**: new Agent skill `create-jira-issue-from-pr` for creating JIRA-ready issue drafts from GitHub PR review context while preserving original task text (#205)
